chronological, not categorical

Development Timeline

Each phase was a distinct request, built on top of the last. Nothing here was planned upfront — this is the actual order things happened in.

phase 0 · baseline

Original POC

Real Kalshi public market data, a simulated whale-signal feed, and a fake broker. Paper mode only, no accounts, no login, one dashboard.

main.py services/kalshi_client.py services/whale_simulator.py services/strategy_engine.py services/risk_manager.py services/paper_broker.py services/config_store.py static/index.html
phase 1 · visibility

Build-status dashboard

A separate, locally-hosted page reading the actual repo to answer "is development progressing as intended" — component ledger, pipeline diagram, safety-gate checklist, config snapshot.

static/status.html · /status route
phase 2 · real accounts

Real Kalshi account access (read-only) + whale-watcher provider library

A second, separate authenticated client using Kalshi's RSA-PSS request signing — read-only endpoints (balance/positions/fills/orders) active as soon as credentials exist; create_order/cancel_order fully implemented but refuse to run unless explicitly enabled. Also generalized the single whale_live.py file into a provider interface + registry so more whale-watcher tools can be added as separate files later.

services/kalshi_account_client.py · services/whalewatchers/{base,generic_rest,template_provider,__init__}.py (replaced whale_live.py)
phase 3 · identity

Google sign-in + Connected Accounts

Single-operator login gate (inert until 3 env vars are set — the app stays fully open otherwise) plus an encrypted local store so whale-watcher credentials can be entered through a form instead of hand-edited into .env. Kalshi's own account deliberately stayed file-path-based — a private key shouldn't pass through a browser form. Along the way, found and fixed an unrelated pre-existing DDEV misconfiguration where two services were both claiming the same hostname/port, causing non-deterministic routing.

services/auth.py · services/accounts_store.py · static/login.html · static/accounts.html · .ddev/config.yaml (router fix)
phase 4 · clarity

Interface cleanup pass

Session/login status moved to its own always-visible bar instead of being buried next to P&L numbers. Fixed a real word collision: the green "running" dot and the real-money account bar both used "live" for different meanings — the account bar became "REAL KALSHI ACCOUNT" everywhere, and paper numbers got an explicit "PAPER" tag so the two kinds of money on screen can't be confused.

static/index.html (header/util-bar restructure) · static/status.html, static/accounts.html (link wording)
phase 5 · correctness

Real data, not silently-wrong data

Found that the code was written against Kalshi field names (yes_bid, volume) that don't exist in the live API — it actually returns yes_bid_dollars and volume_24h_fp. That single mismatch had been silently causing four separate bugs: blank prices/volume on the dashboard, a "top volume" sort that was a no-op, whale-signal prices falling back to random numbers instead of real market prices, and momentum/contrarian bias always picking the same side. Fixed all four, then replaced raw tickers everywhere with Kalshi's own human-readable title field, with combo-parlay-aware formatting once it became clear every currently open market is a multi-leg combination contract.

services/kalshi_client.py · services/whale_simulator.py · main.py · static/index.html
phase 6 · money flow

Positions and trade history made visible

The paper broker had been tracking open positions and a trade log the entire time — none of it was ever rendered anywhere. Added dedicated panels for both, plus a signal→decision funnel stat ("N signals seen → N trades placed → N skipped") so it's obvious at a glance why the bankroll may not be moving, instead of inferring it from a wall of skip reasons.

static/index.html · main.py (stats counters)
phase 7 · range of views

Multi-view dashboard

Split one dense screen into four purpose-built views behind a tab bar: Portfolio (equity-over-time chart, positions, trade log — the definitive "where's my money" view), Markets (a simplified, Kalshi-style card grid — big Yes/No prices, no jargon), Whale Watch (the same cards with a large, aggregated whale-lean indicator per market), and Terminal (the original dense technical view, kept for the signal→decision→config workflow).

static/index.html · main.py (equity_history tracking)
phase 8 · durability

Paper account survives a restart

The paper broker (bankroll, open positions, trade log) was the last piece of runtime state still in-memory only — a restart silently reset it back to config/settings.yaml's starting_bankroll while signal_log.py's whale track record kept growing underneath it. Now persists to SQLite the same way. Fixing that surfaced a second, sharper bug: doing it for the broker alone would have kept the recovered bankroll but reset risk_manager.py's daily-loss baseline to config's number on every restart — either mismeasuring today's loss against a stale figure, or silently un-halting a kill switch that had actually tripped. Persisted that alongside it. POST /api/reset now wipes both in place rather than swapping in a new broker object (which would have left stale rows behind and gotten resurrected on the next restart). Verified against the live dev container: placed a real paper trade, ran a full ddev restart, confirmed the exact position/bankroll/trade survived.

services/paper_broker.py (+ data/paper_broker.db) · services/risk_manager.py (+ data/risk_state.db) · main.py (/api/reset)
phase 9 · hardening

CORS tightened, a real test suite, CI wired up

Three P0/P3 roadmap items in one pass, now that the project has a git repo to actually run CI against. allow_origins=["*"] was a leftover from early local-only development — replaced with a default of the DDEV hostname + localhost:8000, overridable via ALLOWED_ORIGINS in .env. Then 35 tests across four files covering paper_broker.py (fill math, cost capping, cooldowns, persistence across a simulated restart), risk_manager.py (kill-switch trip/stay-halted/resume, persistence), strategy_engine.py (every skip/trade branch), and signal_log.py (series grouping, win-rate math, time windows) — each test isolates its own SQLite file via DB_PATH monkeypatching so none of them touch the real data/*.db. Spot-checked the suite wasn't vacuous by deliberately breaking the "no"-side P&L direction and confirming the right test failed before reverting. GitHub Actions now runs the suite on every push/PR.

main.py (CORS) · .env.example · tests/*.py · pytest.ini · requirements-dev.txt · .github/workflows/tests.yml
phase 10 · the confirmation gate

Real trading needs a deliberate, typed confirmation now

Closed the gap the roadmap had flagged since before this session: nothing previously stopped kalshi_account.trading_enabled from being flipped except a comment in config/settings.yaml. POST /api/config now structurally refuses to touch that field at all — the only path is POST /api/trading/enable, which requires a real connected account and a typed, exact-match confirmation phrase ("ENABLE REAL TRADING"), not a checkbox. POST /api/trading/disable is always allowed, no confirmation — turning real trading back off is never the dangerous direction. The dashboard's account bar gets a matching control. Building the frontend piece surfaced a real bug before it shipped: the account bar re-renders from scratch on every 5-second poll, which would have silently wiped the confirmation phrase (and focus) mid-typing — fixed by capturing and restoring the input's value/selection/focus across each re-render. Verified two ways: 8 backend tests exercise every branch (wrong phrase, no account connected, correct phrase, disable) against an isolated copy of config/settings.yaml and the paper broker/risk DBs — not the real, live ones — and a real Chrome session via the project's ddev selenium-chrome service confirmed the gate renders, the confirm button enables only on an exact match, the mid-typing refresh no longer clobbers the input, and a failed backend call surfaces its real error text in the UI.

main.py (/api/trading/enable, /api/trading/disable, /api/config guard) · static/index.html (trading-gate UI) · tests/test_trading_gate.py
phase 11 · the schema was actually wrong

Order-placement request rewritten to Kalshi's current API

The one open question flagged since before this session — "re-verify the order schema before ever flipping trading_enabled" — turned out to have a real answer, and it wasn't reassuring. Fetched docs.kalshi.com directly (not a changelog, the live pages) and found the implementation targeted Kalshi's legacy order surface: POST /portfolio/orders with action+side("yes"/"no")+integer-cents yes_price/ no_price. Current docs show the endpoint moved to POST /portfolio/events/orders, collapsed action+side into one side field ("bid"/"ask" — there's no "no" value; selling YES and buying NO are the same order-book trade), switched count/price to strings instead of integer cents, and added two newly-required fields (time_in_force, self_trade_prevention_type) with no equivalent in the old shape. Kalshi's own docs note the legacy endpoint's migration deadline was "no earlier than May 6, 2026" — already past by the time this was checked — meaning the old code could have been silently rejected the first time it was ever actually used. Read endpoints (balance/positions/fills) were also checked against current docs and are unchanged. Rewrote create_order/cancel_order to the current shape; 4 new tests verify the exact request (path, method, every body field) against a faked HTTP client — no network call, no real credentials, and trading_enabled stays false throughout. Still true, and still worth repeating: no live order has ever actually been placed with this code.

services/kalshi_account_client.py · tests/test_kalshi_account_client.py
phase 12 · shadow mode

The last P0 item: logs intended real trades, executes nothing

The README has called this a prerequisite to live trading since before this project had git history — now built. services/shadow_mode.py's ShadowTrader runs the identical follow-the-whale gates strategy_engine.py uses (confidence threshold, whale win-rate filter, cooldown, position-size-rounds-to-zero) but sized against a real reference bankroll instead of the paper broker's — the connected account's actual balance when one exists, or risk.starting_bankroll as a clearly-labeled fallback when it doesn't, so this is meaningfully testable without a real Kalshi account connected. It gets its own independent daily-loss kill switch, deliberately not shared with paper trading's — sharing one would have meant a real-bankroll shadow loss silently halting the paper strategy too, or vice versa. Every gate only ever writes to data/shadow_mode.db; nothing in this path can call create_order. Active whenever mode is shadow or live — previously a YAML field nothing actually read; now a real Controls-panel dropdown. Portfolio view gets a Shadow Trades panel explaining itself in the same plain-English register as the rest of the beginner-facing UI. 10 tests cover every gate in isolation; live-verified end to end against the running app — flipped it to mode: shadow, watched a real intended-trade row appear with the correct fallback-bankroll label, confirmed the Controls dropdown and Portfolio panel both rendered it correctly in a real Chrome session, then reverted the live app back to paper and reset the paper broker (depleted from this session's extended live testing) before finishing.

services/shadow_mode.py · main.py (_shadow_reference_bankroll, /api/state shadow key) · static/index.html (Shadow Trades panel, Mode dropdown) · tests/test_shadow_mode.py
phase 13 · a real key, finally

Connected a real account — and the auth signing itself was broken

The last open P0 item needed something no amount of code review could substitute for: an actual Kalshi API key. Once one existed, connecting it immediately 401'd — on both production and demo hosts, ruling out an environment mismatch. Fetched Kalshi's own worked authentication example directly from docs.kalshi.com (path='/trade-api/v2/portfolio/balance') and found the bug: this file's signed message omitted the /trade-api/v2 prefix that path is supposed to include. The request URLs were always correct — only the signature input was wrong, which is exactly the kind of bug that produces a clean 401 with no other symptom, and exactly why this had sat unverified for so long: there was never a real key to notice with before. Fixed by deriving the correct base path from base_url itself (urlsplit(base_url).path) rather than hardcoding it a second time, so it stays correct however base_url is configured. Along the way, discovered demo and production Kalshi environments need separate credentials entirely — added KALSHI_ACCOUNT_BASE_URL so the account client can target either independently of the public market-data client, which stays on production. With auth actually working, the real response shapes finally settled the other half of this roadmap item: several guessed field names were wrong (position_fp not position, market_exposure_dollars not market_exposure, count_fp/yes_price_dollars/ no_price_dollars on fills, not count/price) and silently rendered blank for every row before now. Also found that "balance" and "portfolio_value" are two genuinely different real numbers (cash vs. cash-plus-positions) that the dashboard had been treating as interchangeable fallbacks for each other — now shown side by side, explicitly labeled. Verified against a real connected account holding real open positions and fill history, in a real Chrome session, not just curl.

services/kalshi_account_client.py (_base_path) · main.py (KALSHI_ACCOUNT_BASE_URL) · static/index.html (real position/fill rendering, Cash Balance/Portfolio Value split) · .env.example · tests/test_kalshi_account_client.py
phase 14 · SDK detour, and two small hardenings

Evaluated the official SDK, decided against it — with receipts

A real key made deeper API research worthwhile too. Investigated switching to Kalshi's official, async-native kalshi_python_async SDK — it would remove hand-rolled request-signing as a bug class entirely, which felt appealing right after finding and fixing exactly that kind of bug. Installed it and pointed it at the real connected account rather than deciding from documentation alone: it authenticates fine, but get_positions() and get_fills() both threw Pydantic ValidationError — its models require integer fields Kalshi's live API no longer sends, only the same _fp/_dollars string fields this project's own client already handles correctly. Confirmed by reading the raw response through the SDK's own escape hatch, not just trusting the traceback. Decided to stay on the hand-rolled client. One nice side effect: the SDK's own auth source hardcodes the identical /trade-api/v2 signed-path prefix this session's earlier auth fix added — independent, from-the-source confirmation that fix was exactly right.

Also shipped two smaller findings from the same documentation pass: exponential backoff on 429 (Kalshi's own rate-limit docs ask for this; nothing previously did it), shared by both Kalshi clients via a new request_with_backoff; and a header badge surfacing Kalshi's real exchange open/closed status, so a quiet signal feed reads as "market's closed," not "strategy's stuck."

services/http_client.py (request_with_backoff) · services/kalshi_client.py (get_exchange_status, backoff) · services/kalshi_account_client.py (backoff) · main.py (_fetch_exchange_status) · static/index.html (exchange status badge) · tests/test_http_client.py
phase 15 · the SDK detour, reversed — with more evidence

Full migration to the official SDK, once "decided against it" turned out to be a version artifact

Pushed back on: "the SDK shouldn't be crashing, investigate further" — phase 14's conclusion was real but incomplete. pip index versions had silently resolved kalshi_python_async to 3.2.0 under Python 3.11 with no warning that newer releases existed and needed newer Python; the real PyPI JSON API showed the true latest was 3.27.0, requiring Python ≥3.13, and matching Kalshi's current OpenAPI spec exactly. Bumped the Dockerfile to python:3.13-slim, pinned the exact SDK version (plus urllib3, an undeclared dependency the SDK needs but doesn't list — a real packaging gap on Kalshi's side, found via a genuine ModuleNotFoundError), and re-tested: every read endpoint and order placement worked cleanly against the real connected account. Given the do-over, migrated fully rather than re-verifying the old hand-rolled client — kalshi_client.py and kalshi_account_client.py were rewritten so the SDK owns signing, endpoint paths, and request/response schemas entirely, keeping the same public method signatures and dict-shaped returns so main.py barely changed. Two implementation details that only surfaced by reading the SDK's own generated source, not by guessing from its docs: _request_timeout is accepted by create_order_v2 but rejected outright by every read endpoint and by cancel_order_v2; and the 429 backoff wrapper (phase 14) became dead code the instant Kalshi calls stopped going through raw httpx, so it was rewritten as call_with_backoff to wrap SDK calls directly instead — the SDK's own retry support doesn't cover 429 at all, so this still earns its keep. Whole test suite rewritten around fakes/throwaway keys rather than real credentials.

services/kalshi_client.py · services/kalshi_account_client.py · services/http_client.py (call_with_backoff) · main.py (client lifecycle) · requirements.txt · Dockerfile · tests/test_http_client.py · tests/test_kalshi_account_client.py · tests/test_trading_gate.py
phase 16 · chasing down the 403s for real

Root-caused the recurring ddev-router 403/404s instead of restarting past them again

This exact site had gone briefly unreachable several times already this session, always "fixed" by a ddev restart — until one restart didn't fix it, which was the signal to stop treating it as noise. Read ddev-router's own generated Traefik config directly (/mnt/ddev-global-cache/traefik/config/<project>_merged.yaml) instead of guessing: it showed two routers with the identical HostRegexp rule on the same https entrypoint — one for the custom fastapi service, one for ddev's default, empty-docroot web service, which every ddev project gets whether it's used or not. Traefik's tie-break between two equal-priority routers isn't stable across reloads, so the site would randomly land on web's empty docroot (→ 403) instead of fastapi. config.yaml's existing router_http_port/router_https_port: "" (an earlier fix attempt, whose own comment described this exact symptom) never actually addressed it — those only set the ports ddev-router itself listens on, not which services get routers generated at all, which is exactly why the problem kept coming back despite it. Real fix: a docker-compose override blanking out web's own HTTP_EXPOSE/HTTPS_EXPOSE/VIRTUAL_HOST so ddev's router-config generator never creates a web router for this hostname in the first place. Verified two ways post-restart: re-read the generated config (web routers for this host: 0, was 3), and 10/10 real external HTTPS requests through the actual router path returned 200. Superseded by phase 17, immediately after — this fix worked, but left web sitting there unused; the next phase gave it an actual job instead.

.ddev/docker-compose.web-override.yaml (superseded, removed next phase)
phase 17 · a real frontend/backend split, not just a router fix

main.py is API-only now — nginx serves the dashboard, fastapi has no public URL at all

Explaining phase 16's fix prompted a fair question: why keep an idle, empty web container around at all instead of actually using it? Turned out main.py was serving the dashboard's HTML directly (FileResponse/StaticFiles) from the same process as the API — not a "frontend service" in any real sense, just one process wearing two hats. Split it for real: removed the /, /status, /login, /accounts routes and the /static mount from main.py entirely, gave web (nginx, docroot: static) the job of serving those pages directly, and had it reverse-proxy /api/ and /auth/ back to fastapi (.ddev/nginx/kalshi-proxy.conf). fastapi dropped its HTTP_EXPOSE/HTTPS_EXPOSE/VIRTUAL_HOST entirely — no public URL of its own anymore, reachable only inside the project's docker network as fastapi:8000. That doesn't just avoid phase 16's router collision, it makes it structurally impossible: there's exactly one service left that could ever register a router for this hostname. It also means the actual thing asked for is now true — a separate frontend could be built against this API later without touching main.py at all. One real correctness catch along the way: nginx's try_files <file> =404 serves a matched file from within its own location block rather than re-entering location matching, so the old NO_CACHE_HEADERS behavior (added earlier after a shipped panel silently failed to appear until a hard refresh) had to be set directly on each page's location block, not inherited from one shared regex block — caught by actually curling each route and checking headers, not assumed. Also added --proxy-headers --forwarded-allow-ips=* to uvicorn, since request.url_for() (the OAuth redirect_uri) now needs to trust a second proxy hop (nginx, in front of ddev-router) to resolve the right scheme/host.

main.py (removed page routes) · .ddev/nginx/kalshi-proxy.conf (new) · .ddev/config.yaml (docroot: static) · .ddev/docker-compose.fastapi.yaml (dropped exposure, added proxy headers) · CLAUDE.md
phase 18 · Phase 0.5 kickoff — Simple/Advanced toggle, a real drill-down, two real bugs

First slice of the Kalshi Pro-inspired dashboard overhaul, plus two bugs found along the way

Researched Kalshi Pro's actual feature set and several dedicated Kalshi/Polymarket whale-tracking products directly (not from memory) before writing a word of code — see ROADMAP.md's Phase 0.5 for the full research trail. First concrete slice, chosen for being self-contained and independently verifiable rather than trying to land the whole phase at once: a shared Simple/Advanced toggle (isAdvanced/toggleAdvanced/advToggleHTML, persisted per panel via localStorage); a genuine architectural call, not just a UI tweak — order book depth and price charts don't fit an in-card toggle (can't show a full depth ladder for 20 markets in a grid at once), so they get a dedicated per-market drill-down modal instead, closer to Kalshi Pro's Canvas; that drill-down's first tenant, order book depth, via a new GET /api/markets/{ticker}/orderbook wrapping get_orderbook() (implemented since the SDK migration, never actually called until now) — verified against both an empty real book and one with an actual resting order, driven through a real Chrome session via ddev's selenium-chrome, not just curl; potential-payout math (Kalshi contracts settle to $1 or $0, so max payout is just the contract count) added to every position/trade/fill row, Simple tier; and bounded-height scroll containers on every list panel that didn't have one, so a capped 25-50-row feed scrolls internally instead of making the page enormous.

Two real bugs turned up while wiring the header strip to follow Portfolio's paper/real account toggle (which itself was a bug: the header used to always show paper numbers under a static "PAPER" tag regardless of which account was selected below it). Fixing that surfaced a second, previously-unknown one in the same code path: main.py's real_balance_history was appending the real account's balance in raw cents instead of dividing by 100 — every consumer of that history expects dollars, so a real $1,000.00 balance would have silently rendered as "$100,000.00." Fixed at the source.

static/index.html (toggle component, header strip, drill-down modal, payout math, scroll panels) · main.py (/api/markets/{ticker}/orderbook, real_balance_history fix)
phase 19 · markets stop pretending to be strangers

Event/outcome grouping + clearer row labels, continuing Phase 0.5 in roadmap order

Next item in ROADMAP.md's Phase 0.5 list, marked foundational there for a reason: it changes what every other panel in the phase is describing. Two fixes. First, terminology — position/trade/fill rows across renderPositions/renderTrades/renderRealPositions/ renderRealFills said things like 500 ct @ 62¢ → 65¢ with nothing labeling which number was which; now they read "500 contracts @ 62¢ entry → 65¢ now" (or "fill price" for a completed trade), with a tooltip spelling out each figure. Second, and bigger: event_ticker — the field that ties sibling outcome-markets of the same underlying question together — was being fetched from Kalshi and dropped immediately in _slim_market(), so a real multi-outcome event (confirmed directly against a live 200-market batch: several events genuinely had more than one sibling market) had no way to render as anything but N fully disconnected cards. Carried it through, added a get_event() wrapper to fetch each event's own title/category, and only for events that actually have more than one sibling in the current watchlist batch — a solo-outcome market (the common case) never pays for a lookup it doesn't need, cached the same way market_titles already is. renderMarketCards now groups sibling markets under one event header instead of showing them as strangers. Verified two ways: the grouping logic directly against real sibling events pulled from Kalshi, and the actual rendering by injecting a synthetic multi-sibling state through a real Chrome session and confirming the group/solo split rendered correctly, not just that the code looked right.

main.py (_slim_market, _fetch_event_titles) · services/kalshi_client.py (get_event) · static/index.html (renderMarketCards grouping, row label wording)
phase 20 · real candlesticks, hand-rolled, in the drill-down

Price history joins order book in the per-market modal — a real API quirk caught before it shipped wrong

Next item in Phase 0.5's drill-down group. get_market_candlesticks requires a series_ticker, which - checked directly, not assumed - market objects don't carry (only event_ticker does); getting it wrong is a hard API error, not a quietly-off number, so this resolves it with one real get_event() lookup per drill-down open rather than guessing from the ticker string (a naive ticker.split('-')[0] heuristic matched on every sample tried, but "usually true" isn't the bar for a required, strictly-validated field). New GET /api/markets/{ticker}/candlesticks (fixed window: last 7 days, hourly) and a get_candlesticks() wrapper. Simple is a compact sparkline, matching the equity chart's existing hand-rolled inline-SVG approach; Advanced is real OHLC candlesticks with a volume bar per period, same no-library approach, just more of it - wicks, bodies colored by up/down, volume scaled underneath. One data quirk caught by actually reading a real response instead of assuming the shape: candlestick price.* fields come back null for any period with no actual trade (common on thin markets), which would have silently drawn the line dropping to zero - falls back to the yes_bid/ yes_ask midpoint, then carries the last known value forward. The chart gets its own Simple/Advanced toggle, independent of the order book's directly below it - verified live in Chrome that toggling one doesn't touch the other, and that Advanced's rendered candle/volume-bar count matches the real API response exactly.

main.py (/api/markets/{ticker}/candlesticks) · services/kalshi_client.py (get_candlesticks) · static/index.html (renderCandlestickHTML, candleClose)
phase 21 · per-market trades, then a real complaint: stop making me do math

Recent trades finish the drill-down; then direct feedback drove a real terminology + a real bug fix

Started with the next queued item — recent trades for one market in the drill-down, via a new GET /api/markets/{ticker}/trades and get_trades() wrapper. Simpler than the candlestick work: get_trades takes a plain ticker filter, no series_ticker lookup needed. No Simple/Advanced split — a short recent-trades list doesn't have a meaningfully denser "Advanced" form the way the book/chart do.

Then direct feedback, mid-session, made the remaining gap concrete: a real example ticker (KXMVESPORTSMULTIGAMEEXTENDED-S2026CDE6FF21DD8-582E1DFB434) that a person shouldn't have to parse, plus contracts-times-price math nobody should have to do in their head. Investigating it surfaced a real bug in the same-day event-title code: it read event.get("subtitle"), which doesn't exist — the real field is sub_title (confirmed directly against a live event: "SD vs AZ (Aug 6)" only came back under that key) — so every event's subtitle had silently been None since it shipped a few phases ago. Fixed, and broadened event fetching from "only multi-sibling groups" to every market's event, since the same data also answers "what sport, who vs who" for a single market, not just grouping. market_titles now carries a small object ({title, yes_sub_title, no_sub_title, event_ticker}) instead of one collapsed string, so a Yes/No position's actual meaning is available, not just a side tag. New marketContext()/ contextLineHTML() combine that with event_titles to show category, real matchup, and position meaning on position/trade/fill rows, market cards, and the drill-down title; new costHTML() shows contracts × price as an actual dollar total, next to the existing max-payout figure. Verified through a real Chrome session with realistic clean-market data (an MLB matchup, not the messy combo markets this account's current watchlist happens to favor): a position row correctly read "Sports · SD vs AZ (Aug 6) · Betting: San Diego" plus "$6.20 put in."

main.py (/api/markets/{ticker}/trades, _fetch_event_titles fix + broadening, market_titles shape) · services/kalshi_client.py (get_trades) · static/index.html (marketContext, contextLineHTML, costHTML, renderMarketTradesHTML)
phase 22 · a trade tape scoped to what you're actually watching

Whale Watch gets a real trade tape, deliberately not the whole exchange's

Last drill-down-adjacent item in Phase 0.5's list: a full trade tape, distinct from the per-market drill-down's own recent-trades list built two phases ago. get_trades with no ticker filter returns trades across every market on Kalshi, not just the ones this app is watching - most of that would be noise next to the whale-signal concept the tape ties into, so new _fetch_trade_tape() instead fires one get_trades(ticker=X, limit=5) per watched market concurrently (the same pattern _fetch_markets already uses for its own explicit-watchlist branch), merges, and sorts newest-first. Simple shows only the top quartile by size in the current batch, in plain English; Advanced shows the full tape with a minimum-size filter, matched directly against how Polywhaler and WhaleScanr actually let their own users filter a tape (researched earlier this phase, not invented fresh here). New panel in the Whale Watch tab with its own Simple/Advanced toggle. One small real gap caught by testing through an actual browser session instead of just reading the code back: the filter's <input> had no id, so nothing could ever target it programmatically - harmless for a person clicking it directly, but a real oversight; fixed and reverified. Verified live with synthetic trades: Simple correctly surfaced only the two larger of four trades in plain English; Advanced showed all four, and setting the size filter correctly narrowed it back down to just those two.

main.py (_fetch_trade_tape, /api/state trade_tape) · static/index.html (renderTradeTape, tradeRowHTML)
phase 23 · the watchlist was showing garbage, and it took three rounds to actually fix it

Market discovery was fundamentally broken — not under-filtered, broken

Started from real, provided screenshots of actual Kalshi UI and a direct correction: "you're wrong about the multi-level parlay markets being all that's open." Investigating that assumption instead of defending it led somewhere much bigger than a display bug. Confirmed directly, repeatedly, with real numbers: a flat browse of Kalshi's open markets — even paginating 50,000+ of them via cursor — can return zero with any real trading volume, because Kalshi auto-generates a huge number of "MVE" (multivariate/combo) markets that vastly outnumber real ones in the API's default ordering. get_top_volume_markets had been sorting a 100-market page that was itself already dead.

Three rounds, each correcting the last based on real data rather than reasoning from assumptions: (1) tried mve_filter="exclude" — worked, but "you shouldn't be filtering out markets at all if possible" was fair pushback, since a combo market with real activity is still a real market. (2) Switched to a pure volume filter, "only filter out markets with 0 volume" — correct in principle, but empirically a full unfiltered 1000-market sample had zero markets with any volume at that moment, confirmed three times in a row, not a fluke: combos are numerous enough that even a large unfiltered sample can be entirely dead. (3) The actual fix, verified rather than assumed at every step per an explicit instruction not to guess at Kalshi's data model: get_series_list(include_volume=True) returns all ~12,500 series (a series is a recurring-event template — "Pro Basketball Game," confirmed via the SDK's own docstring) with real lifetime volume in one ~1.1s call. Querying get_markets(series_ticker=...) against just the highest-volume currently-active series reliably returns clean, real, well-titled markets — verified directly against KXMLBGAME, KXPGATOUR, KXBTCD, and KXATPMATCH. One more real SDK gotcha caught along the way: passing mve_filter=None explicitly (instead of omitting the kwarg) silently changed the result set at the wire level — 271 real markets found vs. 0. Cached in state["series_cache"], refreshed hourly rather than every 15s poll tick, since the full series fetch is too expensive to repeat that often. The watchlist now reliably surfaces things like PGA Tour and Bitcoin markets instead of KXMVESPORTSMULTIGAMEEXTENDED-* tickers — verified repeatedly against live data, not declared fixed after one good result.

services/kalshi_client.py (get_series_list, rewritten get_top_volume_markets) · main.py (_get_series_cache, _get_top_series) · config/settings.yaml (min_volume_24h, categories)
phase 24 · a Config tab, real market search, and outcome rows instead of duplicate cards

Search and pin anything (dormant included), and multi-outcome events finally look like Kalshi's own

Two more fixes building directly on phase 23's series-based discovery. First, a new Config tab (5th tab) — the Terminal column's settings form (strategy/risk/whale-signal/mode) moved there, Terminal keeps just the frequently-used operational buttons (Pause/Reset/Kill Switch/Resume). Second, real market search: GET /api/markets/search reuses phase 23's series cache — an earlier version of this endpoint browsed markets directly (even cursor-paginating 5,000+ for one text query) and hit the exact same reliability wall, confirmed directly rather than assumed fixed; text-matching against series title/tags/category first, then querying only matching series, is what actually works. Defaults to full, dormant-inclusive access (min_volume=0) — a market missing from the automatic watchlist was never supposed to mean unreachable, only "not the default view." Results are checkbox-multi-selectable; "Add Selected to Watchlist" merges picks into the already-existing kalshi.markets_watchlist config field via the existing /api/config endpoint — it already preferred pinned markets over automatic discovery, it just had no UI besides hand-editing settings.yaml until now.

Third, prompted by the same real Kalshi screenshots: multi-outcome events (the earlier event/outcome- grouping work) were rendering as N full cards side by side under one header — better than N disconnected cards, but still not how real Kalshi shows it, and still "a clear lack of data for any market in particular." The screenshots showed the real pattern directly: one card, a compact list of outcome rows (name + Yes/No price), not duplicate cards. Rebuilt to match exactly that, each row clickable straight into that specific outcome's drill-down. Also cleared a stale paper position left over from earlier in the session (from back when the watchlist was still combo-dominated) via the app's own POST /api/reset rather than touching data/paper_broker.db directly. Verified end-to-end through real Chrome sessions: searched "golf," multi-selected two Wyndham Championship outcomes, added them to the pinned watchlist, removed one and confirmed it; separately confirmed an event card renders the right category/title/sorted-rows and that clicking a row opens the correct outcome's drill-down.

main.py (/api/markets/search, Config tab wiring) · static/index.html (Config tab, searchMarkets, renderPinnedWatchlist, eventGroupCardHTML)
phase 25 · a real LIVE badge (not a guess), and Portfolio finally looks like Kalshi's own

Chasing down the actual milestone/live-data system, then a full positions-table rewrite

A direct instruction to spend the effort and not guess on the "LIVE" badge paid off. First attempt was a timestamp-only heuristic - openly flagged as not the same guarantee as Kalshi's real signal - and pushed further instead of stopping there. Kalshi's live status turned out to be powered by a separate milestone/ live-data system, not exposed on the market or event object at all: get_milestones (related_event_ticker=...) is the missing link, returning the actual scheduled game/match tied to an event, whose id/type then feed get_live_data() for the real status. One hypothesis tested and killed before it shipped: a milestone's end_date stays null forever, even for a fully settled game, so it can't be used as a live-window boundary. The real signal, confirmed by watching a real AFL match go live at its actual scheduled start time: details.widget_status goes "none" → "live" → "finished." New _fetch_live_status() checks this only for markets whose occurrence_datetime falls in a plausible live window, replaced wholesale every poll tick rather than accumulated (a stale "live" is actively wrong, not just incomplete). Verified against real production data mid-build: a genuine in-progress tennis match was correctly flagged live in the running app, not just a synthetic test.

Second half of the same request round, with a real screenshot of Kalshi's own positions table: renderPositions rewritten from one flat line item per position into a real grouped table - positions sharing an event grouped under one market header with a Total row, matching the screenshot's exact columns (Position, Contracts, Entry, Now, Cost, Payout if right, Value, Return) rather than invented ones. Added a whale-confidence badge per position (reusing computeWhaleLean, already built for Whale Watch's cards, never applied to Portfolio) and a 🐋 icon expanding to show why a position was opened - Trade.reason had already been computed, persisted, and sent to the frontend in broker.recent_trades the whole time, just never displayed. Also investigated, on request, whether Kalshi exposes a "largest open positions on this market" dataset - confirmed three independent ways (SDK method introspection, Kalshi's own API reference endpoint index, and Kalshi's LLM-oriented docs index, which states outright that no such endpoint exists) that this data genuinely isn't public; the real "Kalshi Leaderboard" ranks overall trader performance, unrelated to per-market position size. Not built, since there's no data to build it from - open_interest_fp (aggregate, not per-holder) is the closest real substitute, noted for later. Verified end-to-end through a real Chrome session: correct grouping, a Total row only on the multi-position group, correct sums, and the reason details expanding to real trade data on click.

services/kalshi_client.py (get_milestones_for_event, get_live_data) · main.py (_fetch_live_status) · static/index.html (isLive, liveBadgeHTML, rewritten renderPositions, whaleConfidenceBadgeHTML, reasonDetailsHTML)
phase 26 · sortable trade/decision tables, an observe-live-only gate, and a self-serve reset

Three separate direct requests, all shipped and tested in one pass

Trade Log and Strategy Decisions both got an Advanced mode: renderTrades/ renderDecisions now offer a real sortable, filterable table alongside the existing plain-English card feed, reusing the same Simple/Advanced toggle every other panel already uses. Trade Log's table adds a P&L column (current price vs. fill price, the same math renderPositions already does) and a Confidence column pulled out of Trade.reason's formatted text via a new parseConfidence() - that number had only ever existed embedded in a sentence, never its own field. Strategy Decisions' table adds an action filter (trade/skip). Both share new small sortRows/toggleSort/ sortHeaderHTML helpers - click a column header to sort by it, click again to reverse. Verified through a real Chrome session with synthetic data: correct P&L math, correct sort order and direction toggling, and both filters actually excluding the rows they should.

Second request: a configurable "live markets only" trading gate, so real-world strategy accuracy can be observed in isolation from thin/pre-market signal noise. New strategy.live_markets_only config field (off by default) makes both FollowTheWhaleStrategy.evaluate() and ShadowTrader.evaluate() skip/decline any signal on a market that isn't currently live - deliberately reusing the exact same live-status lookup the LIVE badge already shows (state["live_status"]) rather than inventing a second definition of "live." main.py's trading loop resolves ticker → event_ticker → live status once per signal and passes it to both evaluators. Config tab gets a matching checkbox; 5 new tests cover on/off and live/not-live for both evaluators.

Third request: a self-serve "Reset Paper Account & Logs" button in a new Config tab Danger Zone group - the exact same POST /api/reset Terminal's Controls button already calls (paper bankroll/positions/trade log, daily-loss baseline, in-memory signal/decision feed; persisted whale track record and shadow trades are deliberately untouched, since those track long-run accuracy across resets), just reachable without asking for it every time. No new endpoint needed. Also folded in: a project-scoped Claude Code hook (.claude/hooks/run_tests.py) that runs the test suite automatically after editing main.py or any services/*.py file, and a pass condensing this file's sibling, ROADMAP.md, down from ~820 lines to keep completed-item narrative from crowding out the still-open to-do list - the full detail for every condensed item is still exactly this page, plus git log for anything committed.

services/strategy_engine.py, services/shadow_mode.py (live_markets_only gate) · main.py (is_live resolution, /api/reset already existed) · static/index.html (Advanced trade/decision tables, sort helpers, Danger Zone) · tests/test_strategy_engine.py, tests/test_shadow_mode.py
phase 27 · selectable-domain reset, and Open Positions → full market detail

Two more direct requests

First: phase 26's Danger Zone reset was paper-only, with shadow trades and the whale track record deliberately untouched - useful as a default, but there was no way to also wipe those short of asking for it. POST /api/reset now takes an optional JSON body ({paper, shadow, signal_log}, each independently toggleable, paper defaulting true so the Terminal Controls button's old no-body call still behaves exactly as before) and reports back which domains it actually cleared. New ShadowTrader.clear() wipes every logged shadow trade and resets the shadow daily-loss baseline; new signal_log.clear_all() wipes the whale track record. The Config tab's Danger Zone is now three checkboxes (Paper checked by default, Shadow and Whale track record opt-in) behind a single "Reset Selected" button, with a confirm() prompt naming exactly what's about to be wiped. Verified: new unit tests for both clear() methods (including that a fresh instance re-reading the same on-disk DB sees the wipe, not just the in-memory object), plus live curl proving each flag combination reports back the right domain list.

Second: "essentially all the data on the market landing page on Kalshi itself," reachable by clicking a market straight from Open Positions - previously the per-market drill-down modal (phase 19-ish: order book, price chart, recent trades) existed but wasn't wired up from Portfolio at all, and even where it was reachable it never showed event context, current prices, volume, or sibling outcomes the way Kalshi's own market page does. New GET /api/markets/{ticker}/detail bundles one get_market() call with one get_event() call - for a multi-outcome event, every sibling market's own live price comes back as part of that same event payload, no per-sibling round trip needed. Open Positions' group-title row and every individual position row are now clickable into the modal. New renderMarketInfoHTML() renders a category tag, event title/subtitle, current Yes/No prices with a 24h change arrow, 24h volume/open interest/status/close time, the rules text, and - for multi-outcome events - an "Other Outcomes In This Event" list (reusing the existing .outcome-row styling from eventGroupCardHTML), each row clickable to jump the modal straight to that sibling. Verified live against a real multi-outcome event (an ITF tennis match) via a synthetic paper position: the group title opened the modal, the sibling row's price/name matched the real exchange, clicking it re-fetched into that sibling, and no console errors at any step.

phase 28 · /api/state efficiency pass, plus two real bugs caught live along the way

"Make data consumption, storage, and retrieval hyper-efficient" - measured before touching anything

Direct request, but deliberately investigated before implementing anything - the request named "storage" as a concern, and data/*.db turned out to be a red herring: 332KB total, and PRAGMA freelist_count showed most of even signal_log.db's share was empty pages left over from testing phase 27's reset-domains feature minutes earlier, not organic growth. The real weight was GET /api/state itself: 43.8KB per fetch, polled every 5s (setInterval(refresh, 5000)) regardless of whether the backend's own poll_interval_sec (15s default) had produced anything new - meaning roughly two of every three polls re-sent and re-parsed byte-for-byte identical data. Follow-up clarification from the requester ("keeping polling intervals as short as possible... it's data latency I'm concerned about, not space") ruled out the tempting-but-wrong fix (just slow the frontend poll down) in favor of making an unchanged poll cheap instead of infrequent.

Fix: a state["generation"] counter, bumped once at the end of every trading-loop poll tick and by every control endpoint that mutates state outside the loop (/api/toggle, /api/risk/halt, /api/risk/resume, /api/reset, /api/config, /api/trading/enable, /api/trading/disable, and - caught by audit, see the bug below - GET /api/markets/search), used as /api/state's ETag. An unchanged poll now gets a 304 with no body instead of 43.8KB re-fetched and re-parsed; live curl confirmed a real 304 round trip. The response body itself is memoized per generation too, so signal_log.stats()/shadow.recent()/ shadow.stats() - real SQLite queries - now run once per actual change instead of once per HTTP request. One real-environment wrinkle caught live, not assumed: nginx's gzip module rewrites a strong ETag to weak ("2" → W/"2") on the way out, which silently broke a first, naive exact-string comparison - fixed with an RFC-7232-style weak comparison that strips a W/ prefix before comparing. Separately, account.fills/ account.positions - full raw Kalshi objects, 13.4KB of the 43.8KB, mostly serving debug tooltips and the raw-JSON detail view rather than the rendered rows themselves - got the same _slim_market-style trim (new _slim_position/_slim_fill, field names taken from exactly what renderRealPositions/renderRealFills already read, not re-guessed). WebSockets came up as an alternative and were deliberately deferred: the dominant latency source here is poll_interval_sec itself, not the polling transport, so the marginal win didn't clear the added complexity (reconnect/backoff, multi-tab fanout, an nginx Upgrade-handshake config change) - worth revisiting only if 15s-granularity updates still feel slow after this pass.

Two real bugs turned up during live verification of the above, both confirmed via Selenium, not assumed fixed from reading the diff. First, a genuine regression from the new caching itself: GET /api/markets/search writes into state["market_titles"] but wasn't bumping the new generation counter, so a freshly-searched market's resolved title wouldn't propagate to /api/state - and therefore to Open Positions or the detail modal - until the next real poll tick caught up some 15s later, reported live as titles "reverting" to raw tickers like KXITFMATCH-26AUG08PLEPAP-PAP. Fixed by adding the missing bump. Second, a pre-existing bug, unrelated to this pass but surfaced during the same live testing: the market- detail modal's "click outside to dismiss" backdrop handler only checked the click event's final target, but refreshMarketDetail() replaces the modal body's innerHTML on every poll tick while the modal is open - so a mousedown-then-mouseup that straddled one of those replacements got its click event retargeted by the browser to the backdrop itself (the nearest ancestor still in the DOM), silently closing the modal mid-interaction. Reported live as "refresh forces closing of modals while I'm trying to examine them." Fixed by also requiring mousedown to have started on the backdrop itself (new __backdropMouseDownOnSelf flag) before a click counts as "outside" - verified with a real mousedown → DOM-swap → mouseup race via Selenium ActionChains (modal stayed open) alongside a genuine outside click (still closes it, so the fix didn't just disable the feature).

main.py (state["generation"], _bump_generation, _build_state_body, _if_none_match_hits, _slim_position, _slim_fill) · static/index.html (__backdropMouseDownOnSelf + the backdrop's mousedown/click handlers)
phase 29 · whale simulator: relative sizing, volume-weighted selection, composite confidence

"More accurately reflect real-world behavior and volatility" - built from research already on file

Direct request, pointed explicitly at reusing research already sitting in ROADMAP.md from an earlier pass rather than re-deriving it: two long-open P2 items had already confirmed, by reading real Kalshi/Polymarket whale-tracker products directly, that (1) WhaleScanr sizes a "whale" relative to each market's own trade-size distribution - "roughly the top few percent of that market's own trades, plus an absolute dollar floor" - not one flat number across every market, and (2) Polywhaler's "Insider Score" is a composite of trade size relative to market depth, how unusual the price/timing is, proximity to resolution, and broader market context, not a single factor. Both items had already flagged whale_simulator.py's flat size_range pick and single size-based confidence score as exactly the gap.

Implemented both, scoped to the simulator only (the request named "the fake whale watcher service" specifically - whalewatchers/generic_rest.py's matching single-factor score is real- provider code, left as its own still-open item). New _pick_market() weights market selection by volume_24h_fp instead of a flat random.choice, so simulated whale attention concentrates where a market actually has liquidity and price action - the "volatility" half of the request. New _size_for() draws log-normal (real large-trade sizes cluster with a heavy right tail, not uniformly) centered on 3% of the chosen market's own 24h volume, with the configured size_range reinterpreted as an absolute floor/cap rather than a flat pick range. New _score_confidence() replaces the single size-based number with Polywhaler's four weighted factors (depth 40%, price-unusualness 25%, close-time proximity 20%, market-context 15%) plus noise. Verified against the live app, not just unit tests: real near-resolved markets (3¢/90¢ prices) now correctly score low confidence (0.11-0.18) despite large sizes, where the old size-only version would have scored a big print on a foregone conclusion the same as one on a genuine toss-up. 12 new tests in tests/test_whale_simulator.py, one per factor plus edge cases (zero-volume markets, missing/malformed close_time) - this module had no dedicated tests before.

services/whale_simulator.py (_pick_market, _size_for, _score_confidence) · new tests/test_whale_simulator.py
phase 30 · connectivity/staleness badge and price-change indicators

Working through ROADMAP.md's remaining Phase 0.5 items in order, autonomously

First of a self-directed pass through the roadmap's still-open items, in the order they already appear in the file. Visible staleness/connectivity state: refresh()'s catch block used to be a silent console.error and nothing else - for an app whose whole premise is "watch this and trust what it shows you," a dead connection deserved the same loud treatment the exchange-open/closed badge already gets. New #connectivity-badge in the header tracks consecutive refresh() failures and seconds since the last real success, shows a pulsing "⚠ CONNECTION LOST · Ns" once anything fails, clears on the next success. Along the way, switched refresh()'s own /api/state fetch off the shared fetchJSON helper to add an explicit res.ok check - fetchJSON doesn't check status, so a 500 with a valid-JSON error body (FastAPI's default unhandled-exception shape) would otherwise parse "successfully" and silently masquerade as a real update. Verified live via Selenium: a simulated network failure, a simulated 500-with-JSON-body, and recovery back to a clean badge.

Price-change indicators: every price in the app used to silently replace on each 5s poll with zero acknowledgment it moved. New shared priceChangeHTML(ticker, price) compares against previousPrices (last poll's snapshot, updated at the end of refresh() only after every render that needed the old value has run) and emits a brief flash + arrow + ¢ /% delta - but only on the tick where the price actually changed, so it disappears again on its own once the price settles, no separate timer needed. Wired into the three highest-value spots - Terminal's compact list, Markets/Whale Watch cards, and Portfolio's positions "Now" column - deliberately simplified from the roadmap item's original Simple-flash/Advanced-delta split into one universal compact format, since the Advanced tier's real destination (the screener table further down this same roadmap section) doesn't exist yet. Skipped the compact multi-outcome eventGroupCardHTML rows on purpose - already dense, a flashing badge per row read as clutter rather than signal. Verified live: three synthetic price sequences (up, down, unchanged) across all three render paths, confirming the correct arrow/delta and correct disappearance, zero console errors.

static/index.html (#connectivity-badge, renderConnectivity, priceChangeHTML, previousPrices)
phase 31 · signal feed filters, sort, grouping, and card enrichment

Continuing the self-directed roadmap pass

renderSignals/the Terminal signal feed had zero filter or sort controls and a thin card (ticker · side · size · confidence%). Matched against Polywhaler's proven set rather than invented from scratch (researched directly, same research already on file from the whale-simulator pass): a filter bar (reusing the existing .table-filter-row styling) for time range (1h/6h/24h/7d/30d/all) and side (Yes/No), a sort toggle between recency and "impact," and a position-grouping checkbox. "Impact" isn't a field this app tracks - defined as size × confidence, tiered low/medium/high relative to the currently visible feed rather than a flat cutoff, the same relative-not-flat lesson the whale-simulator sizing fix just applied one phase earlier. Position-grouping combines every signal on the same ticker+side into one card with a "N× prints" tag - a light, client-side, non-persisted taste of real accumulation detection, explicitly not a replacement for the P2 stretch item's actual same-actor clustering.

Card enrichment, same pass: added position size in both $ (new) and contracts (already shown) alongside the new impact tag. Deliberately skipped 24h price change, not silently - this app has no per-ticker historical price data available in bulk (candlesticks are fetched on-demand per market in the drill-down modal, one API call per ticker, not something to fan out across every ticker on a live feed), so faking that number wasn't an option. Verified live via synthetic signals spanning old/ new timestamps and both sides: time-range and side filters correctly excluded what they should, impact sort correctly ordered highest-impact first, and group-by-market correctly combined two same-ticker signals into one 2×-prints card, zero console errors throughout.

static/index.html (renderSignals rewrite, signalFilter, signalImpact, .impact-tag)
phase 32 · a real, browsable signal history

WhaleScanr's framing, copied directly: "every flag and how it settled, misses included"

The Whale Watch tab's "Whale Track Record" cards only ever showed the rolled-up percentage - win rate, resolved count - even though services/signal_log.py has persisted every individual signal and its resolution outcome since it was built. No UI existed to actually browse them. New signal_log.recent(limit, offset, resolved_only) and signal_log.total_count(), backing a new GET /api/signals/history endpoint - deliberately a separate, on-demand/paginated fetch, not folded into the /api/state poll cycle the way most of this dashboard's data is. That distinction mattered: a live-refreshing history table would fight with someone actively paging or sorting through it, resetting their place every 5 seconds.

New "Signal History" panel on Whale Watch, right below the existing track-record cards: a "Resolved only" checkbox, sortable columns (market/side/size/confidence/seen-at, reusing the existing sortRows/toggleSort/sortHeaderHTML helpers built for the Trade Log/Decisions tables), a pending/correct/miss outcome column, and Prev/Next pagination. Loads once when the tab opens (hooked into showView()) and again only on filter/sort/ page changes. 4 new tests in tests/test_signal_log.py covering ordering, pagination, and the resolved-only filter - including that an unresolved signal's correct comes back null, not conflated with a resolved-and-wrong 0. Verified live against the real, already-populated signal_log.db (100+ logged signals): correct pagination count, working sort toggle, working filter, zero console errors.

services/signal_log.py (recent, total_count) · main.py (GET /api/signals/history) · static/index.html (#signal-history panel, loadSignalHistory, renderSignalHistory) · tests/test_signal_log.py
phase 33 · shared screener table, and Phase 0.5's remaining items closed out

One renderer, three panels, and a resolved Markets-vs-Whale-Watch split

Three separate market-list renderers existed before this - renderMarkets for Terminal's compact column (the thinnest of the three: ticker, YES price, volume, nothing else), renderMarketCards for Markets and Whale Watch's card grids, and nothing shared between any of them. New renderScreenerTable/renderScreenerTableFromState: a dense, sortable table (Market/Category/Yes/No/Spread/24h Volume, plus a Whale Lean column when includeWhale is set) reused across all three former call sites as each panel's Advanced mode, each with its own independent sort state (screenerState, keyed by panel id - same pattern as tradeLogFilter/decisionFilter). Two Kalshi-Pro-screener columns were deliberately left out rather than faked: real order-book depth would cost one extra API call per market per poll tick (the exact kind of request-fan-out growth the earlier /api/state efficiency pass worked to avoid), and "5-minute volume" would need far more trade data than _fetch_trade_tape actually pulls (5 trades/market, 30 total across the whole watchlist - nowhere near enough for a real rolling figure). 24h volume is shown instead of a number that would quietly be wrong on anything busier than a handful of trades. Spread came for free - yes_ask_dollars was already on every market object _fetch_markets fetches, just never exposed through _MARKET_FIELDS before.

That table also resolved the standing "reassess the Markets vs. Whale Watch split" question, which had sat open since Phase 0.5 began - both tabs rendered near-identical cards off the same function. Decided for "give them a genuinely distinct job," not "fold them into one filtered tab": the merge option would have meant cramming Whale Watch's now-substantial whale-specific content (Track Record cards, the new browsable Signal History panel, Trade Tape) alongside general market discovery behind a toggle, awkward for both jobs. Instead, Markets stayed pure discovery (includeWhale=false everywhere); Whale Watch keeps its three whale-specific sections and renders its cards/table whale-annotated (includeWhale=true - lean blocks in Simple, a Whale Lean column in Advanced). Same renderer, same code, genuinely different output per tab now - not just a different label over identical content. This also closes out Phase 0.5 as a whole: every remaining open item in that section is now checked off except the polling-model item, which was already deliberately left open pending a WebSockets decision two phases back. Verified live across all three panels: Advanced renders the table, a header click re-sorts, toggling back to Simple restores the original card/list view exactly, and the Whale Lean column appears only on Whale Watch's table.

main.py (_MARKET_FIELDS + yes_ask_dollars) · static/index.html (renderScreenerTable, renderScreenerTableFromState, screenerState, screenerRowData)
phase 34 · a live-markets-only toggle one level upstream, on the simulator itself

Direct follow-up to phase 22's strategy-level gate

The existing strategy.live_markets_only gate (built a few phases back) only decides whether a signal that already exists gets acted on - a thin/pre-market print still shows up in the feed, it just never becomes a trade. Direct request for a matching toggle one level upstream: whether the simulator generates a print at all for a non-live market in the first place. New whale_signal.live_markets_only (default off, config-only - deliberately a separate flag from the strategy one, not a shared name, since they gate genuinely different stages of the pipeline). WhaleSimulator.maybe_generate() takes new live_status/ live_only params and filters candidate markets down to live_status.get(event_ticker) == "live" before picking one - same state["live_status"] lookup the LIVE badge and the strategy gate both already use, no third definition of "live" introduced. If the filtered set comes up empty, no signal fires that tick at all, rather than quietly falling back to a non-live market. One subtlety worth a real fix, not left as a rough edge: _score_confidence()'s "market context" factor (how busy this market is relative to the rest of the batch) now compares against the same live-filtered candidate set when the toggle is on, not the full unfiltered watchlist - otherwise a live market's "context" score would be diluted by comparison against quiet markets it was never actually competing with for selection.

Config tab gets a matching checkbox under Whale Signal (simulated), next to the existing size-range fields. 4 new tests in tests/test_whale_simulator.py: on with a live market present, on with none available (correctly emits nothing rather than falling back), off ignores live_status entirely, and the parameter-omitted default behaves as off. Verified live end to end - toggled via the Config tab UI, confirmed the flip round-tripped correctly through GET /api/config, reverted back to off afterward so the test didn't leave the running app in a changed state.

services/whale_simulator.py (maybe_generate live-status filtering) · main.py (both maybe_generate call sites) · config/settings.yaml (whale_signal.live_markets_only) · static/index.html (Config tab checkbox) · tests/test_whale_simulator.py
phase 35 · watchlist diversification: round-robin across events, not a flat volume sort

A real bug caught live via direct report: "the only market that shows up isn't even live"

Reported directly against the running app: only one market visible, and it wasn't even live, despite Kalshi itself showing 50 live markets at the time. Diagnosed against real data, not guessed - the entire 8-market watchlist turned out to be every sub-market of one Wyndham Championship golf tournament, starting in ~15 hours (well outside the live-detection window), while live_status came back completely empty. Root cause: get_top_volume_markets (phase "market discovery" a while back) fixed what gets browsed but still picked the final watchlist via a flat top-n-by-volume sort with zero diversification - one high-volume multi-outcome event, every sub-market individually ranking in the global top 8, can silently fill the entire watchlist and crowd out every other series, confirmed live, not hypothetical.

First fix tried and explicitly replaced before shipping: a flat per-event cap (max 2 markets per event). It solves the crowding-out case but creates the mirror problem - a genuinely multi-outcome event's own legitimate sub-markets get needlessly truncated to 2 even when nothing else is competing for the remaining slots, caught by the requester asking "would there be a way to dynamically adjust caps... to avoid cutoffs." Shipped instead: round-robin selection across distinct events (still highest-volume-first within each event) - no hardcoded per-event number at all. The effective share per event self-sizes: it shrinks automatically as more distinct events compete for the same n slots, and grows toward the full watchlist size when one event genuinely has no real competition. Verified directly against the live Kalshi API (not just unit tests): the watchlist went from 1 distinct event / 0 confirmed-live markets to 20 distinct events (tennis, MLB, Bitcoin, UFC, WNBA) / 4 confirmed-live. 6 new tests in a first dedicated tests/test_kalshi_client.py (this module had none before) - including the "only one real event available" case explicitly, so the round-robin behavior doesn't regress back toward either extreme.

Same pass, direct follow-up request ("increase the caps throughout the API to something more sensible"): audited every numeric limit in the poll loop and bumped the ones sized for an 8-market watchlist that hadn't been revisited since - watchlist_size 8→20, top-series candidates 15→30, per-market trade-tape fetch 5→10 (total tape cap 30→50), unresolved-signal-resolution batch 3→10 per tick (and switched that function from sequential to concurrent fetching in the same change, so the bigger batch doesn't stack up round-trip latency within one poll tick), real-account fills page size 25→50, the long-run market_titles/event_titles caps 300→500, and equity_history/real_balance_history 200→500 points.

services/kalshi_client.py (get_top_volume_markets round-robin rewrite) · main.py (cap bumps, _check_signal_resolutions concurrency) · config/settings.yaml (watchlist_size) · new tests/test_kalshi_client.py
phase 36 · P1 closed out: Help modal, plain-English skip reasons, a real-money banner

"A first-timer understands what's happening" - all five P1 items, one pass

First-run walkthrough, persistent glossary, and a "how prediction markets work" explainer were three separate roadmap bullets but really the same job - built as one combined Help modal instead of three surfaces. New #help-backdrop reuses the market-detail modal's exact chrome and mousedown-guard outside-click fix (phase 30-ish) rather than a new pattern: a plain-English prediction-markets primer (what a price means as a probability, why Yes + No ≈ 100¢, what settlement means) plus an 11-term glossary written in terms of how this app actually uses each word, not textbook definitions pulled from nowhere. Auto-opens once on a genuinely fresh browser (a localStorage flag set the same way whether it opened itself or someone clicked the new util-bar "Help" link), never reopens itself automatically after that. Deliberately scoped down from "walkthrough" in the step-by-step-guided-tour sense - highlighting individual UI elements one at a time needs real positioning/overlay machinery this app doesn't have; reading one reference page once is an honest, smaller bar, not the full ask. Verified live with real localStorage clearing: opens fresh, stays closed after being dismissed once, reopens on demand.

Plain-English skip reasons: strategy_engine.py's _skip() reasons stayed exactly as they were (concise technical strings, correct for logs and Advanced mode) - a new plainEnglishSkipReason() pattern-matches all five real shapes into full sentences for Simple mode client-side, the same Simple/Advanced split as every other panel in this app. Falls back to the raw string for anything unrecognized rather than hiding or guessing at it, verified directly alongside the five known shapes.

The real-money indicator: today's PAPER tag lives inside the header's equity strip, easy to miss amid everything else there, and the account-connection bar only speaks to connection status, not real-order risk. New full-width, sticky #real-money-banner above the util-bar - the very top of the page, impossible to scroll past unnoticed. Exactly two states, keyed off account.trading_enabled specifically (the field that actually gates whether create_order can ever fire, confirmed by reading kalshi_account_client.py's _require_trading_enabled - not "is an account connected," which is still fully safe on its own under this app's architecture): calm green by default, a pulsing red warning only once real orders are genuinely possible. Loud specifically when it needs to be, not desensitizing by crying wolf the rest of the time. Verified live in both states via a synthetic trading_enabled flip.

phase 37 · manual series exclusion, smooth feed updates, persistent price deltas, Markets-tab search

A batch of direct corrections and one deliberately-planned feature

Manual whale/source exclusion (P2), planned before writing any code per direct instruction to lay out intermediary steps first: Kalshi's trade tape is anonymous, so a literal per-whale denylist isn't implementable - resolved to series-level exclusion, the same grouping the automatic win-rate filter already uses. signal_log._series_of promoted to public series_of() so both filters share one series definition rather than risking two that drift apart. New strategy.excluded_series, checked in both evaluators (mirroring the live_markets_only pattern), a Config denylist editor, a plain-English skip-reason case, and 6 new tests.

Then four direct corrections against the running app. Terminal's signal/decision feeds flickered on every poll and felt non-scrollable - actually scrollable, but a full innerHTML rebuild every 5s reset scrollTop to 0 each time. New renderFeedListSmooth() exploits that new items are only ever prepended server-side: the common case only inserts the new items' HTML, leaving existing DOM nodes (and scroll position) completely undisturbed, falling back to a full rebuild only when a filter/sort/grouping change (or a Simple/Advanced switch) actually changes what should be visible. Verified live via a planted DOM marker surviving an update untouched and scrollTop never resetting to 0. Scrollbars everywhere got a thin, semi-transparent restyle. Price- change badges (phase 30) were redesigned around a persistent per-ticker session baseline instead of last-poll comparison, after direct correction that they should stay visible and keep updating rather than flash once and vanish - dropped the pulse animation that made sense for a flash but not a steady badge. And the Markets tab gets its own search/browse panel (a real, previously-unmet request) - same GET /api/markets/search Config's watchlist manager already used, but purposed for looking (click into the real detail modal) rather than configuring, with a secondary Pin button as a bridge into watchlist management rather than the point of the panel.

phase 38 · real series metadata, and persistent flow clustering

Two P2 items closed out - "proceed with as much as you can without real whale watch data"

Series/category grouping had sat open waiting on "better Kalshi series metadata" - checked directly rather than assumed still missing, and it turned out get_series_list() already carries a real title and a real, clean 18-category taxonomy (via category, e.g. "Sports") plus finer tags ("Tennis", "Soccer") per series, already being fetched and cached hourly for the watchlist/search and then discarded. The grouping key itself (signal_log.series_of's ticker-prefix heuristic) checked out fine - every real prefix matched a real series ticker exactly - what was missing was just a name for it. New main.py:_series_meta_map() builds a ticker→{title, category, tags} lookup from the already-cached series list. Caught and fixed a real mistake before it shipped: the first version exposed the entire ~9,400-entry cache through /api/state, measured (not assumed safe) at over 1MB - a 20x+ regression against the whole earlier efficiency pass. Fixed by scoping to just the series actually on the current watchlist, landing at ~49KB. Now used in the whale win-rate block and its matching skip reason ("ITF Women's Match (Tennis)-type markets" instead of a raw "KXITFWMATCH"), falling back to the raw ticker whenever a series isn't in the scoped map yet.

Persistent flow clustering (P2 stretch item), the real/lasting version of the ephemeral client-side position-grouping toggle the signal feed already had: WhaleScanr's real methodology (researched directly) groups same-ticker/same-side prints close in time and size into probable-same-actor clusters, without ever claiming verified identity - Kalshi's real trade tape is anonymous, no account data exists. New signal_log.find_clusters() walks the persisted signal log ordered by ticker/side/time and greedily joins consecutive signals within a time window (30min) and a size-similarity ratio (4x), so a lone small print never gets lumped in with an unrelated large one just for sharing a ticker/side. Cluster confidence scales with print count and timing tightness, capped at 0.95. New GET /api/signals/clusters and a "Possible Accumulation" panel on Whale Watch. 7 new tests. Verified live against the real simulated signal log - a genuine 16-print, 408,645-contract, 95%-confidence cluster on a real market, clicking it opens the real detail modal.

main.py (_series_meta_map, GET /api/signals/clusters) · services/signal_log.py (find_clusters) · static/index.html (seriesLabelHTML, #signal-clusters panel) · tests/test_signal_log.py
phase 39 · mobile/responsive pass, a partial accessibility pass, and a modal-bubbling bug

P3 closed out, plus a direct bug report caught in the same pass

This app had zero @media breakpoints before this - the Terminal view's fixed 3-column grid in particular was unusable on a phone. New @media (max-width: 860px): .layout stacks into one naturally-scrolling column instead of three independently-scrolling panes pinned to calc(100vh - 57px) - itself a desktop-only assumption, since mobile browser chrome resizes the visible viewport as you scroll and fights a fixed vh height. Header, util-bar, and the equity strip wrap instead of overflowing; wide sortable tables get their own horizontal scroll instead of taking the whole page sideways with them. Verified live at real narrow viewports (375-500px) by measuring document.body.scrollWidth directly, not read off the CSS and assumed correct - which caught two genuine overflow bugs neither look at the CSS alone would have surfaced: a classic flexbox gotcha (a flex item's default min-width: auto refusing to let .account-bar .msg shrink/wrap below its own unbroken text width) and one white-space: nowrap status tag simply too wide for a narrow screen. Both fixed and re-verified at zero horizontal overflow across Portfolio, Terminal, Markets, and Config.

Accessibility pass, deliberately scoped and reported as partial rather than overclaimed: aria-labels added to icon-only controls (modal close buttons, the 🐨 "why this position was opened" disclosure). Colorblind fallback checked, not assumed adequate - every yes/no indicator in this app already pairs color with a "YES"/"NO" text label, confirmed by reading every call site, not just the CSS variables. Full keyboard navigation for the many clickable <div onclick=...> cards/rows across this app (no tabindex or keydown handling today) is a real, larger gap - left open rather than claimed done off a handful of aria-label additions.

Direct bug report caught in the same pass: the 🐨 disclosure sits inside a <tr onclick="openMarketDetail(...)"> (see renderPositions) - expanding it also opened the market-detail modal underneath, since the click bubbled up to the row. Fixed with event.stopPropagation() on the <details> element. Verified live: the icon expands without opening the modal; the row itself still opens it as before.
