phase 40 · active position management, an automated exit algorithm, and a Trading History tab

Positions were never touched after entry - direct report: "once positions are opened they are never monitored/changed to better reflect whale trends and minimize potential losses/maximize gains... this needs to be adjusted," plus "kalshi lets you set sell prices so if say you open a position at 50c you can set to cash out at 75c instead of 99c and profit."

New PaperBroker.close_position(ticker, exit_price, reason) - sells back at a given price instead of only holding to $1/$0 settlement, reusing the existing trades table exactly (a close is just a trade whose reason starts with "closed: ", no schema change). New FollowTheWhaleStrategy.check_exits(), run every trading-loop tick regardless of new signals, checked per open position in priority order: (1) market settlement - unconditional, not opt-in, closes at the terminal $1/$0 price the instant market.result is set, which is also the correctness fix for "left open forever" above; (2) take_profit_pct; (3) stop_loss_pct; (4) exit_on_sentiment_reversal (same _whale_lean math the dashboard's own whale-lean already uses); (5) auto_exit_enabled - a further direct request for "an automated, tweakable algorithm based on sensible factors from kalshi and whale watch data." _exit_confidence() blends three independently-weighted 0-1 factors (unrealized P&L magnitude, whale-sentiment reversal strength, and staleness - time since the last whale print on the ticker) into one composite score against a configurable threshold, the same "confidence" mental model the entry side already uses; a factor with no data is omitted from the average, never scored as zero. All opt-in checks default off/null - existing behavior is unchanged unless configured. 30 new tests. Config tab gets a new "Position Management (Exits)" section for all twelve new strategy.* fields.

New Trading History tab, direct request: "a trading history tab that shows graphs, positions made, win loss rates, times auto management changed positions or sold at a price less than what a full win would provide..., with relevant whale data and relevant config metadata." New services/trade_analytics.py (pure functions, no new persistence) classifies each closed trade's close_type by parsing the reason-string conventions already written, pairs each close back to its entry trade, and computes side-aware cost_basis/cash_back (actual dollars in/out) and left_on_table - the "sold at 75c instead of $1" figure, computed only for a genuine early profit-take and explicitly framed as a hypothetical, never a claim about what would actually have happened. New GET /api/trading-history returns per-trade rows, aggregate summary stats, a cumulative realized-P&L curve, and sample-size-hedged insights - heuristic config-tuning hints (e.g. win rate by entry-confidence bucket, average left-on-table on take-profit closes), each tagged with a trade count and a low/moderate/higher confidence label. Resolved directly with the user rather than assumed: explicitly not a recommendation engine and never writes config - full config-versioned performance tracking and any auto-generated tuning recommendation stay out of scope until there's real trade volume to make either trustworthy. 18 new tests. New tab renders summary cards, a cumulative P&L chart (renderEquityChart generalized to take a target element id), a by-close-type breakdown, an insights panel, and a paginated trade table with per-row dollar cost/payout - verified live via Selenium with synthetic data covering every close type and zero console errors.

Direct bug report caught mid-build: "im still seeing strings like KXITFWMATCH-26AUG08MILYOS-MIL in the possible accumulation section, signal history..., and positions section." Root cause: state["market_titles"]/state["event_titles"] were in-memory only, wiped on every uvicorn --reload restart (routine during development), and only ever refreshed for whatever's in the current top-volume watchlist rotation - a ticker whose title was learned before the last restart, or that had since rotated off the watchlist, permanently lost its title. New services/title_cache.py persists both caches to data/title_cache.db, write-through on every update, so a title learned once survives restarts and watchlist rotation for the rest of the app's life; _build_state_body still scopes what /api/state actually sends to what's currently relevant (same reasoning as the series-metadata payload-size fix in an earlier phase), while /api/signals/history and /api/signals/clusters now separately enrich their own responses from the full, unbounded cache. Also fixed the closely-related gap that made this matter for exit management specifically: _fetch_markets now always includes every currently-open position's ticker even if it's rotated out of the top-volume selection, so latest_prices - and therefore every take-profit/stop-loss/auto-exit check above - never goes stale for a position that's still genuinely held.

Found and fixed, via direct live-data investigation, not hypothetical: the settlement code above initially computed terminal_price from whether the position won rather than from the actual settled YES price, which for a no position double-applies close_position's own side inversion and silently pays $0 on a real win. A failing test caught it, but it had already run live for a few ticks and mis-paid 2 real trades. While tracing that, found a much larger, genuinely pre-existing bug (not introduced this session): PaperBroker.open_position() charged size * price unconditionally, but price is always the yes price by convention - a no position's real cost is size * (1 - price). This under-charged every no-side entry the app has ever opened and manufactured phantom profit on any no position that never even moved. Fixed in open_position (plus its bankroll-capping math, which had the identical bug) and the same bug in evaluate()'s/ShadowTrader.evaluate()'s position sizing, where it also silently blew past the intended max_position_pct risk cap for a no-side trade. New PaperBroker.cost_basis(ticker) is now the one place this math lives. Asked directly rather than assumed: user chose to fix the code and reset the paper account (POST /api/reset) over trying to retroactively correct historical no-side trades.

Found and fixed, via a deep-scan review requested directly after the above ("make sure you're not missing any gaps or errors" across market data, whale data, positions, management): evaluate() had no check for a ticker that already had an open position - only cooldown was checked, so a signal could silently overwrite an existing position via self.positions[ticker], discarding its cost basis with zero accounting trail (confirmed this had actually happened in live trade history, not hypothetical) - now skipped as "position already open on this market." Neither evaluate() nor ShadowTrader.evaluate() checked whether a market had already resolved before opening a position on it - now skipped via the same settlement data check_exits already uses. Four more frontend instances of the same no-side dollar-math bug class turned up (Positions panel Cost/ Value/Return, the Explain-Like-I'm-5 panel's capital-at-risk, the whale signal card's dollar-size, the paper Trade Log's cost figure), plus a distinct sign-flip variant in the Advanced Trade Log's per-row P&L, which had no direction term at all. And the whale-print "why this position was opened" disclosure was silently snapping shut on every dashboard refresh - same class of bug as phase 39's modal-bubbling one, direct report - renderPositions now preserves which tickers were expanded across its rebuild. All fixed, all covered by new/updated tests. Full suite: 171 passing (was 127 immediately before this batch).

services/paper_broker.py (close_position, cost_basis, side-aware open_position), services/strategy_engine.py (check_exits, _exit_confidence, already-open/already-resolved guards), services/shadow_mode.py (matching fixes), services/trade_analytics.py (new), services/title_cache.py (new, see phase 40's title-persistence fix below), main.py (check_exits wiring, market_results, /api/trading-history), config/settings.yaml (12 new strategy.* fields), static/index.html (History tab, Config tab additions, 4 dollar-math fixes, renderPositions disclosure-state fix)
phase 41 · a rule-based advisory engine, and the config-fingerprinting it needed first

"I want a recommendation engine/advisory system... keep it disabled until that data threshold has been reached"

Phase 40's Trading History tab shipped compute_insights() as deliberately descriptive-only - hedged prose, never a suggested value, never scoped per config. This phase built the real thing on top of it, in two steps requested and confirmed directly: rule-based, not ML (explainable, no training pipeline needed for a POC's data volume), and it must not surface a real recommendation until there's enough data to trust it - not as a UI nicety, but as a property of the function itself.

Step one: new services/config_performance.py - fingerprint() hashes strategy.* minus name (so a newly added tunable field joins the fingerprint with zero code change here), config_variants/applied_changes tables in data/config_performance.db. services/paper_broker.py's Position/ Trade gained config_fingerprint via an idempotent ALTER TABLE migration (the trades/positions tables already had live rows). A closed position always inherits the fingerprint active at entry, even if config changed mid-hold - a documented limitation, not a bug, since the entry gates are what a variant comparison is actually about.

Step two: new services/advisory_engine.py - variant_summaries() groups resolved trades by fingerprint (reusing trade_analytics.compute_summary(), not reimplementing it); generate_recommendations() upgrades compute_insights()'s hedged prose into a concrete suggested value (e.g. the actual confidence-bucket boundary for entry_threshold, an adjusted take_profit_pct/stop_loss_pct from observed left-on-table/overshoot), plus cross-variant comparison once two variants each individually clear the threshold. The gate lives inside this function, not the route or the UI - there is no code path that can hand back a real recommendation for an under-sampled variant. New advisory.* config (enabled default false), GET /api/advisory/status, GET /api/advisory/recommendations, POST /api/advisory/recommendations/apply (always a human click, re-validates fresh against a freshly recomputed list rather than trusting the request body, writes an audit row), GET /api/advisory/applied-changes, and a POST /api/config rejection guard for advisory.auto_apply_enabled - same shape as the existing kalshi_account.trading_enabled guard, since opt-in auto-apply's actual confirmation-phrase enable endpoint was deliberately left unbuilt, a separate and more carefully reviewed follow-up once the manual-apply path has run for a while. New "Advisory Recommendations" panel on the Trading History tab, directly below "Config Tuning Hints" (left unchanged - the permanent descriptive layer, not replaced).

Direct follow-up in the same session, added deliberately as scaffolding rather than built out: "eventually I want to be able to feed this heuristic suggestion data, market data, historical data, portfolio data, recommendation engine data, to a machine-learning agent that will work WITH these things, not as a replacement... let's not pursue that until the project is already finished." New services/ml_feed.py - one pure, dependency-free function, build_context_snapshot(), assembling exactly that bundle out of data every existing service already produces. Not wired into any route or called from anywhere in the app; only tests/test_ml_feed.py exercises it, specifically so it can't silently drift out of sync with the shapes it wraps while nothing else calls it.

services/config_performance.py (new) · services/advisory_engine.py (new) · services/ml_feed.py (new, scaffolding) · services/paper_broker.py (config_fingerprint) · services/strategy_engine.py (fingerprint threading) · services/trade_analytics.py (config_fingerprint on rows, confidence_label made public) · main.py (/api/advisory/*, config guard) · config/settings.yaml (advisory.*) · static/index.html (Advisory Recommendations panel) · docs/advisory-engine-plan.md (new, full design) · tests/test_config_performance.py, tests/test_advisory_engine.py, tests/test_ml_feed.py (all new)
phase 42 · the Config tab becomes an actual control panel

"The config tab is hard to make sense of for a like-I'm-5 user"

Direct report: every setting in the app lived on one long flat scroll, all expanded at once regardless of how often it's actually touched - the opposite of the app's own dummy-proof guiding principle applied to its own settings screen. Rebuilt as ten collapsible <details class="config-section"> accordions (native, not a hand-rolled JS toggle - free keyboard support) - Mode and Strategy open by default, the rest collapsed, each with a one-line plain-English subtitle instead of a bare heading.

Second half of the same report: "highlight config options that aren't fully baked yet due to data limitations (like not real/complete whale watch data or lack of trade history)." New three-badge system, explained once in a legend box at the top of the tab rather than repeated inline: 🧪 sim data (tuned against the built-in whale simulator, not a real feed - the whale win-rate filter fields, the sentiment-reversal exit block, the auto-exit sentiment/staleness weights, the whole Whale Signal section); 📉 needs history (the Advisory Engine section from phase 41 - needs more of a user's own resolved trades before its output is trustworthy); 🆕 new (the Market-Native Strategy section from phase 43 below - a brand-new feature with zero track record yet, a different concern from data quality entirely). Verified live via Selenium: all ten sections present and expand/collapse correctly, all three badge types render, Save Config still round-trips every field through /api/config unchanged.

static/index.html (Config tab restructure, accordion + badge CSS, #config-badge-legend)
phase 43 · real market data, logged now, and a second strategy that never touches whale data

"Market data is real, the whale data is fake... there's no reason why I shouldn't start storing and analyzing market data now"

Direct request, with a stated longer-term plan: start building a real, whale-independent dataset from this app's real Kalshi market data today, and once a real whale-watcher feed is connected, incorporate those genuinely-real whale-driven trades alongside it rather than waiting. Asked directly which shape "storing and analyzing" should take rather than assumed: passive logging only, or an actual second live automated strategy - the answer was the latter, kept backend-only for now (no dashboard panel yet).

New services/market_history.py: a real per-market snapshot log (price/spread/volume/time-to-close, data/market_history.db) populated every trading-loop tick from the market data already fetched for the whale-follow strategy - zero extra API cost, and completely independent of whale signals or of whether either strategy ever trades a given market. momentum() computes real price movement over a trailing window, with a minimum-window- coverage guard so a ticker with only seconds of logged history can't report a confident-looking "30-minute momentum" reading computed from noise. compute_hypothetical_trades() - explicitly labeled retrospective/hypothetical, never a claim about a real position - characterizes what a simple "buy the side the price already favored" entry would have returned, per lookback window, against real settlement outcomes.

New services/market_strategy.py: MarketNativeStrategy, a second, fully independent automated paper strategy - momentum continuation gated by a price band, a max spread, a minimum volume floor, and a minimum time-to-close, composite entry confidence blending momentum/liquidity/spread (same weighted-factor mental model as whale_simulator._score_confidence and phase 40's _exit_confidence). Zero whale-signal input anywhere in it. Off by default (market_strategy.enabled: false), same ship-disabled precedent as phase 41's advisory.enabled.

Running a second strategy safely needed a real architecture fix first: PaperBroker and RiskManager both only supported one hardcoded module-level DB_PATH each - a second live instance would have silently shared (and corrupted) the whale-follow broker's own broker_meta/positions/trades rows. Both gained an optional per-instance db_path, resolved at call time so existing tests' monkeypatch.setattr(..., "DB_PATH", ...) pattern keeps working unchanged; main.py derives the new market_broker/market_risk pair's paths from the whale-follow pair's own already-redirectable db_path attribute (not a fresh path lookup of their own) specifically so tests/test_trading_gate.py's real-file-safety guarantee still holds for the second pair too. Also extracted strategy_engine.py's settlement-closing math - the terminal_price = 1.0 if result == "yes" else 0.0 fix from phase 40, a real bug that shipped and was fixed once already - into a shared close_if_settled(), so a second strategy couldn't silently reintroduce that exact bug class by duplicating the old inline version. trade_analytics.py gained a momentum_reversal close-type pattern, the market-native analog of sentiment_reversal. main.py's market fetch now includes both brokers' open positions in extra_tickers, the same "a held position shouldn't go stale after rotating off the top-volume watchlist" fix phase 40 already made for the whale broker, applied proactively here before it could recur.

Caught and fixed a real bug while writing this phase's own tests, not hypothetical: market_history.py's _connect() originally defaulted its db_path parameter at function-definition time (db_path: Path = DB_PATH) - Python binds a default argument value once, when the function is defined, not per call, so monkeypatching DB_PATH afterward in a test had no effect at all and every write silently went to whatever path was live at import time. Fixed by requiring an explicit argument and having every internal caller pass the module-level name (resolved dynamically, at call time, the same way PaperBroker/ RiskManager's own default already worked correctly). New tests/test_market_history.py, tests/test_market_strategy.py (13 + 24 tests), db_path-isolation tests added to tests/test_paper_broker.py/ tests/test_risk_manager.py, route smoke tests added to tests/test_trading_gate.py. Full suite: 262 passing. Verified live: curl against the real running app confirmed market_history was already logging real snapshots (21 tickers, 120 rows) within seconds of the reload.

phase 44 · a real whale-watcher provider, and the simulator steps aside

"Is there a website we can scrape kalshi leaderboard data for whale watching, or an api I can access that gives it?"

Researched before building anything, not assumed: Kalshi has no public trader identity or leaderboard - trades are anonymous member-to-member, confirmed across multiple independent sources. Unlike Polymarket's on-chain wallet data (the actual data source behind real third-party "whale trackers" studied this session, see docs/simmer-integration-research.md), there is no reputation-based whale signal available for Kalshi through any legitimate channel. But size-based detection turned out to already be sitting in this app, unused for anything but display: services/kalshi_client.py's public, no-auth get_trades() - the real exchange-wide trade tape, already fetched every trading-loop tick for the UI's trade tape panel.

New services/whalewatchers/kalshi_trade_tape.py: KalshiTradeTapeProvider classifies a real trade as a whale print once its notional dollar size clears a configurable threshold (whale_watcher_kalshi.min_notional_usd, default $2500) - side-aware (count_fp * yes_price_dollars or no_price_dollars), deliberately applying the same lesson phase 40 already paid for once (a no-side position's real cost uses the *no* price, not the yes price). Needs zero credentials and costs zero extra API calls: a new optional market_context param on WhaleWatcherProvider.fetch_signals() (added to every provider for interface consistency, only this one reads it) lets it reuse the tick's already-fetched market/trade data instead of an independent fetch. Deduplicates by real trade_id (a bounded seen-set) so a trade sitting in get_trades()'s rolling last-10 window doesn't re-fire as a "new" signal every tick until it ages out. Confidence scoring reuses WhaleSimulator's existing four-factor "Insider Score" formula exactly - newly extracted into a shared, noise-free composite_confidence() in services/whale_simulator.py, since a real trade's confidence shouldn't have the simulator's synthetic randomness mixed into it.

Verified against real, live data before trusting it, not just fixtures: ran the classifier directly against the real trade tape mid-session (top real trade at the time, $850 notional, correctly produced zero signals against the $2500 default; temporarily lowering the threshold to $1 produced 46 real, well-formed signals). Then verified the full path, not just the provider in isolation - set WHALE_WATCHER_PROVIDER=kalshi_trade_tape, did a real ddev restart (a genuine process restart, not just --reload), confirmed state["whale_source"] correctly reported "kalshi_trade_tape" with no errors, then temporarily lowered the live threshold via the real /api/config endpoint and watched one real trade flow all the way through signal_log → strategy.evaluate → the decision feed (correctly skipped for a legitimate "market is not currently live" reason) before reverting both back to defaults.

Direct follow-up once the real provider was proven working: "there's no reason to have the simulator enabled by default." services/whalewatchers/__init__.py's default flipped from generic_rest (which silently ran the simulator whenever no URL was configured - true for every fresh checkout) to kalshi_trade_tape - a real signal now needs no setup at all, and simulator-only behavior becomes the opt-*out* path (WHALE_WATCHER_PROVIDER=generic_rest with no URL) instead of the default. The Config tab's "Whale Signal" section split into a simulator half and a real half, the inactive one visually grayed out based on which is actually running that tick (updateWhaleProviderStatus()). Caught and fixed a real bug while wiring this: the status note was computed once in loadConfig() at page load, before the first /api/state poll could possibly have completed, so it was permanently stuck reading "checking..." - fixed by calling the same status function from every poll tick inside refresh() too, not just once at load.

13 new tests (tests/test_whalewatchers_kalshi_trade_tape.py) plus 2 locking in the composite_confidence() extraction's behavior. Full suite: 275 passing (was 262). Closes the long-open P2 roadmap item for a second real whale-watcher provider.

phase 45 · wider coverage, and LIVE-only filtering pushed upstream

"You're not looking at trades from nearly enough markets even with the round robin... you should be monitoring closer to 50."

Direct report following straight from phase 44's real whale provider - more markets watched means more real trade-tape coverage for it to actually classify. kalshi.watchlist_size 20 → 50, with the dependent caps that scale with it bumped in tandem, same discipline as the earlier 8 → 20 change: _get_top_series's candidate-series pool 30 → 40 (series diversity feeding the round-robin, not just more slots from the same handful), and _fetch_trade_tape's total cap 50 → 100 (promoted to a named _TRADE_TAPE_TOTAL_CAP) - this cap stopped being just cosmetic for the UI panel once phase 44 shipped, since it's now the entire input to the real whale classifier too. Verified live across several ticks: 50 distinct markets, 100-entry trade tape, no errors, no 429s.

Two direct corrections to phase 44's "gray out the inactive whale-signal section" work, caught by real usage: "by gray out that section... I also mean disable it from any input/selection" (opacity alone still left the simulator's fields editable while the real provider was running - new setSectionInputsDisabled(), called from the same per-tick updateWhaleProviderStatus(), actually disables them, leaving only the accordion's own expand/collapse clickable) and "update the badges on the config page to reflect current status" (the Strategy section's seven "sim data" badges were hardcoded regardless of which whale-signal source was actually active, stale ever since a real provider became the default - now a shared whale-dependent-badge class hidden live whenever a real provider is confirmed active).

Then a real, separate gap surfaced by direct testing: "even if I set the whale threshold low to say, 100, I'm not seeing any signals show up." Investigated live rather than assumed - signals were actually flowing fine (196 seen); the real blockers were two independent downstream gates doing exactly what they were built to do (strategy.live_markets_only skipping non-live markets, strategy.entry_threshold sitting well above what real trade-derived confidence scores were producing) plus whale_watcher_kalshi.min_notional_usd having drifted to 0 during testing, admitting literally every real trade as a "whale." Cleared via POST /api/reset's signal_log domain once confirmed directly, paper/shadow untouched.

Which led to the actual new feature: "for market discovery and watchlist I want to have the option to only include LIVE markets" - a third, genuinely different gate from the two that already existed (strategy.live_markets_only only gates whether the strategy acts on a signal; whale_signal.live_markets_only is simulator-only); this one filters which markets get selected into the watchlist/candidate pool in the first place. Two real design forks resolved directly before writing any code: fallback behavior when too few markets are live (shrink the watchlist, even to zero, never backfill with non-live markets to hit watchlist_size) and the cost/latency tradeoff of checking live status across the *wider candidate pool* before round-robin selection rather than only the final watchlist afterward (accepted - checking just the already-narrowed watchlist would have meant "only live" really meant "only live among whichever markets already won on volume," which could easily be zero of them). services/kalshi_client.py's get_top_volume_markets() split into two composable, independently-testable pieces - get_candidate_markets() (fetch, filter, sort, no cutoff) and a new static round_robin_select() (the existing selection logic, now a pure function of its input) - with get_top_volume_markets() itself becoming both composed together, fully behavior-preserving. New kalshi.live_markets_only config (default false); when on, main.py's _fetch_markets() fetches the full candidate pool, checks live status across all of it, filters to live-only, then round-robins the final watchlist from that subset. Extended to GET /api/markets/search too, direct request ("same for market search") - a new live_only query param, reused by both search UIs (the Markets tab browse panel and the Config tab's watchlist-management search).

Verified live across several ticks: watchlist correctly shrank to 6, then 12 markets with the flag on (100% actually live each time, per state.live_status), back to 50 with it off; search's live_only=true returned 12 real, currently-live results; all three new checkboxes render correctly via Selenium, zero console errors. 6 new tests (tests/test_kalshi_client.py). Full suite: 281 passing (was 275).

phase 46 · hardened confidence scoring, and a gated calibration tool

"The confidence level algorithm is decent i guess, a bit basic... id also like an agent to study whale prints and their actual outcomes so as to create a more accurate formula."

Two tracks, both confirmed directly (AskUserQuestion) before writing anything - "more robust" and "an agent to learn from outcomes" are genuinely different problems with different prerequisites, so they got separated rather than bundled into one vague pass.

Track 1 hardened services/whale_simulator.py's composite_confidence in the three specific ways named as real weaknesses up front, not a general rewrite. The depth factor's hard cap (min(size/volume, 1.0)) meant a print of exactly one day's volume and one 100x bigger scored identically - replaced with exponential saturation (1 - e^(-k·ratio)) that still lands at ~0.9 for one day's volume (preserving roughly where the old cap used to bind) but never plateaus beyond it. The context factor's raw ratio against the single busiest market in the batch was fragile to one outlier crushing every other market's score toward zero - replaced with percentile rank among the batch, robust to that by construction. A new fifth factor, agreement (15% weight): do recent real whale prints on this *same market* agree with this one - independent same-direction prints plausibly share a real catalyst, the same thesis behind docs/kalshi-whale-provider-and-strategy-porting-plan.md Part 2's whale-consensus idea, folded into the base score itself. Computed by the caller via a new signal_log.recent_sides_for_ticker() query (6h lookback) since the scoring function itself has no access to signal history; neutral 0.5 (not agreement or disagreement) when there's no recent print to compare against, or for the simulator, which has no real history to check. Weights rebalanced (depth 40→35%, unusualness 25→20%, proximity 20→15%, context unchanged 15%, agreement new at 15%). New composite_confidence_breakdown() returns every factor plus the final score; composite_confidence() itself kept its existing plain-float contract as a thin wrapper, so the simulator and every existing caller/test needed zero changes.

Track 2 re-confirmed the exact rule-based-over-ML reasoning docs/advisory-engine-plan.md made at the very start of this whole advisory-engine effort - but checked live with real numbers this time before deciding, not just by analogy: 5,738 real signals seen, only 9 resolved. Nowhere near enough to fit anything. New services/confidence_calibration.py needed a real data-model addition first - signal_log's signals table gained a nullable factors_json column (idempotent ALTER TABLE, the same migration pattern paper_broker.py's config_fingerprint already used) so individual factor values persist per signal, not just the blended number that was already there - there'd be nothing to calibrate *from* otherwise. Only real providers populate it (kalshi_trade_tape.py, via the new breakdown function); simulator rows leave it null, which doubles as the "real signals only" filter for calibration with no second, driftable source-string match to maintain. generate_calibration_report() is gated *inside the function itself* (the same precedent advisory_engine.generate_recommendations already set) - splits resolved real signals into low/mid/high tertiles per factor and reports each bucket's win rate and the gap between them. A real bug, caught by this module's own tests, not hypothetical: a near-constant factor (proximity_factor is often exactly 0.0) with plain index-based tertiles would silently reflect insertion order instead of the factor itself, since a stable sort of tied values doesn't actually separate them - fixed by requiring at least 3 *distinct* values before a factor gets split at all, reporting "not enough variance to say" honestly instead of a fabricated gap. Proposes concrete suggested_weights (proportional to each factor's own discrimination gap, floored so nothing goes to zero off one report) rather than only descriptive stats - matching advisory_engine's "emit an actual value, not just hedged prose" ethos while staying fully deterministic, never a fitted model. New confidence_calibration config (enabled: false, min_resolved_signals: 50) and two read-only routes - deliberately no apply endpoint at all in v1, unlike advisory_engine's manual-apply button: the weights aren't config-editable today, just hardcoded constants, so there's no live value yet to PATCH.

Verified live: fresh real signals confirmed carrying full factor breakdowns with sane values (no more hard-capped 1.0s, percentile-style context values), the migration applied cleanly to the live data/signal_log.db with zero data loss (older rows correctly factors_json=null, new ones populated), both new routes confirmed correctly gated/dormant (resolved_count starts at 0 for factor-carrying signals, since the 9 previously-resolved signals predate this change). 24 new tests. Full suite: 297 passing (was 285).

services/whale_simulator.py (composite_confidence_breakdown, rebalanced weights) · services/confidence_calibration.py (new) · services/signal_log.py (factors_json migration, recent_sides_for_ticker, resolved_signals_with_factors) · services/whalewatchers/kalshi_trade_tape.py (agreement-factor lookup, breakdown persistence) · main.py (/api/confidence-calibration/status, .../report) · config/settings.yaml (confidence_calibration.*) · tests/test_confidence_calibration.py (new), tests/test_signal_log.py, tests/test_whalewatchers_kalshi_trade_tape.py, tests/test_whale_simulator.py
phase 47 · light polling, and a market catalog that finally finds what's live

"They should have scheduled open and close times for you to do some light polling... otherwise use the schedule and its previous live status" - then, after real data proved that wasn't the whole problem: "you really must have done something wrong... i'm seeing 72-86 live markets on Kalshi."

Started as a polling-efficiency request. _fetch_live_status was re-deriving live status from 2 fresh API calls per candidate event on every 15s tick with zero memory of prior results - investigated directly and also found a real, pre-existing bug along the way: the window-bounds comment claimed "started up to 6h ago, or starting within the next hour," but the actual inequality did the opposite (hand-derived, not assumed - included events starting hours in the future, dropped anything that had been running more than about an hour). New state["live_status_cache"] (survives across ticks, unlike state["live_status"] itself, still wholesale-replaced per tick for the current view) - a market is only re-polled after _LIVE_STATUS_REPOLL_SEC (5 min, ~20x fewer calls than every tick), a market past its own close_time is treated as finished from the schedule alone with no poll, and a terminal status is never polled again for the process's life. Window bounds corrected alongside it. A schedule-based fallback was added per the original request, for when a real poll happens but Kalshi's milestone/live-data system has nothing to say - then tightened after a direct correction ("just because a market is open doesn't mean it's live like sports or mentions or award shows - be careful how you infer"): the fallback only infers "live" for events Kalshi has confirmed are milestone-tracked (a real game/mention/show) but whose live-data confirmation didn't land this particular tick; a market with no milestone registered at all is left out of the result entirely, never guessed at either way.

Then real-world pushback that turned out to be the actual bug: real Kalshi showed 72-86 live markets, almost all sports, while this app's live-only watchlist found effectively zero. Investigated directly rather than defending the polling logic already shipped - ran discovery's actual candidate selection against real Kalshi data and found the true root cause sat entirely upstream of live-status checking: kalshi.live_markets_only discovery only ever sampled the top 40 series by 24h trading volume, and of ~775 real candidates in that sample, only ~30 fell in any plausible live time window, 0 confirmed live. A series being high-volume overall and "has a game live right now" are genuinely different things - volume-ranking the candidate pool was quietly the whole problem.

Fix, shaped directly by the user: "fetch a complete list of markets... paginate api fetches and populate/store data in a usable manner until all markets are scanned," then scoped back down once the size of that got named out loud: "maybe it doesn't have to be a FULL catalog... within days or weeks, not 6 months or a year from now." New services/market_catalog.py, an incrementally-scanned, near-term-only catalog (data/market_catalog.db). get_series_list() already returns Kalshi's full ~9,400-series catalog in one call, no pagination problem there - the actual gap was market-level fetching only ever sampling the top 40 of those. New per-tick _scan_catalog_batch() fetches a bounded batch (40 series/tick) of the least-recently- scanned series - a self-healing rotation with no fragile cursor to keep in sync with a series list that itself changes size - storing only markets within a near-term horizon (1 week past through 3 weeks future, occurrence_datetime/close_time parsed to unix timestamps up front for cheap SQL range queries instead of per-row Python parsing on every read) per the explicit scope correction. Gated entirely behind kalshi.live_markets_only - zero extra API cost for anyone who hasn't opted in. Both _fetch_markets' live-only discovery and GET /api/markets/search's live_only path rewired to draw candidates from this catalog instead of a fresh top-N-series fetch - a second real bug caught and fixed while rewiring search: it still truncated to the top-30-by-volume series before ever checking the catalog, discarding most of the catalog's own breadth advantage whenever search had no text query narrowing anything down; fixed to only apply that narrowing when a real q/category filter is actually active. New GET /api/market-catalog/status debug endpoint.

Verified live against the real running app, not just in tests: before this shipped, live-only discovery found 0 confirmed-live markets against real data - the user's exact complaint, reproduced directly. After: watched the catalog build in real time (0 → 680+ series scanned, 7,500+ near-term markets, across a few minutes of real ticks) and the live watchlist grow alongside it (0 → 24 confirmed-live real markets - real PGA head-to-head golf matchups, a live CS2 esports match, and a milestone-tracked MrBeast mention market, confirming the milestone-gated fallback correctly covers mention-type markets too, not just sports). Search's live_only confirmed working both with and without a query after the truncation fix. Zero errors in logs throughout. 26 new tests. Full suite: 323 passing (was 297).

main.py (_fetch_live_status caching/fallback rewrite, _scan_catalog_batch, /api/market-catalog/status) · services/market_catalog.py (new) · tests/test_market_catalog.py (new), tests/test_trading_gate.py
phase 48 · what "parent market" actually means, and a detour through Kalshi's own docs to find out

"I don't mean each individual position as a 'market' - I mean the overall parent market - ex: Wyndham Championship... only parent markets should count against the watchlist."

Confirmed live before building anything: 42 of 47 real watchlist slots (89%) were individual KXPGAH2H golf head-to-head pairings. round_robin_select() grouped candidates by event_ticker, and each pairing has its own distinct event_ticker - round-robin's "one per group before a second from any" fairness never noticed all 23 pairings were one tournament.

Two designs were tried and rejected before this shipped, each for a real reason. First, grouping by ticker prefix (series) directly - fixed the golf case cleanly, but live-verifying it (rather than trusting the synthetic tests, which happened not to catch this) surfaced a real regression: two genuinely different, concurrently-live matches sharing one series - two separate Dota2 games, both under KXDOTA2MAP - got merged into one inflated fake "parent." Rather than guess at a third heuristic, went to Kalshi's own API documentation directly, per direct instruction ("please review the kalshi API documentation thoroughly and revisit"). Confirmed Kalshi's real hierarchy is only Category → Series → Event → Market - no tournament/competition/bracket level exists - and that Event is explicitly documented as "the basic unit that members should interact with." Checked whether get_event()'s product_metadata.competition field could substitute for a real "tournament" field - it's tournament-specific for golf ("Wyndham Championship") but sport-generic for esports ("Dota 2", identical for both unrelated matches, confirmed live) - not usable as a universal grouping key. Second design, shaped by that research: kept event as the selection unit (matching Kalshi's documented model exactly) but round-robinned across series to decide which events filled the watchlist - correct by the book, and it did stop golf from crowding everyone else out, but it still let those two different Dota2 matches each cost their own separate watchlist slot. Direct override once that tradeoff was named out loud: "i dont want those pairings to count against the watchlist count, only the parent series."

Shipped design: series (services/signal_log.series_of's ticker-prefix definition, already used everywhere else in this app) is the parent/watchlist unit - the top-most level of Kalshi's own hierarchy that still represents one cohesive market, one below the broader Category grouping that spans unrelated market types entirely. Once a series is selected, every event and market under it - an entire tournament's worth of pairings - is included for free and doesn't count separately against kalshi.watchlist_size, highest-volume child first (direct request: "I want the ability to cap but for now i want every child, and i want these children markets prioritized by volume"), optionally capped by new kalshi.max_children_per_parent (default null = unlimited). The known, explicitly-accepted tradeoff: two unrelated matches sharing one series are watched together under that series's single slot rather than counted as two - a deliberate choice this time, not an oversight. Threaded through both _fetch_markets branches and both GET /api/markets/search branches; new Config tab field ("Max markets watched per parent series").

Verified live: KXPGAH2H's 23 real pairings no longer individually dominate the watchlist - the whole tournament now costs one slot, and real series diversity holds alongside it. 13 tests in tests/test_kalshi_client.py, including a direct regression mirroring the real Wyndham scenario and one confirming same-series concurrent matches are watched together by design. Full suite: 326 passing.

services/kalshi_client.py (round_robin_select, get_top_volume_markets) · main.py (_fetch_markets, search_markets) · config/settings.yaml (kalshi.max_children_per_parent) · static/index.html (Config tab field) · tests/test_kalshi_client.py
phase 49 · the dashboard catches up to what "parent" now means

"The UI presentation needs to be updated to reflect these groupings across all the tabs and sections as necessary. For example in the terminal under market these markets are grouped the same way but should be [grouped by series]."

Phase 48 changed what the watchlist selects and counts, but shipped with no matching change to how the dashboard displays it. Terminal's compact Markets list (renderMarkets) was a flat row per individual ticker with no grouping at all - a whole tournament's 42 markets still showed as 42 disconnected rows. Markets/Whale Watch's card view (renderMarketCards) already grouped by event_ticker (one pairing) but not a level higher, so those same 42 markets rendered as ~23 separate, unrelated-looking cards with nothing tying them together as one series.

New shared seriesOf()/seriesLabel() JS helpers - seriesOf() mirrors services/signal_log.series_of's exact ticker-prefix definition, kept textually identical rather than a second one that could drift; seriesLabel() looks up the real title via series_meta (already sent by main.py from an earlier phase) instead of showing a raw ticker prefix. renderMarkets now inserts a .series-header divider (title, market count, total volume) above a run of same-series rows - relies on state.markets already arriving grouped consecutively by series (see round_robin_select), so this is just a "series changed since the last row" check, no client-side re-sorting. renderMarketCards nests a series' event-cards inside a new .series-section wrapper the same way, but only when that series actually has more than one event - a series with a single live event (the common case) renders exactly as it did before, no added clutter, matching the precedent eventGroupCardHTML already set for the event-vs-solo-card split. The Advanced screener table (shared by all three panels) isn't nested the same way - grouping would fight its whole reason for existing, flexible column sorting - so it gained a new sortable "Series" column instead, alongside the existing "Category" one.

No browser was available in this sandbox to drive Selenium directly, so verified differently: extracted the real render functions verbatim out of static/index.html and executed them in Node against a live /api/state snapshot with minimal DOM stubs (a fake document.getElementById capturing innerHTML). Confirmed all 69 real markets rendered with the correct row/card count, the real Wyndham series collapsed to one "23 events · 42 markets" header instead of 42 disconnected rows, and only the three series that actually had multiple concurrent events at the time (WTA Set Winner, PGA Head-to-Head Matchups, BTTS) got a visible .series-section wrapper - every single-event series rendered as a plain card/row, unchanged, confirmed by diffing card/row counts against the raw market count.

phase 50 · every card was showing 50¢/50¢ - the real root cause was two phases back

"Showing 50c in green and red for all sets of yes/no values all across the app. its not updating either like it used to be." Plus a correction: "youre mistaken about the browser testing because you did in fact install selenium with ddev."

Two things in one report. First, an incorrect claim in phase 49's writeup: no browser was available to verify visually, so verification there leaned on a Node harness instead. That was wrong - a dedicated selenium-chrome ddev service (.ddev/docker-compose.selenium-chrome.yaml) was already running the whole time, reachable at selenium-chrome:4444 from inside fastapi, which already has the selenium Python package installed. The earlier check looked for a local Chrome binary on the host and in the container instead of this service - confirmed via ddev describe once pointed at it directly.

Second, the real bug. Traced state["latest_prices"] directly rather than guessing at the frontend: every single selected market was pinned at exactly 0.5. Root cause - market_catalog rows (the entire candidate source for kalshi.live_markets_only since phase 47) only ever store schedule/title/volume metadata for discovery purposes; no yes_bid_dollars column exists on them at all. Every catalog-sourced market therefore fell straight through to float(m.get("yes_bid_dollars") or 0.5)'s fallback - the exact bug a comment already on that line had warned about once before, for a different underlying cause. This has been true since market_catalog shipped in phase 47; it likely went unnoticed until phase 49's grouping made far more cards visible on screen at once, and until the series-level watchlist change earlier this same day meant an unbounded, much larger number of individual markets were being selected and priced this way.

Fixed in _fetch_markets's live_markets_only branch: after round_robin_select picks the final watchlist, re-fetch real, full market objects - batched by distinct selected series (one real get_markets(series_ticker=...) call per series, the same per-series cost the non-live-only branch already pays, just deferred until after selection instead of spent on the whole broad candidate pool) - with a per-ticker get_market() fallback for whatever the status="open" batch fetch didn't return (a market that settled between the catalog scan and now - confirmed live as a real, smaller residual case of the same bug). Falls back to the original catalog row only if even that direct fetch fails, same "degrade honestly, never silently drop" pattern as the rest of this app.

Verified live, not just in tests: before the fix, /api/state's latest_prices was exactly 0.5 for all 206 selected markets; after, real diverse values (0.06, 0.93, 0.34, 0.001, ...) that visibly changed between two consecutive polls on a real in-progress tennis match. Verified visually too, this time actually via the selenium-chrome service found above - real screenshots of the Terminal and Markets tabs confirmed varied real prices throughout (no more uniform 50¢) alongside correct series-header/section grouping rendering, together, in the same real page. 2 new tests in tests/test_trading_gate.py covering both the batch-hydration path and the per-ticker fallback. Full suite: 328 passing.

main.py (_fetch_markets's live_markets_only hydration pass) · tests/test_trading_gate.py
phase 51 · "technically" different markets that are just inversions of each other

"Do a thorough evaluation of how you group and show child markets... 'technically' they may be different markets but they are just inversions of each other."

Investigated before building anything, per this app's own established habit. Pulled real 2-sibling events directly and confirmed numerically: a genuine game/match/map/set-winner event's two sibling markets' yes_bid_dollars sum to ~1.0 - "Toronto vs Philadelphia Winner" was 0.92 + 0.07. The second row is entirely derivable from the first; showing both is pure repetition, not two pieces of information. But not every 2-sibling event is like this - "Max Scherzer 15+ outs" and "Aaron Nola 18+ outs" share an event yet are completely independent props (0.76 + 0.0, nowhere near 1.0), and a genuine multi-outcome market ("Wyndham Championship Winner", 60+ golfers) also has many siblings with no simple pairwise complement at all - collapsing either of those would have silently thrown away real information.

The authoritative signal turned out to be one field away, already being fetched: get_event()'s mutually_exclusive - real Kalshi data already pulled into every main.py::_fetch_event_titles call, previously discarded outright. Added it to title_cache.py's event_titles table (idempotent ALTER TABLE, same pattern as every other live-DB migration here) and to the fetched dict. Asked directly, rather than guessed, which exact display treatment to ship - offered two options with previews (drop the redundant row vs. build a new combined "vs" row) - and the minimal one won: show only the higher-probability side's existing row, since its own Yes/No pair already implies the other side's odds by complement.

New shared dedupeInversionPairs() for Terminal's flat list, and matching logic inside eventGroupCardHTML for the Markets/Whale card view - both only ever drop the redundant *display* row; the honest totals (volume, "N markets," the top watchlist badge) still reflect every real underlying Kalshi ticker, unchanged. Caught and fixed a real staleness gap before it shipped inert: _fetch_event_titles only ever fetches an event that's "not yet cached," so every event already cached before this field existed would have stayed null forever - confirmed live, all 36 pre-existing cache entries showed mutually_exclusive: null right after deploying. Fixed by also re-fetching whenever a cached entry's value is still None, since a real Kalshi event always returns a real True/False for this field - self-heals over the next few ticks with no backfill script or DB wipe, confirmed live: the cache went from 100% null to a mix of real True/False values within about half a minute of real ticks.

8 new tests (tests/test_title_cache.py, tests/test_trading_gate.py) covering the round-trip, the idempotent migration against a pre-existing table, extraction of both True and False, and the backfill-vs-already-complete-cache-entry distinction. Verified live via direct data checks and real selenium-chrome screenshots: "Toronto vs Philadelphia," "Chicago C vs Kansas City," "Minnesota vs Milwaukee," "KOI vs. 100 Thieves: Map 4," and several tennis/esports match-winner events each collapsed to one row in both the Terminal and Markets tabs, while independent-prop events ("Outs Recorded," "Hits," "Team Total") and the genuine 60+-outcome Wyndham Championship Winner market correctly stayed fully expanded. Full suite: 338 passing.

phase 53 · the hints panel finally has something to say about 81% of real trades

"The config tunings hints section in the history section doesnt seem to give me actual advice at all."

The panel wasn't broken - it was being honest about not having anything to say - it was just silent on the one close type that actually mattered.

phase 54 · the header was showing all-time P&L wearing an "unrealized" name tag

"When I look at the portfolio tab I get values for bankroll, equity, and unrealized P&L, but the open positions themselves aren't shown, what a lie."

Investigated against the real running app rather than assumed: confirmed live, broker.bankroll and broker.equity were both $13,806.23 with zero open positions - exactly correct, since PaperBroker.equity() is defined as bankroll + total_unrealized_pnl(open positions), which is necessarily $0 with nothing open. But the header strip's "Unrealized P&L" figure showed +$3,806.23 anyway - a real, reproducible mismatch, not a stale-cache artifact.

Root cause: renderHeaderStrip()'s paper-mode branch computed the P&L stat as broker.equity - broker.starting_bankroll - cumulative all-time P&L including every historical closed trade's realized gain, not just the mark-to-market on currently-open positions. The correct figure was already one subtraction away and didn't need any new backend data: equity - bankroll isolates exactly total_unrealized_pnl by the broker's own definition, correctly landing on $0.00 when nothing's open. All-time P&L already has its own correctly-labeled home on the Trading History tab, so nothing was lost by fixing the label to match its own math.

Verified live via selenium-chrome against the real running app: header now reads Bankroll $13,806.23 / Equity $13,806.23 / Unrealized P&L $0.00, consistent with the Open Positions panel's real "No open positions" state. Full suite: 351 passing (frontend-only fix, no backend/test changes needed).

phase 55 · deep research, seven-factor confidence, real fees, and a doctorate-level LLM analyst

"I need you to be a single source of truth on how these markets work... don't simplify anything" → "compare these strategies and logic and make adjustments to all the relevant algorithms... I want to add an 'agent' that acts as a 'doctorate level prediction market trader'"

Two direct requests in sequence: deep, no-simplification research into prediction-market mechanics, then a systematic pass comparing this app's actual live algorithms against that research and adjusting them. Two docs came first: docs/prediction-markets-research-reference.md (market-mechanism theory and academic evidence, Kalshi-specific mechanics, and the regulatory landscape) and docs/prediction-market-strategy-alignment-plan.md (six concrete gaps between the research and this app's live code, phased into an implementation plan). Two load-bearing facts came from live-verifying against the real running app rather than trusting scraped docs: Kalshi's trade tape carries zero identity fields (confirmed anonymous, not just undocumented), and the real taker fee rounds up to the nearest $0.0001 (0.07 × contracts × price × (1-price)), not nearest-cent as blog sources claimed — checked against 3 real fills.

What shipped from the alignment plan: real fee modeling (services/kalshi_fees.py, wired into paper_broker.py's open/close so realized P&L is fee-inclusive, surfaced as a Fees column and Fees Paid stat on Trading History); two new composite-confidence factors reflecting Barclay & Warner's stealth-trading research (cluster_factor, reusing signal_log.find_clusters(), and trend_factor, reusing market_history.momentum()) rebalancing all seven factor weights; a fixed-width confidence-band calibration report alongside the existing tertile split, reflecting the hit-rate-vs-calibration distinction the research surfaced; and a favorite-longshot-bias-aware entry threshold in strategy_engine.evaluate() that requires extra confidence in a configurable longshot price zone, since FLB is confirmed worse for takers and this app's real order path is taker-only.

And the agent itself: new services/market_analyst_agent.py — an LLM (tool-forced structured output via the Anthropic SDK) reads a market's real title/rules/price plus this app's own accumulated whale/advisory track record and forms an independent probability estimate, later graded against real settlement via hit rate and Brier score. Advisory-only, never places a trade or writes config, and dual-gated (market_analyst.enabled config flag AND a real ANTHROPIC_API_KEY) so it stays fully inert by default. This is also the first real wiring of services/ml_feed.py's build_context_snapshot(), which had sat scaffolded and unwired since it was first built. New Config-tab "Market Analyst (AI)" section and History-tab panel (Analyses/Resolved/Hit Rate/Brier Score), both live-verified via selenium-chrome against the real running app — including a full enable/Save Config/reload round-trip confirming market_analyst.* persists correctly, then reverted back off.

48 new tests (kalshi_fees, market_analyst_agent, plus additions across whale_simulator, confidence_calibration, strategy_engine, paper_broker, trade_analytics, signal_log, whalewatchers_kalshi_trade_tape). Full suite: 399 passing (was 351).

phase 56 · from a background scan to a button, and an eighth confidence factor

"would it make more sense to let the heuristic analysis engines run and then just have an option to weave in the market analyst agent... with a button click versus fully automated?" → "whenever the market analysis agent runs I want it to inform the various engines... so they can run the added logic without consuming AI tokens"

Direct pushback on phase 55's own design, landed the same day. The market analyst agent was the first thing in this app that spends real money per call - everything else is zero-marginal-cost arithmetic - so an ambient per-tick background scan was the wrong shape for it, closer to the advisory engine's own "prove it, then promote it" philosophy than to how the whale/momentum strategies run. main.py's _maybe_run_market_analyst() was replaced with _run_market_analyst_for_ticker(), called only from a new POST /api/market-analyst/analyze endpoint, itself only ever called by a new "🔎 Analyze this market" button added to the per-market detail modal - a real API call now happens only when a human deliberately asks for one, for one specific market they're actually looking at. The button's result area lives in a new market-detail-analyst container deliberately outside market-detail-body, since that container gets wholesale-replaced by refreshMarketDetail() on every ~5s poll tick while the modal is open - inside it, an in-flight "Analyzing…" state or a just-shown result would get silently wiped mid-interaction, the same class of bug already fixed once for the Portfolio tab's whale-reason disclosures. market_analyst.max_analyses_per_tick (no longer meaningful once nothing runs on a tick) was removed from config, the Config tab, and the save path.

Two more direct requests landed alongside the redesign. First: "would it make sense... to add a database for the market analyst AI agent so it doesn't do extra work... and leverages its own history" - the database and the do-no-extra-work cooldown already existed (market_analyst_agent.last_analyzed_at(), wired since phase 55), but nothing fed the agent's own past accuracy back into its own prompt. New market_analyst_agent.build_prompt() parameter own_track_record, populated from the agent's own stats() (hit rate, Brier score, resolved count - a few numbers, not a token-heavy replay of past reasoning) and threaded through by analyze_market() before every call, so the model can see whether it's been running overconfident and self-calibrate.

Second: "I noticed you made a lot of changes to the whale simulator but is this also being worked into the 'real' whale watcher data and algorithms?" and immediately after, "whenever the market analysis agent runs I want it to inform the various 'engines' and heuristics features as well on what to do/how to act/what to change." composite_confidence_breakdown() (services/whale_simulator.py) gained an eighth factor, analyst_factor - agreement between a whale print's side and the market analyst's own independent lean on that exact ticker, via a new market_analyst_agent.analyst_lean() (a single indexed SQLite read, 24h freshness window, never a new LLM call) - defaulting neutral 0.5 the overwhelming majority of the time since it's populated only after a human has actually clicked Analyze on that market. All 8 weights rebalanced (depth .21/unusualness .09/proximity .13/context .08/agreement .13/cluster .13/trend .08/analyst .15). Wired into both real callers, confirmed directly against the "is this also in the real provider" question: services/whalewatchers/kalshi_trade_tape.py (the real, active-by-default whale-follow provider) and, since that engine never touched whale confidence at all, services/market_strategy.py's _entry_confidence() - there, conditionally folded into the factor set only when a fresh estimate exists, specifically so an ordinary momentum-only entry isn't permanently diluted by a phantom neutral factor the rest of the time.

15 new tests (market_analyst_agent's self-track-record + analyst_lean, kalshi_trade_tape's analyst_factor agreement/staleness, market_strategy's conditional-inclusion behavior, the new on-demand route's gating). Full suite: 414 passing (was 399). Live-verified via selenium-chrome: the Analyze button renders and is clickable from a real market detail modal.

phase 57 · a race condition found by asking the right question

"do another deep dive through the entire project to make sure no logic gaps, no failures to account for relevant data from the APIs, no bugs, and complete integration"

A dedicated second-pass audit of phase 56's own on-demand redesign, independent of the audit that shipped phase 56 itself. Found a real race: _run_market_analyst_for_ticker()'s cooldown check reads last_analyzed_at early, but nothing gets recorded until the very end, after two real awaits (get_market, then the LLM call itself). Concretely: open the market detail modal, click Analyze, close the modal before the response lands, reopen it for the same ticker (the button renders fresh/enabled every open), click Analyze again - both requests read the same pre-commit cooldown state, both pass, both spend a real Anthropic API call. Fixed with an in-process _analyzing_tickers set guarding the whole request (this app runs as one uvicorn worker, not a distributed fleet, so an in-process guard is the right level of complexity, not a database lock) - and, per this app's own established discipline, verified with a real regression test that interleaves two asyncio tasks against a deliberately slow fake client rather than trusting the fix by inspection.

Two more findings from the same pass. liquidity_dollars, open_interest_fp, and last_price_dollars were already sitting in the exact market_detail dict main.py fetches for the analyst - the same raw dict the dashboard's own market-detail modal reads those three fields from - but market_analyst_agent.build_prompt() never read them, so the LLM was reasoning with strictly less market context than a human sees on the same market's own detail view. Added at zero extra API cost. Separately: Trading History's Cost/Payout column tooltips still read "what was actually paid"/"actually received" from before fee modeling existed (phase 55) - technically wrong now that a Fees column sits right next to them holding the rest of the real number - fixed to describe the pre-fee raw figures honestly instead, the same "label must match what's displayed" bug class this project has hit before (see CLAUDE.md).

3 new tests (the concurrency regression, a `get_market()`-raises path that previously had no coverage at all, and `build_prompt()`'s new fields). Full suite: 417 passing (was 414).

main.py · services/market_analyst_agent.py · static/index.html · tests/test_trading_gate.py · tests/test_market_analyst_agent.py
phase 58 · the research finally reaches exits, risk, and advisory - and a foundational equity bug along the way

"i want to apply the same 'doctorate level market analyst' methodologies and practices and inferences to all the heuristics, engines, existing algorithms/formulas"

Everything through phase 57 applied the research to entry-side confidence scoring. Asked to extend it everywhere, scoped after a direct choice toward tightening the existing arithmetic against the research rather than adding new LLM calls, to exits, the kill switch, and advisory config-tuning specifically. check_exits' pnl_pct - driving take_profit_pct/stop_loss_pct/ auto_exit in both strategy_engine.py and market_strategy.py - turned out to be fee-blind, comparing against raw mark_to_market() price movement with neither the entry fee already paid nor the close fee the exit itself would incur ever subtracted: a stop_loss_pct: 0.10 didn't actually cap a loss at 10% of what was put in. Fixed in both files - several of strategy_engine.py's own exact-boundary tests needed their thresholds recomputed against the new, correct numbers rather than the old raw ones. Separately, sentiment-reversal exits weighted every whale print equally regardless of its own confidence - extended the same confidence-weighting the entry side already uses (Barclay & Warner's stealth-trading research) into _whale_lean(), mirrored in computeWhaleLean() (static/index.html) to keep both implementations' math identical, per that function's own explicit two-languages design note.

The daily-loss kill switch turned out to be checking realized bankroll only: both FollowTheWhaleStrategy.evaluate() and MarketNativeStrategy.evaluate_all() called self.broker.equity({}) - an empty prices dict silently makes every open position's unrealized P&L compute as exactly 0 (mark_to_market falls back to entry_price), so this was mathematically identical to just checking bankroll directly. A large real unrealized drawdown sitting in open positions could never trip the kill switch until something actually closed. Fixed by threading real, this-tick prices through both functions.

Verifying that fix surfaced something bigger: PaperBroker.equity() itself (bankroll + total_unrealized_pnl) turned out to under-report true portfolio value by the full cost basis of every open position - confirmed two independent ways (the project's own equity test's comment named a position's real $60 value right next to an assertion that didn't include it; and equity showed a real discontinuity, jumping by roughly a position's cost basis the instant it closed even at zero net price change). Not introduced this session - the same formula behind the dashboard's own Equity header figure since it was first built. Flagged directly rather than silently patched, given the blast radius; confirmed, then fixed as part of the same pass: new total_position_value() (cost basis + gain/loss, not gain/loss alone), equity() redefined to use it, and unrealized_pnl added as its own explicit field in PaperBroker.state() so the header strip reads it directly instead of re-deriving equity - bankroll - the exact re-derivation that broke once already (phase 54) and would have broken again the instant equity() stopped meaning what that subtraction assumed.

A live regression turned up mid-session (direct report: "im seeing an old bug re-appear. market ids instead of human readable titles") - the kill-switch fix's own latest_prices construction in market_strategy.py used float(m["yes_bid_dollars"]) guarded only by is not None, which lets an empty-string price through to float(""), which raises - aborting the whole tick before _fetch_event_titles ever ran, so any market not already cached fell back to its raw ticker. Fixed to the same or 0.5 idiom every other yes_bid_dollars read in this codebase already uses; the identical pre-existing latent bug in check_exits' own price lookup was fixed alongside it. Finally, the advisory engine gained a longshot-bonus-specific recommendation (_longshot_bonus_recommendation(), mirroring _entry_threshold_recommendation's exact shape) - closing the loop on favorite-longshot-bias research being applied at entry but never checked at the advisory layer.

9 new tests (2 kill-switch regressions, 2 equity-formula regressions incl. the close-continuity check, 1 state-field check, 4 advisory longshot-bucket tests), plus several existing exit-threshold tests recomputed against the corrected fee-inclusive math. Full suite: 426 passing (was 417). Live-verified via selenium-chrome and direct /api/state checks after the regression fix: zero raw-ticker leakage across all four main tabs, equity/bankroll/unrealized_pnl all consistent with zero open positions.

phase 59 · the real gap behind "raw ticker ids keep coming back"

"i see only ticker ids and such in the portfolio trade log, but when i click on the history tab then back to the portfolio it shows titles"

The phase-58 regression fix (an unguarded float() aborting the tick) was real, but the user kept seeing the same symptom afterward - this direct clue pinned down a second, different, pre-existing bug entirely. main.py's _relevant_tickers() scopes /api/state's market_titles payload to the current watchlist, open positions, and the signal/decision feeds - but never the Trade Log's own tickers (broker.recent_trades, exactly what the Portfolio tab's Trade Log renders). The instant a closed position's ticker aged out of the watchlist and feeds, its title silently dropped out of /api/state, even though the backend's full, unbounded title cache (services/title_cache.py) still had it. Visiting History only ever looked like a fix because that tab's own /api/trading-history endpoint separately backfills the shared client-side marketTitles object with its own, differently-scoped title lookup - the real gap was in _relevant_tickers() the whole time, unrelated to the History tab itself. Fixed by adding the last 25 trade-log tickers (matching recent_trades' own cap, so the scoped payload stays small) to the ticker set. New regression test manually confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code (via a real revert-and-rerun, not just reasoning about it) before trusting the fix.

Also updated the Whale Signals panel's info-icon tooltip and the Help modal's Confidence/Whale-lean/ Kill-switch glossary entries, both still describing the pre-session 4-factor confidence formula (with its old percentages), a stale 0.65 entry threshold, and a bankroll-only kill switch - none of which have been true since phase 55/56/58.

1 new test. Full suite: 427 passing (was 426).

main.py · static/index.html · tests/test_trading_gate.py
phase 60 · the real account gets the same title resolution paper trading always had

"make sure the REAL Kalshi stuff works just as well as the paper default"

Direct follow-up right after phase 59 landed - the trade_log fix closed the paper broker's gap, but the real connected Kalshi account's own positions and fills turned out to have no title-resolution path at all, not even a lagging one. renderRealPositions/renderRealFills in static/index.html already call the exact same marketLabel() the paper panels use - the frontend was never the problem. The gap was two-layered on the backend: (1) _relevant_tickers() never included the real account's tickers, so even a title that had been fetched once would fall out of /api/state's scoped market_titles; and (2) more fundamentally, trading_loop()'s open_position_tickers - the set fed to _fetch_markets' extra_tickers, the only mechanism that fetches title data for a market outside the current watchlist - never included them either, so a real position's title was often never fetched by this app in the first place, whether or not the market happened to already be on the watchlist.

Fixed both halves. trading_loop() now folds the previous tick's real account.positions.market_positions/account.fills.fills tickers into open_position_tickers (one tick old is fine - a real account's holdings don't change fast, and it's self-correcting every subsequent tick anyway, same "previous snapshot bootstraps this tick" pattern already used elsewhere in this loop). _relevant_tickers() now includes the current tick's real-account tickers in the scoped set, falling back to a fill's market_ticker field when ticker isn't present (both are real, confirmed fields - see _FILL_FIELDS).

Verified live against the real connected account, not just unit tests: all 11 real open positions and every distinct real fill ticker resolved to a real title after the fix (e.g. KXOSCARPIC-27-ODY → "Will The Odyssey win Best Picture at the Oscars?", KXMLBGAME-26AUG082010LADAZ-LAD → "Los Angeles D vs Arizona Winner?"), where every one of them previously showed only the raw ticker.

2 new tests. Full suite: 429 passing (was 427).

main.py · tests/test_trading_gate.py
phase 61 · a fix regressing the very next thing it should have protected

"the market watchlist doesn't appear to be updating/repopulating/removing closed markets and those with insufficient volume"

Direct fallout from phase 60, found immediately after: that fix's open_position_tickers folded in both the real account's open positions and its recent fills, feeding both into _fetch_markets' extra_tickers - the mechanism that force-fetches a ticker into state["markets"] regardless of whatever live-only/volume filtering discovery itself applies. That's the right call for a position (paper or real) - it needs continuing price/title updates even off the watchlist for its own exit checks - but wrong for a fill. get_fills(limit=50) returns real historical trade records that can span days or weeks, and unlike a position, a fill never "closes" - a market only rolls out of that window once enough newer fills replace it. The result: every market a fill referenced got force-fed back into the live watchlist every single tick for as long as its fill sat in the last-50 window, whether or not that market had finalized, resolved, or gone to zero volume days earlier.

Confirmed live before fixing: 15 markets sitting in /api/state's markets list with status: "finalized" and 0 24h volume, several closed 2-3 days earlier - permanently pinned there purely because a fill referencing them was still within the last-50-fills window. Fixed by splitting the ticker extraction into a new shared helper, _real_account_position_tickers() (positions only, no fills), used by both trading_loop()'s extra_tickers and _relevant_tickers() - fills still get their own title resolution for the real Trade Log display via _relevant_tickers()'s separate, unchanged fills lookup, they just no longer drive what's force-included in the live watchlist itself.

Verified live across three consecutive poll ticks rather than just reasoning about it: the watchlist count dropped 99 → 93 → 84 → 81 as the stale fill-pinned entries aged out, ending at zero finalized or below-min_volume_24h markets remaining. 1 new test. Full suite: 430 passing (was 429).

main.py · tests/test_trading_gate.py
phase 62 · re-tuning the whale threshold against what's actually live right now

"i have this feeling ... there should be a vast number more whale signals coming in even despite my whale watch settings"

A hunch, checked against real data before touching anything - same discipline as phase 45's near-identical investigation. Pulled the last 100 real trades across the current live-only watchlist and computed their real notional (count_fp × price, side-aware): zero cleared the live whale_watcher_kalshi.min_notional_usd value of $2,500 (the "documented default," restored there in the previous commit after being at $1 for earlier testing) - median trade size was ~$12.65, the single largest was $1,740. data/signal_log.db's own hourly bucket counts confirmed the same story from the other direction: roughly thousands of signals/hour while the threshold sat at $1, cratering to ~10-20/hour within the hour the threshold was restored to $2,500. The live-only watchlist right now leans toward lower-liquidity niche markets - ATP Challenger tennis, secondary golf pairings, mention markets, esports - where Kalshi's real trade sizes just don't reach four figures often, unlike the higher-volume political/macro markets $2,500 was originally calibrated against.

Presented the finding with a concrete recommendation rather than changing it unprompted, given it's a deliberately-set live tuning value, not a bug. Live-retuned to $500 (clears 8% of that same real sample - a real, meaningful jump without going as low as $1's "admits nearly every real trade" failure mode phase 45 already found) via the real POST /api/config endpoint, confirmed new signals landing within one poll tick. No code changed.

Direct follow-up flagged for later, not built yet: per-market-type (per-series) min_notional_usd thresholds instead of one global value, since a single number can't be right for both a quiet niche market and a high-volume headline one at the same time - added to ROADMAP.md's P2 section as an open design question.
