phase 63 · one threshold can't fit a golf pairing and a presidential election

the direct follow-up flagged at the end of phase 62, now built

Phase 62 re-tuned whale_watcher_kalshi.min_notional_usd to $500 as one global number, but flagged directly that no single number is right for both a quiet ATP Challenger tennis market and a high-volume political one at the same time. Built the fix rather than another retune: a new min_notional_usd_by_series dict, keyed by signal_log.series_of() - the exact same series-prefix definition excluded_series and series_stats already key off, so this didn't introduce a second notion of "series" alongside the one already in use. Every real trade in services/whalewatchers/kalshi_trade_tape.py's notional check now looks up its own series in that dict first, falling back to the existing global min_notional_usd for any series with no override set - so leaving the dict empty (the shipped default) changes nothing about current behavior.

New Config-tab field, "Per-series overrides", sitting directly under the existing threshold field in the Whale Signal (real) section - same comma-separated PREFIX:amount pairs convention the existing excluded_series field already uses, so it didn't need a new input pattern. Verified live with a real selenium-chrome round trip: typed values in, saved, reloaded the page cold, confirmed the value survived the reload through GET/POST /api/config and settings.yaml, then cleared it back to empty to leave the live config clean. 3 new tests. Full suite: 433 passing (was 430).

Side fix found while verifying, not part of the feature itself: .claude/hooks/run_tests.py (runs the suite via ddev exec -s fastapi after every service-file edit) was silently failing with "No module named pytest." Root cause: the repo-root Dockerfile only ever pip installed requirements.txt, never requirements-dev.txt (where pytest actually lives) - so pytest only ever existed in the running container by way of some earlier, unrecorded manual pip install, and a fresh image build (the exact thing a Dockerfile is supposed to make reproducible) silently lost the ability to run tests at all. Fixed by baking requirements-dev.txt into the same image - this is a dev-only container with no real deployment target yet, so there's no separate "prod image" to keep dev tooling out of. Verified with a real ddev restart (a genuine rebuild, not just --reload's in-process reimport): the full suite ran clean immediately after.

phase 64 · a missing scroll bound and a 69-market golf tournament

"ends up creating a mosaic of cards and extending the scrollable page to a ridiculous height"

Direct report about the Whale Watch tab's market-card grid. Confirmed live before touching anything: #whale-cards (and its identical Markets-tab twin, #markets-cards - same shared renderMarketCards()) was the one panel in either tab that never got the .scroll-panel height-bounding treatment applied to every other feed panel in an earlier pass - a bare, unbounded <div>. No backend changes, no new tests possible (no JS suite in this repo).

phase 65 · three different reasons a market's own identity kept getting lost

"A's vs Boston Total Runs? yes 1,290 - doesn't show the total run count"; "What will Walmart Inc. say... yes 76c no 24c"

Direct report with concrete examples of markets/positions losing which specific child market or side a price actually referred to. Three distinct root causes, not one: (a) yes_sub_title/ no_sub_title were already fetched and cached, but only 2 of 15+ render sites in static/index.html ever read them - marketCardHTML was the closest match, computing marketContext() and then only ever using .category/.matchup from it, silently discarding .positionMeans every time; (b) real Kalshi positions showed zero price at all (confirmed against the SDK: MarketPosition has no price field, ever) and no event-level grouping (event_positions was fetched every tick and dropped entirely before /api/state, already flagged unused in a code comment); (c) true combo/MVE markets got a fragile cosmetic label instead of real per-leg data - confirmed against the SDK that Kalshi has no parlay concept at the position/fill/order level at all, only at the market layer (Market.mve_selected_legs).

Fixed: consolidated 3 independently-duplicated title/sub-title builders (main.py's new_market_titles, /api/markets/search, market_catalog.upsert_markets) into one shared title_cache.market_title_fields(); restored + slimmed event_positions and used it for real-position event grouping; added _join_real_position_prices() attaching each real position's current yes-price server-side (not re-derived client-side, per this app's own documented displayed-financial-figure bug pattern); wired marketCardHTML to actually show the sub-title next to each side's price - confirmed fixed against both the real "Total Runs" market and the real Walmart earnings-call market from the report. Added real per-leg combo resolution off mve_selected_legs (new comboLegsHTML() expandable disclosure, reusing the whale-reason disclosure's exact pattern including its stopPropagation fix), wired into paper positions, real positions, and the market-detail modal.

Found and fixed a real, currently-live bug while building this: formatTitle()'s old comma-split heuristic was mislabeling an ORDINARY market whose title happened to contain a date ("before Aug 10, 2026?") as a fake "2-leg parlay" - confirmed on an actually-held real position before fixing it to key off the real mve_selected_legs field instead of guessing from text; updated the Help modal's glossary entry to match. Direct same-session follow-up: click-to-detail (openMarketDetail, already wired on paper positions and every market card) was missing on real positions, Trading History, the paper Trade Log, the whale signal feed, and the decision/"strategy log" feed - all six now open to full market detail, verified live on each. Scope note: the originally envisioned single shared display resolver migrated across all 15+ sites was descoped to these targeted highest-value fixes - Advanced fills/orders tables and the screener table still call the older marketLabel() alone, a known remaining gap (see ROADMAP.md). 8 new tests. Full suite: 440 passing (was 433, including phase 66 below).

phase 66 · a data-robustness audit, and a test that had been reading production data

"ensure real market data gets stored across all future changes/tests/refactors/rewrites... a robust dataset to act upon"

Direct instruction, proactive rather than tied to one incident - codified as a standing rule in CLAUDE.md's data-safety section and in memory, then acted on immediately with a real audit of the persistence layer. Found, and fixed the same session: tests/test_market_catalog.py's test_next_series_to_scan_prioritizes_never_scanned had no monkeypatch isolation at all - every pytest run in this repo had been reading and writing the real ~12MB data/market_catalog.db, fixed ahead of everything else given it was an active risk.

_scan_catalog_batch called market_catalog.mark_scanned() on its whole batch unconditionally, regardless of whether each series' fetch actually succeeded - return_exceptions=True swallowed failures with no logging at all, so a persistently-failing series would mark itself "freshly scanned" every ~59-minute rotation forever, indistinguishable from a healthy-but-quiet one, never actually writing a row. Fixed to only mark genuinely-succeeded series scanned, with a stdout print (this app has no logging framework at all, confirmed by repo-wide grep) so a failure is at least visible via ddev logs -s fastapi.

The entire trading-loop tick's own failure state (state["error"], present in /api/state's response the whole time) had zero frontend consumers anywhere, confirmed by grep - unlike the connectivity badge, which only tracks whether the browser's own poll reaches the server, a completely different failure mode. A backend tick could fail repeatedly forever, silently aborting real data collection mid-tick, with no visible indication anywhere in the app. Added a new #tick-health-badge (same pulsing visual language as the connectivity badge), verified live in all three states (normal/simulated-error/cleared). Two more real gaps found but not yet fixed this session, tracked in ROADMAP.md: a swallowed exception in the market analyst agent pointing at "server logs" that don't exist, and Danger Zone reset-coverage gaps for market_analyst/market_catalog/market_history. 3 new tests.

phase 68 · a chip that already existed everywhere except where it was needed

"the config tunings hints mechanism refers to variables that a human cant match on the config page"

Direct report, three complaints at once: Config tab fields are vague (labels, impact of changing a value, descriptions), and a Config Tuning Hint names a variable with nothing on the Config page to visually match it against. Catalogued all 55 tunable fields across the 10 core accordions before touching anything: only ~5 actually explained what raising or lowering the value does - the rest were a bare label, or a label plus a restated description with no impact framing. Confirmed the vocabulary mismatch concretely: a hint says "raising exit_sentiment_lean_pct or exit_sentiment_min_signals"; the Config labels for those two fields said "leaning against the position by at least this much" and "requires at least this many recent prints" - no shared words at all.

The fix for the "can't match the variable" half already existed elsewhere in the app - the History tab's Config Tuning Hints/Advisory Recommendations panels already render a monospace dotted-path chip next to each suggestion (strategy.exit_sentiment_min_signals-style); it just never made it onto the Config tab itself. Added a new .config-path CSS class reusing that exact visual pattern to all 55 fields, plus a .info-icon ⓘ tooltip (also an existing pattern, already used on the Terminal tab's Whale Signals panel) with plain-English "what does raising vs. lowering this do" text on 42 of them. Also gave each of the 10 <details> accordions a stable id (8 of 10 had none before) - groundwork for a future "jump to this setting" link from a suggestion card, not built yet.

Found and fixed three real defects along the way, not just vagueness: Market-Native Strategy's max-price field's entire label was the fragment "...and", meaningful only next to the prior field; that same section's take-profit/stop-loss fields said far less than the near-identical Exits-section fields for the exact same cost-basis mechanic, brought up to matching depth; the Risk section's daily-loss field was never called "kill switch" despite that being the exact term the Help modal's own glossary already uses for this precise mechanism. Frontend-only, no backend changes. Verified live via selenium-chrome: all 10 section ids present, exactly 55/55 path chips rendered (matching the grounding pass's own count), all 3 defect fixes read correctly, and a full Save Config → reload → value-persisted round trip still works with no regression.

Explicitly deferred, not part of this phase: dynamic data-driven impact previews (e.g. "raising this would have changed N past trades") - discussed directly and scoped out because it depends on still-unbuilt change-tracking machinery and has a real technical asymmetry (cheap for entry-style fields, not honestly computable for exit-timing fields without a real backtest engine).

phase 69 · a watchlist with no memory finally gets some

"i want a 'series evaluator' that checks to see if its even 'whale-worthy' ... an increasingly strict back-off"

Direct request. Confirmed before building anything: the watchlist is fully recomputed from scratch every tick (main.py's _fetch_markets → KalshiClient.round_robin_select) with zero memory across ticks - nothing dampens a series flapping in and out near a volume/live-status cutoff, the literal mechanism behind the flapping the user wanted stopped. Also confirmed no per-series "worth watching" concept existed anywhere - every whale-quality filter (excluded_series, min_whale_winrate_pct) is per-*signal*, post-hoc, so none of them could judge a series with zero history - and no escalating-backoff building block existed either; every cooldown in the app is a flat constant.

New services/series_evaluator.py (data/series_evaluator.db), gated behind series_evaluator.enabled (default off). Resolved the "before or after" question as a hybrid, not either/or: BEFORE re-admission, a cheap batch query (ineligible_series()) filters both discovery branches' candidate pools ahead of round_robin_select each tick - this is what actually stops the flapping, free since it needs no whale-signal data. AFTER admission, the real whale-worthy verdict (evaluate_pending()) can only render once a series has had real trade-tape data to observe - judged on the qualifying RATE (real trades observed vs. how many cleared the whale-notional threshold), not raw count, sample-size-hedged the same way confidence_calibration.py/advisory_engine.py already gate their own reports. The denominator (trades_observed) is genuinely new - nothing before this tracked how many real trades were simply *seen* on a series, only how many qualified. Recorded unconditionally in kalshi_trade_tape.py's fetch_signals() loop, not gated behind enabled, so the feature has real history the moment it's turned on instead of a cold start - validated live during implementation itself: the running app picked up the code change via --reload and had already accumulated real observation data for 24 real series (KXPGATOUR, KXMLBGAME, KXBTCD, ...) before the rest of the feature was even wired into main.py.

Approval is sticky (never auto-revoked), matching how strategy.excluded_series is already a settled, human-legible judgment. A rejected series serves an escalating backoff (doubling each consecutive rejection, capped) before re-admission - or a human can manually re-evaluate early, a deliberate fresh start that clears the strike count too (unlike automatic reactivation after backoff is served, which carries it forward as a continuation of the same escalation). New Config-tab accordion (8 knobs, same path-chip/tooltip pattern as phase 68, defaults flagged as untuned placeholders) and a History-tab log - every series ever evaluated, independent of the current watchlist (the persisted table doubles as the log), with a Re-evaluate button. New GET/POST /api/series-evaluator/* routes and a Danger Zone bulk-reset flag.

Found and fixed a real test-isolation gap while implementing this: the existing test_whalewatchers_kalshi_trade_tape.py fixture redirected three other modules' DB_PATH but not the new module's, so test runs briefly wrote to the real data/series_evaluator.db before the fixture was fixed - the exact bug class this app's own "preserve real data across all future changes" standing rule (added earlier this same session, now in CLAUDE.md) exists to prevent. Cleaned the two test-contaminated rows out of the live file by hand, explicitly preserving the genuine real-data rows already there. 33 new tests. Full suite: 464 passing (was 442). Verified live via selenium-chrome: Config-tab section renders and round-trips through save/reload, History-tab panel correctly displays the real 24-series accumulated data.

phase 70 · two advisors that couldn't see each other's evidence

Merging Config Tuning Hints into Advisory Recommendations, part 1 of the self-tuning unification

First step of a larger initiative (direct request: Config Tuning Hints named config fields the Config tab's own labels didn't visually match; Advisory Recommendations were needlessly gated to trades placed under the *exact* current config; the market analyst agent should eventually analyze a whole series or the whole platform; all of it should "work in tandem"). Grounding found these were two engines answering the same underlying question in two shapes: trade_analytics.compute_insights() was purely descriptive prose with no apply mechanism, while advisory_engine.generate_recommendations() had the real apply/audit-trail machinery but filtered every per-field suggestion to rows where config_fingerprint == current_fp - so changing any one strategy.* field reset every *other* field's sample back to zero. One fix covers both complaints: give Advisory's per-field logic compute_insights()'s broad, all-trades data scope, and give the hints Advisory's structured/appliable shape.

Merged into one engine and one panel. Every per-field suggestion (entry threshold, longshot bonus, take-profit/stop-loss, and three newly-ported ones - auto-exit threshold, sentiment-exit lean/signals) now reads the full trade history with no fingerprint gate, in one structured shape. Fixed two real bugs surfaced while porting the old hints: the dropped exit_sentiment_min_signals suggestion (the old insight's topic only ever named exit_sentiment_lean_pct, even though its own text recommended tuning both), and a wrong strategy. prefix hardcoded on min_momentum_delta - which turned out worse than it first looked: that field actually lives under market_strategy.*, and since momentum_reversal closes are only ever produced by the separate Market-Native Strategy broker (never the whale-follow broker compute_insights was reading), the old insight was unreachable dead code the whole time, not just mislabeled. Confirmed fixed live: /api/advisory/recommendations now returns a real market_strategy.min_momentum_delta suggestion (n=20) against real production data. generate_recommendations() dropped its old blanket gate entirely - per-field suggestions now self-hedge on their own sample size, same as compute_insights always did; only the cross-variant "a fully different past config did better" comparison still needs a per-variant floor, since that comparison can't mean anything on a thin sample. compute_insights() itself was removed; the one observation that never mapped onto a tunable field (settled-vs-actively-managed win rate) survives as its own small exit_management_split() function. The apply route generalized from assuming every config_path starts with strategy. to splitting on the first ., since a suggestion can now carry a market_strategy.* path too.

Frontend: the two History-tab panels merged into one ("Advisory Recommendations"), plus a small new "Exit Management" panel for the one non-appliable observation. Suggestion cards now show the same dotted .config-path chip the Config tab overhaul shipped (phase 68) instead of a plain monospace span - and since both sides now render identical path text, clicking a suggestion's chip jumps to the Config tab, auto-expands the right accordion, scrolls to, and briefly highlights the matching field, with no per-field id-mapping table needed at all. 466 tests passing (was 464). Verified live via selenium-chrome: the old panel is fully gone, Exit Management renders real stats (69% vs 41%, n=39/n=88), Advisory Recommendations shows 3 real suggestion cards from live history, and the chip-click-to-jump behavior works end to end.

services/advisory_engine.py · services/trade_analytics.py · main.py · static/index.html · tests/test_advisory_engine.py · tests/test_trade_analytics.py · tests/test_trading_gate.py
phase 71 · an audit trail with a blind spot on its most important field

Change-history logging for every config-change source, plus a real before/after effect delta

Second step of the self-tuning unification, following phase 70. Grounding found config_performance.log_applied_change() only ever fired from the Advisory apply route (POST /api/advisory/recommendations/apply) - a plain manual Config-tab save via POST /api/config, including the real-trading enable/disable confirmation toggle, was never logged to the applied_changes audit trail at all.

Added a source column to applied_changes (backfills every pre-existing row as unified-advisory via the column's own SQL default - factually correct, since that was the only source that ever existed before this) and a new config_performance.diff_patch() helper that mirrors ConfigStore.update()'s own one-level-deep merge logic exactly, so a route can compute precisely which fields a patch changed (skipping no-op saves) without reimplementing that merge separately. Wired logging into all three previously-silent routes: POST /api/config (one row per changed field, source='manual'), POST /api/trading/enable, and POST /api/trading/disable - the last two were a deliberate inclusion beyond the original grounding's scope, since kalshi_account.trading_enabled is arguably the single most safety-critical field in the app and leaving it as the one blind spot in an otherwise-complete audit trail felt wrong.

Also shipped the "effect tracking" half: advisory_engine.change_effect() computes a real, measured before/after win-rate + realized-P&L delta for a strategy.* change that actually created a new config variant with trades resolved on both sides, reusing the exact variant_summaries() machinery cross-variant recommendations already read - not a new computation. Deliberately does NOT attempt this for market_strategy.*/risk.*/etc. - this app's fingerprinting has only ever covered the strategy.* subset, so those changes always log an identical fingerprint_before/fingerprint_after, which change_effect() correctly reports as "nothing to compare" rather than fabricating a number. A real, disclosed scope boundary (extending fingerprinting to cover market_strategy.* would be a separate, larger change), not silently dropped.

New "Change History" panel on the History tab shows every logged change - .config-path chip, source + timestamp, old→new value, rationale, and the measured effect line when one exists. 482 tests passing (was 466). Verified live end to end: a real, disposable config round-trip (series_evaluator.min_qualify_rate 0.01→0.02→0.01 via curl) appeared correctly with source='manual'/effect=null, and config/settings.yaml diffed clean afterward; the new panel itself confirmed rendering both real rows correctly via selenium-chrome, no console errors.

main.py · services/advisory_engine.py · services/config_performance.py · static/index.html · tests/test_advisory_engine.py · tests/test_config_performance.py · tests/test_trading_gate.py
phase 72 · teaching the analyst to grade a whole tournament, not one match

Market analyst per-series analysis mode

Third step of the self-tuning unification, following phases 70-71. Direct request: the market analyst agent should be able to "analyze a whole series," not just one market. A series-wide whale-signal/closed-trade quality judgment is a genuinely different question from a single market's own probability estimate, so it got its own tool schema (record_series_analysis) rather than overloading the existing single-market one.

services/market_analyst_agent.py gained a new, separate series_analyses table - kept distinct from the pre-existing analyses table rather than repurposing it, since that table's schema is tightly single-market-probability-shaped (estimated_probability/llm_confidence/ticker are all NOT NULL) and SQLite can't cleanly relax a NOT NULL constraint via an additive ALTER TABLE - a new table is itself an additive change, not a drop-and-recreate. New build_series_prompt()/analyze_series() mirror the existing single-market functions' exact gating/error-handling shape. Context assembled by main.py's new _build_series_context(): real whale-signal stats scoped to the series, this series' own closed-trade summary, its current strategy.excluded_series membership and min_notional_usd_by_series override, and its series_evaluator verdict if it's ever been evaluated - real synergy between Item 1 and Item 3, as originally planned.

Suggestions land in the exact same unified {config_path, current_value, suggested_value, id, rationale} shape Advisory's rule-based suggestions already use - advisory_engine._rec_id made public as rec_id since it's now a shared id-generation recipe used by two modules. Deliberately scoped the agent's actionable output to strategy.excluded_series membership only, a real disclosed limitation: a per-series notional-threshold suggestion would need bespoke nested-dict merge logic the app's config-patch apply mechanism doesn't support, since min_notional_usd_by_series is a dict keyed by series and a naive patch there would clobber every other series' own override - the model still sees that value as read-only context, it just can't act on it yet. New "🔎 Analyze" button on every Series Evaluator row (Item 1's log panel, its natural home), rendering into a new #series-analysis-result area with the same suggestion-card/apply/jump-to-setting UX the merged Advisory panel already has. New routes POST /api/market-analyst/series/analyze and .../series/apply (looks up a suggestion by id against what was actually persisted at analysis time, since an LLM's raw output isn't deterministically recomputable the way a rule-based suggestion is) - applying one logs to the same applied_changes audit trail from phase 71, tagged source='series-analyst'.

509 tests passing (was 482). Verified against the real running app via curl: POST /api/market-analyst/series/analyze correctly returned the honest "No ANTHROPIC_API_KEY configured" gate (this dev environment has no real key set), proving the complete gating/wiring path end to end without spending a real API call.

phase 73 · the last piece of the loop: an analyst that can touch any dial

"Feed the Analyst" full-spectrum scan - Item 3 complete

Fourth and final step of the self-tuning unification, following phases 70-72. Direct request: the market analyst agent should be able to run a "full-spectrum" scan across all config/history/ whale-watching data, suggestion→confirm→apply, same mechanism as the single-market and per-series modes but broader. Unlike those two, a full-spectrum suggestion can name ANY config field, not a fixed one (3B was deliberately scoped to just strategy.excluded_series) - a materially wider blast radius, so this is the one mode where every raw model suggestion gets validated against the LIVE config before being persisted as appliable: the config_path must resolve to a section+field that already exists (never inventing a new one), must not be one of the two fields already protected from generic manual edits (kalshi_account.trading_enabled, advisory.auto_apply_enabled), must actually differ from the current value, and must be a reasonably type-compatible value - a new _types_compatible() helper treats int/float as interchangeable but checks bool strictly on both sides, since Python's bool is technically an int subclass and a stray True/False landing in a numeric field would be real config corruption, not a legitimate suggestion.

services/market_analyst_agent.py gained a third table (full_spectrum_analyses - same reasoning as 3B's separate table, no natural "subject" column to reuse), a widened tool schema whose suggestions array lets the model return MULTIPLE config changes in one call, and analyze_full_spectrum() mirroring the existing two modes' gating/error-handling shape exactly. main.py's new _build_full_spectrum_context() assembles the prompt from ONLY aggregated rollups - compute_summary() for both brokers, variant_summaries(), the current unified advisory recommendations list, the phase-71 change-history audit trail, and the busiest 10 series' whale breakdown - deliberately never raw per-trade/per-signal rows, so prompt size stays bounded regardless of how much history accumulates.

Given no cost/latency numbers exist anywhere for this agent and this prompt is materially bigger than the other two modes, the "🔬 Feed the Analyst" button is gated behind a native confirm() dialog disclosing exactly what gets sent - matches the severity of Advisory's own apply-confirmation, not the typed phrase reserved for real-money trading. New panel on the History tab between Market Analyst and Series Evaluator, reusing the same suggestion-card/apply/ jump-to-setting UX the other modes already established. New routes POST /api/market-analyst/full-spectrum/analyze and .../full-spectrum/apply (same persisted-lookup trust model as 3B's apply route). Applying a suggestion logs source='full-spectrum-analyst' - closing the loop across all four sources the change-history schema was designed to support back in phase 71.

538 tests passing (was 509). Verified against the real running app: the route correctly returned the honest "No ANTHROPIC_API_KEY configured" gate via curl, and a selenium-chrome pass confirmed the confirm() dialog text, button disable/re-enable cycle, and result rendering all work end to end with zero console errors. This completes Item 3 (the unified self-tuning subsystem) entirely - 3A/3B/3C/3D have all shipped.

phase 74 · closing the loop, one finding at a time

Deep-scan finding 3: the market analyst's estimate finally gets a say on exits

First item implemented from docs/platform-deep-scan-findings-2026-08-10.md's own recommended sequencing - deliberately started with its cheapest, safest item. market_analyst_agent.analyst_lean() already fed entry confidence on both strategies (market_strategy.py, kalshi_trade_tape.py) but had zero exit-path callers - a position could sit at a healthy unrealized gain while a freshly re-analyzed analyst estimate now put the true probability well below the current price, and nothing would ever notice. Added a fourth analyst_divergence factor to strategy_engine.py's _exit_confidence() composite auto-exit score, same "leave it out of the average entirely when absent" idiom the sentiment/staleness factors already use - 50/50 means no exit pressure, fully opposite means full pressure, correctly flipping direction for a held "no" vs "yes" position. New strategy.auto_exit_analyst_weight config field (default 0.5), added to the Config tab's Exits section with the same path-chip/tooltip pattern every field there uses.

Found and fixed a real test-isolation gap while adding coverage: tests/test_strategy_engine.py never redirected market_analyst_agent's DB_PATH, so the new analyst_lean() call inside check_exits() would have read the real data/market_analyst.db on every test run - the same bug class this project already caught and fixed twice earlier this session. No actual contamination occurred this time (the real file already existed with empty tables, and analyst_lean() never writes), but the redirect is now in place regardless. 4 new tests. 542 tests passing (was 538). Verified live: /api/config reflects the new field, and a selenium-chrome pass confirmed it renders correctly in the Config tab with zero console errors. The new config field was isolated into its own clean single-line commit via a temporary reset/edit/commit/restore sequence, leaving the live file's own real concurrent user tuning completely undisturbed.

phase 75 · five different markets, one real-world bet

Deep-scan finding 2: a concentration-risk cap across simultaneously-open positions

Second item implemented from docs/platform-deep-scan-findings-2026-08-10.md's own recommended sequencing, following phase 74. The only per-position entry gate either strategy had was per-ticker ("is this exact market already open") - nothing aggregated by series, so a burst of correlated signals (a whole tournament, an election contract family) could each individually clear max_position_pct while collectively representing a much bigger bet on one real-world outcome than the risk config implies. Added a shared strategy_engine.open_position_count_in_series(broker, ticker) helper, following the exact same "shared helper lives in strategy_engine.py, market_strategy.py imports it" precedent close_if_settled() already established - wired into both strategies' entry gates right after the existing per-ticker check.

phase 76 · the last sequenced finding, plus what the real data actually said

Deep-scan finding 1: edge-aware position sizing, a real calibration crash fix, and data-backed tuning

Third and last item from docs/platform-deep-scan-findings-2026-08-10.md's recommended sequencing, following phases 74-75 - position sizing was pure fixed-fraction (risk_manager.max_trade_size = bankroll × max_position_pct), so a signal that barely cleared the entry bar and one at near-perfect confidence got identically sized positions, throwing away the composite confidence score the instant the entry decision was made. New shared strategy_engine.kelly_scaled_max_size(max_size, confidence, effective_threshold, kelly_fraction) - a fractional-Kelly-style linear interpolation, not literal Kelly (this app's confidence score isn't a calibrated win probability yet). New strategy.kelly_fraction_of_cap/market_strategy.kelly_fraction_of_cap config fields default to 0.0 (today's flat-cap behavior, unchanged) - same opt-in precedent as every other optional engine in this app. Wired into both strategies right where contracts = int(max_size / unit_cost) is computed. 13 new tests, 562 passing.

Direct request: consulted the app's own real accumulated data before touching config further. Found and fixed a real crash bug along the way - confidence_calibration.generate_calibration_report() would KeyError instantly against real production history, since cluster_factor/trend_factor/ analyst_factor were all added to the composite score after this app had already logged its first ~9000 real signals; confirmed directly against data/signal_log.db that all 9231 currently-resolved signals lack those three keys. _bucket_win_rates() now excludes rows missing a given factor before bucketing, same "leave it out when absent" idiom used everywhere else in this app, instead of crashing.

Applied the three "higher"-confidence Advisory Recommendations this app's own real trade history already supported, through the real apply route (full audit trail, not hand-edited): strategy.exit_sentiment_lean_pct 85→90 and strategy.exit_sentiment_min_signals 9→11 (sentiment-reversal exits have closed 88 real positions averaging -$12.89 each - independently corroborated by exit_management_split showing actively-managed exits winning only 40.9% of the time vs 68.1% for positions held to resolution, the single largest, best-evidenced problem in the live trade history), and market_strategy.min_momentum_delta 0.04→0.05 (momentum-reversal exits have closed 20 real positions averaging -$85.77 each, the same pattern). Enabled confidence_calibration.enabled now that the crash is fixed and real data (9231 resolved signals) clears its 50-signal gate by two orders of magnitude. The report itself surfaced a genuine, disclosed finding, not silently acted on: unusualness_factor and agreement_factor both show negative discrimination against real outcomes (-11.2 and -9.7 points) - the opposite of their current positive hardcoded weights in whale_simulator.py. Per that module's own explicit v1 read-only scope, nothing here changes those weights automatically - flagged for a human to review, not resolved.

phase 77 · a formula that used to be permanent gets a config key

Whale-confidence scoring weights made config-editable, retuned from real data

Direct follow-up on phase 76's own calibration finding, same session, per direct instruction to auto-fix what needs fixing: composite_confidence_breakdown()'s 8 scoring weights were hardcoded literals with no live value - confidence_calibration.py's own module docstring already named this as the exact blocker to acting on any calibration finding at all. Extracted into a new whale_simulator.DEFAULT_WEIGHTS dict and a weights: dict | None parameter (partial-override semantics - a dict missing a key falls back to DEFAULT_WEIGHTS for that key, never silently scores it as 0). Wired into kalshi_trade_tape.py (the real provider) via a new whale_confidence_weights config section; the simulator's own synthetic-data path stays on DEFAULT_WEIGHTS intentionally, since real calibration findings have no bearing on fake data.

Actual values shipped, real-data-informed: depth_factor 0.21→0.25, unusualness_factor 0.09→0.03, proximity_factor 0.13→0.20, context_factor 0.08→0.13, agreement_factor 0.13→0.03 - redistributed the "evidence-backed" 64% of the weight budget proportional to each factor's own real discrimination gap (unusualness/agreement floored at the module's existing 0.05 minimum, same floor _suggested_weights() already used). cluster_factor/trend_factor/ analyst_factor (0.13/0.08/0.15) left completely unchanged - zero real data exists for any of them yet. generate_calibration_report() now takes a current_weights param reflecting whatever's actually live in config instead of a stale hardcoded mirror - dropped its own duplicate CURRENT_WEIGHTS constant in favor of importing DEFAULT_WEIGHTS directly, one source of truth instead of two copies that could drift.

New read-only "Whale-Signal Calibration" History-tab panel (between Change History and Market Analyst) - the report had zero UI consumer before this, a real gap this session already found. Shows the per-factor discrimination table (current vs. suggested weight side by side) and the confidence-bands table; editing weights stays config-file/API only for now, same "backend ships first, editable UI is a disclosed follow-up" precedent market_strategy already established. 11 new tests. 569 tests passing (was 562). Applied live via ddev restart (0 open positions confirmed first) so this actually changes what real trades get scored with, not just what's on disk - confirmed via the live report endpoint and a selenium-chrome pass against real, live-updating data (10188+ signals) with zero console errors. Closes deep-scan finding 4 in spirit - calibration findings now have a real destination to act on.

phase 78 · counterfactuals

Rejected-candidate logging - what happened to the trades every gate turned away

Direct follow-up to docs/config-tuning-data-gaps-2026-08-10.md's Gap 1, the single highest-leverage gap found during that session's own full settings.yaml data review: advisory_engine's own entry_threshold/longshot-bonus recommendations return None against real trade history specifically because nothing ever recorded what happened to a candidate a gate rejected - only the ones that passed. Every entry/discovery gate in this app previously produced a bare boolean; now each one calls a new services/candidate_log.py's record_rejection() instead: strategy_engine.py's entry_threshold/min_whale_winrate_pct, market_strategy.py's min_price/max_price/ max_spread/min_volume_24h/min_seconds_to_close/ min_momentum_delta/entry_confidence_threshold, and kalshi_trade_tape.py's min_notional_usd.

Own SQLite file (data/candidate_log.db), same idiom as every other module - but with a dedup-keyed upsert on (ticker, strategy, gate_name) rather than one row per rejection, since market_strategy.py re-scans every real market every tick and would otherwise grow the table unboundedly for a market that just sits below a threshold for hours. Resolved passively off the same market_results dict the trading loop already builds each tick - zero new API calls, mirroring market_analyst_agent.resolve_from_market_results's existing shape exactly. gate_summary()'s hypothetical_win_rate (would a position on the implied side have won) is only computed where that side was actually knowable at rejection time - market_strategy.py's price/spread/volume gates run before a side is ever chosen, so those rows stay side=None and report a plain yes/no resolution split instead of a fabricated rate; the momentum-delta gate is the one exception where a hypothetical side could still be inferred from the delta's own sign even though the gate itself failed.

New GET /api/candidate-log/summary, a candidate_log Danger Zone reset flag (parity with market_catalog/market_history/series_evaluator), and a read-only "Rejected Candidates" History-tab panel between Whale-Signal Calibration and Market Analyst - real gate rejections were already accumulating within seconds of deploy (77 min_notional_usd, 11 entry_threshold, confirmed live). 14 new tests: a new tests/test_candidate_log.py plus integration assertions in the three files whose gates got wired up, each of which also needed its existing DB-isolation fixture extended with a candidate_log.DB_PATH redirect - the same real-data-contamination bug class this project has already found and fixed twice before.

services/candidate_log.py · services/strategy_engine.py · services/market_strategy.py · services/whalewatchers/kalshi_trade_tape.py · main.py · static/index.html · tests/test_candidate_log.py · tests/test_strategy_engine.py · tests/test_market_strategy.py · tests/test_whalewatchers_kalshi_trade_tape.py
phase 79 · unfragmented effects

A second, looser before/after measurement that isn't starved by config-variant fragmentation

Direct follow-up to Gap 3 of docs/config-tuning-data-gaps-2026-08-10.md: the existing advisory_engine.change_effect() (phase 71) requires an exact config_performance fingerprint match on both sides of a change, and real live data confirmed why that starves for evidence fast - 16 distinct strategy.* fingerprints exist, most with 0 resolved trades, since fingerprint() hashes the entire strategy.* dict and almost any single-field tweak mints a brand-new one that then needs its own trades to accumulate from zero. New change_effect_windowed(config_path, applied_at, rows) is a looser, complementary measurement: splits trades by entry_timestamp before/after the change's applied_at, regardless of which exact fingerprint produced them - a real, disclosed tradeoff (broader data, weaker causal attribution, since other fields may also have changed in the same window), meant to be read alongside the strict measurement, not replace it. It also needs no fingerprint transition at all, so it's the only effect measurement a market_strategy.*/risk.*/etc. change can ever get - change_effect()'s own fingerprinting only ever covers strategy.*.

Wired into GET /api/advisory/applied-changes as a new effect_windowed field on every row (picks market_broker's own trade history instead of the whale-follow broker's when config_path starts with market_strategy.). The Change History panel falls back to rendering it, clearly labeled "time-windowed, not config-isolated", only when the strict effect is null. Verified live against real applied-change history: several strategy.exit_sentiment_lean_pct/exit_sentiment_min_signals rows that showed effect: null now show a real windowed delta (50.0% win rate n=134 before → 100.0% n=1 after).

services/advisory_engine.py · main.py · static/index.html · tests/test_advisory_engine.py
phase 80 · stateless replay

Backtest sweeps - what a different threshold would have done to real historical signals, without a full replay engine

Gap 2 of docs/config-tuning-data-gaps-2026-08-10.md, stateless half only (the doc's own harder "stateful" half - replaying the whole trading loop's cooldown/concentration/bankroll state - was explicitly deferred, not attempted here). New services/backtest.py: entry_threshold_sweep() and min_whale_winrate_pct_sweep(), pure functions that replay a candidate gate value against every already-logged resolved signal and report what win rate the signals it would have accepted actually had. No new persistence - these are pure functions over data signal_log.py already has.

A real, disclosed scope boundary was found while building this, not assumed up front: the gaps doc itself claimed "signal_log already has the input data for signals that exist," but the signals table only ever stored confidence/side/series/correct - never price, spread, volume, or raw notional. That means this replay can faithfully cover strategy.entry_threshold and strategy.min_whale_winrate_pct cleanly, but not market_strategy.py's price-band/spread/volume/momentum gates or the longshot-zone fields - documented directly in the module's own docstring rather than silently overclaimed. Two new signal_log.py helpers support the win-rate-floor sweep specifically: all_series_stats() (every series' resolved count/win rate in one GROUP BY query instead of N per-series calls) and resolved_signals_with_series() (the raw resolved rows, tagged by series, the sweep recombines under a candidate floor).

New GET /api/backtest/entry-threshold/.../min-whale-winrate + a read-only "Backtest Sweeps" History-tab panel (between Rejected Candidates and Market Analyst), current live threshold/floor highlighted inline in each table. 16 new tests, 598 passing. Verified live against real history (11.5k-17k resolved signals depending on the sweep) - and surfaced a genuinely interesting real finding along the way, not fabricated: the entry-threshold sweep's win rate is roughly U-shaped across real data (52.7% at threshold 0.0, dipping to 49.7% near 0.30, climbing back to 58-65% above 0.45) - another data point alongside the earlier confidence-calibration finding (phase 76's <50% band outperforming the 70-80% band) suggesting the composite confidence formula's own calibration deserves more scrutiny before leaning on it further (see Gap 6, still open).

phase 81 · raw context

Capturing the raw notional/spread/volume a signal's factor breakdown was derived from, not just the derived scores

Gap 8 of docs/config-tuning-data-gaps-2026-08-10.md: kalshi_trade_tape.py's fetch_signals() already computes real notional dollar size, market spread, and 24h volume for every signal - but those locals were discarded the moment the WhaleSignal was built, leaving only the already-derived 0-1 factor scores persisted. That's a real, disclosed limitation: a future analysis can ask "does depth_factor discriminate" but never "would a differently- shaped transform of the same raw depth data discriminate better," since re-deriving raw inputs after the fact is unreliable (market_catalog/market_history are watchlist-scoped and rotate).

New WhaleSignal.raw_context field, deliberately kept separate from factors rather than folded in - every value in factors is a 0-1 score confidence_calibration.py's tertile-bucketing logic assumes, and a raw dollar/ratio magnitude mixed into that dict would corrupt it. Three new nullable signal_log columns (raw_notional_usd/raw_spread/ raw_volume_24h, idempotent ALTER TABLE); resolved_signals_with_factors() now returns them alongside confidence/correct/factors - null for every signal logged before this column existed, same "filter for non-null" convention confidence_calibration.py already established for factors_json keys added after data existed.

Pure data-capture, no new UI panel - there's zero historical data under this schema until enough new signals accumulate, same framing the gaps doc itself used ("turns an unanswerable question into a real one, without needing to touch the formula itself yet"). Directly sets up Gap 2's backtest sweeps to eventually cover more than confidence/side/series. 7 new tests, 601 passing. Verified live via a direct sqlite3 query against the real data/signal_log.db - new real signals already capturing raw context within seconds of deploy.

phase 82 · cross-checked

Two independent series-quality mechanisms, cross-checked against each other for the first time

Gap 4 of docs/config-tuning-data-gaps-2026-08-10.md, diagnostic-only, no new persistence (both data sources already existed). This app has two genuinely independent per-series quality mechanisms - series_evaluator.py judges by *qualifying rate* (real trades observed vs. how many cleared the notional threshold), strategy_engine.py's own min_whale_winrate_pct gate judges by *realized win rate* - and they had never been checked against each other. GET /api/series-evaluator/status now joins series_evaluator.overview() against signal_log.all_series_stats() (Gap 2's bulk per-series query) inline, attaching whale_win_rate/below_winrate_floor to every row - the latter mirrors strategy_engine.py's real gate comparison exactly (resolved >= min_resolved_for_whale_filter AND win_rate < floor), not a second, possibly-drifting definition.

Surfaced a real disagreement the moment this shipped, not a hypothetical: KXATPCHALLENGERMATCH is series-evaluator-approved for the watchlist, but below_winrate_floor: true (34.4% win rate over 2326 resolved signals, under the 40% floor) - the *other* gate is already silently filtering its real trades out even though this one approved it for discovery. Exactly the kind of disagreement this cross-check exists to catch. The Series Evaluator History-tab panel now shows each row's real win rate inline, flagged with a warning when it's below the floor.

No new tests - a thin inline join in main.py over two already-tested functions, verified live via curl (the real disagreement above) and selenium-chrome (panel renders, zero new console errors). 601 tests passing, unchanged.

phase 83 · trend, not snapshot

Calibration history - is discrimination improving as more data accumulates, or stuck?

Gap 6 of docs/config-tuning-data-gaps-2026-08-10.md: confidence_calibration.py already computes a real report on demand - overall win rate, per-factor discrimination gaps, current weights - but only ever as a single point-in-time snapshot, recomputed fresh and discarded on every request. There was no way to ask "is calibration improving as more data and the retuned weights accumulate, or stuck" without manually pasting numbers from two different sessions.

New services/calibration_history.py - due()/record_snapshot() deliberately split so the trading loop's per-tick hot path stays a single cheap MAX() query; the expensive part (a full signal_log.resolved_signals_with_factors() table scan + generate_calibration_report()) only runs when a snapshot is actually due - by default every confidence_calibration.snapshot_interval_sec (new config field, 6h). Costs zero API tokens, unlike the market analyst's LLM-gated button, so automatic background capture is fine here. New GET /api/confidence-calibration/history + a calibration_history Danger Zone reset flag. The Whale-Signal Calibration panel gains a history table, deliberately hidden until at least 2 snapshots exist - one point can't show a trend.

8 new tests, 609 passing. Verified live: the very first snapshot recorded automatically within one trading-loop tick of deploy (11,859 resolved signals, 52.6% overall win rate, full per-factor gap/weight breakdown) - confirmed via GET /api/confidence-calibration/history directly.

phase 84 · two strategies, one view

Cross-strategy comparison - whale-follow vs. market-native, joined for the first time

Gap 7 of docs/config-tuning-data-gaps-2026-08-10.md, and the user's own direct question this session ("comparing whale watch strategy historical data with the market-native strategy historical data"). The two strategies write to fully separate storage (paper_broker.db vs market_broker.db, own bankroll/risk state) and nothing had ever joined them - the only comparison available before this was eyeballing two separate History-tab summaries side by side.

New services/cross_strategy.py: aggregate_comparison() reuses trade_analytics.compute_summary() as-is for both strategies (can never silently drift from what each strategy's own summary already says), and ticker_overlap() finds every ticker where both strategies independently opened a position - two unrelated strategies, their own capital, their own gates, their own entry logic - reporting which side each took and whether they agreed. New GET /api/cross-strategy/comparison + a new "Cross-Strategy Comparison" History-tab panel.

phase 85 · when, not just what

Regime segmentation - does win rate actually hold up the same at every hour of the day?

Gap 9 of docs/config-tuning-data-gaps-2026-08-10.md, and the "time-of-day/liquidity-regime awareness" half of deep-scan Finding 7 (docs/platform-deep-scan-findings-2026-08-10.md) - every win-rate figure used anywhere in this app up to this point was a single global or per-series aggregate. A disclosed, partial implementation: new services/regime_analytics.py's by_hour_of_day()/by_day_of_week() both derive their bucket straight from entry_timestamp, a field every trade row already carries, and reuse trade_analytics.compute_summary() per bucket so results can't drift from what the aggregate summary already says. Category segmentation - the other half both the gaps doc and Finding 7 name - is explicitly NOT attempted: market_catalog.category is watchlist-scoped and rotates, so joining a historical trade back to its category at entry time would need a new category-snapshot persistence layer, not just an aggregation pass over data that already exists.

New GET /api/regime/by-hour/.../by-day-of-week + a new "Regime Segmentation" History-tab panel, whale-follow only (market-native's 22 closed positions are too thin to segment further without every bucket collapsing to n=0-1). 8 new tests, 624 passing. Verified live against real data - genuine variation across hours, not noise-flat: 18:00 UTC shows a 70.0% win rate over 20 closed positions, 20:00 UTC shows 23.1% over 13.

phase 86 · real math, not eyeballing

A shared, tested margin-of-error utility - the last of the 9 buildable config-tuning gaps

Gap 10 of docs/config-tuning-data-gaps-2026-08-10.md: every ad hoc sample-size threshold already scattered through this codebase (trade_analytics.confidence_label()'s 5/15, advisory.min_resolved_trades_per_variant's 10, confidence_calibration. min_resolved_signals's 50, series_evaluator.min_trades_observed's 1000) is a reasonable-looking round number, not something derived from a real power calculation - and this session's own per-series win-rate comparisons had to fall back to "eyeballing sqrt(p(1-p)/n) by hand." New services/stats_power.py: margin_of_error_pts() (the normal-approximation/Wald confidence-interval half-width, in percentage points, around an observed rate at a given n) and its inverse min_n_for_margin() - real, documented, unit-tested math available as one shared utility instead of re-derived ad hoc every time.

Deliberately doesn't rewrite any of the five existing ad hoc thresholds - each was chosen for its own local reason, and rewriting five independent gates in one pass would be a much bigger, riskier change than adding a documented convention. Instead: confidence_label()'s own docstring now states what its 5/15 cutoffs actually mean in real terms (roughly ±44 and ±25 points at a 50% base rate - both genuinely wide, exactly why "low"/"moderate" undersell rather than oversell what that size of sample supports), and one concrete consumer got wired: the Gap 4 series-evaluator win-rate cross-check now shows each series' real margin of error next to its win rate (e.g. "34.4% ±1.9pts" for KXATPCHALLENGERMATCH's 2326 resolved signals) - a series barely under the floor with a wide margin now visibly reads differently than one clearly under it with a tight one.

11 new tests, 635 passing. Verified live via curl (real margins on every series row, and confirmed the float("inf") edge case - n=0 - is structurally unreachable at this call site, so no literal Infinity ever risks breaking JSON.parse() client-side) and selenium-chrome. This closes all 9 buildable gaps from the config-tuning-data-gaps doc - Gap 5 (stop-loss calibration) was never on the build list, it needs time with the field live rather than tooling; Gap 2's stateful replay half was explicitly deferred within Gap 2 itself.

services/stats_power.py (new) · services/trade_analytics.py · main.py · static/index.html · tests/test_stats_power.py (new)
phase 87 · not stalled, silently obeying

The daily-loss kill switch never actually rolled over daily - three real, connected bugs found in one investigation

Triggered by a direct report: "the market-native strategy seems to have stalled." Root cause, confirmed live: services/risk_manager.py's reset_day() was only ever called manually (POST /api/reset, or a dashboard halt/resume click) - nothing rolled the daily-loss baseline over at a real day boundary, so once a kill switch tripped it stayed tripped indefinitely. market_strategy.py's own risk manager had tripped (-43.1%) and sat halted for 55+ hours with zero automatic recovery - it wasn't stalled, it was correctly, silently obeying a kill switch nothing had ever cleared. New day_start_date column; check_daily_loss() now runs an automatic rollover first (once per real UTC calendar-date change, not once per tick, and migration-safe - a pre-existing halt doesn't silently clear itself the instant this ships, only at a genuine subsequent day boundary) - every existing call site gets this for free with no new call needed anywhere.

Direct follow-up from the user ("i think the same or similar problem is happening in shadow mode") led straight to a second, independently-confirmed instance of the identical bug: services/shadow_mode.py's own daily-loss tracker (a deliberately separate implementation, not a RiskManager reuse) was also stuck halted (-99.7%), dormant only because mode was paper at the time - the moment mode ever flips to shadow/live this would have silently blocked every shadow evaluation with no recovery path. Fixed the same way, plus a new resume() method. New POST /api/market-risk/halt/.../resume and POST /api/shadow-risk/resume (neither had any route before this - market-native's kill switch could only ever be managed by hand-editing data/market_risk_state.db), plus a new market_native POST /api/reset flag. All three trackers manually un-halted live as part of this fix (non-destructive - resume() doesn't touch bankroll or trade history); market-native confirmed evaluating real candidates again within a minute of being un-halted.

Separately found and fixed while investigating (direct report: "not seeing the results of the positions in the trade log"): the Portfolio Trade Log, both Simple and Advanced views, never showed a closed position's real outcome - every row rendered identically whether still open or already settled, showing "cost to enter"/"payout if right" even for an already-won-or-lost trade, and the Advanced table's P&L column ran a mark-to-market formula against a close row's price that was meaningless once that trade had already settled. New main.py _enrich_recent_trades() re-derives close_type/realized_pnl/won via the existing trade_analytics.build_trade_history() over the *full* trade log (not just the displayed tail-25, so an entry outside that window still pairs correctly with a close inside it) and merges the real fields onto both brokers' recent_trades - the frontend now shows a real ✅/❌ Won/Lost badge with the actual realized P&L and close-type label per closed row.

14 new tests, 649 passing. Also cleaned up 9 stray TICK-A test-fixture rows a pre-isolation-fixture pytest run had written into the real data/candidate_log.db earlier this same session (found while diagnosing market-native's candidate-log activity) - a live instance of exactly the "test touches real data" bug class this project has hit and fixed before, caught and cleaned up the same session it happened.

phase 88 · live, not frozen at tab-open

History tab panels now keep refreshing while the tab stays open

Direct report: "the advisory recommendations seem out-of-date, and should update." Root cause: loadTradingHistory() was deliberately called only once, on tab-open - a protective measure so the paginated Trading History table's own paging/sort state wouldn't reset out from under someone actively browsing it on every 5s poll. But that same one-shot load covered *every* panel on the tab, not just the paginated table - Advisory Recommendations, Change History, Whale-Signal Calibration (report + history), Cross-Strategy Comparison, Regime Segmentation, Rejected Candidates, Backtest Sweeps, Series Evaluator, and Market Analyst all inherited the same staleness despite none of them having any pagination or sort state of their own to protect.

New refreshHistoryInsightsIfActive(), called from the existing refresh() 5s poll cycle (guarded on currentView === 'history', a new tracker showView() now maintains) - re-fetches just those nine panels, leaving loadTradingHistory()'s own paginated fetch completely untouched. Verified live two ways: intercepted window.fetch and confirmed /api/advisory/recommendations now refetches every poll cycle while the tab is open (4 calls over ~11s, was 1 before this fix), and confirmed paging forward (historyFilter.offset = 25) survives an 11s wait completely unchanged - the original regression this fix had to avoid reintroducing.

phase 90 · auto-apply, for real this time

A broken auto-apply feature found and fixed, plus a real one built for calibration

Direct request: "i want the option to enable auto whale-signal calibration... have them auto-enable and start getting put into play with my whole system once there *is* enough data." Investigating the existing analogous mechanism to mirror turned up a real, previously-undiscovered bug: advisory.auto_apply_enabled was already a protected config field, with an error message pointing at POST /api/advisory/auto-apply/enable/.../disable - but those routes never existed, and grepping the whole codebase found zero uses of auto_apply_min_confidence/auto_apply_cooldown_sec anywhere outside the config schema itself. The feature was reachable from no path at all - not the generic config route (explicitly blocked) and not the dedicated one (didn't exist).

Fixed both, consistently: new POST /api/advisory/auto-apply/enable/.../disable and the equivalent POST /api/confidence-calibration/auto-apply/enable/.../disable, both gated behind the same typed-confirmation-phrase pattern real trading already established (asymmetric - disabling needs no phrase). New services/confidence_calibration.blended_weights_for_auto_apply() automates the exact blend a human did by hand earlier this same session - factors with real discrimination data get the suggested value, factors with none stay completely untouched, the whole set renormalizes to sum to 1.0. This deliberately reverses that module's own earlier-documented "read-only, a human must apply this by hand" decision, at direct request, keeping the same safety rails (opt-in, confirmation-gated, cooldown, gated behind the existing sample-size floor) rather than removing them. New config_performance.last_applied_at(source) gives both auto-apply blocks a real cooldown check, reusing applied_changes' own timestamps instead of a second parallel tracker. Both wired into the trading loop - advisory applies only the single highest-priority recommendation clearing auto_apply_min_confidence per cooldown window, never a burst of every qualifying one at once.

14 new tests, 656 passing. Verified live end-to-end, not just unit-tested: enabled calibration auto-apply through the real route (wrong phrase rejected, correct phrase accepted), then forced an immediate cycle by briefly dropping snapshot_interval_sec to 1s - confirmed a real calibration-auto-apply-sourced row landed in the audit trail, whale_confidence_weights updated to real, correctly-renormalized values, and the trading loop kept running with zero tick errors. Restored snapshot_interval_sec afterward. Left calibration auto-apply enabled live (directly requested, already well past its data-sample gate) but left advisory auto-apply at its default off - fixing a broken mechanism isn't the same as being asked to turn it on, and it's a materially broader blast radius than one well-scoped config section.

Category segmentation - the persistence layer the original regime work had to defer

Direct follow-up request: "add those things, and have them auto-enable and start getting put into play with my whole system once there *is* enough data" - referring to the gaps this session's own analysis (docs/config-tuning-data-gaps-2026-08-10.md) had explicitly deferred. Recorded once per position open, not close, since the category a market belongs to doesn't change over a hold. Older trades are excluded, not backfilled with a guess. 28 new tests, 668 passing. phase 94 · say what "yes" means

Market/position labeling: degenerate sub-titles, a scope gap, and one-line-cramming

Three direct reports, same root theme: "Cleveland vs Detroit Winner? Root cause 1, a real Kalshi API data quirk rather than a caching bug - confirmed directly against KalshiClient.get_market() that for many simple 2-way matchup markets, Kalshi's own yes_sub_title/no_sub_title come back identical (both literally say the same team name). marketContext()/marketCardHTML() now detect that degenerate case and fall back to an honest "not {yes_sub_title}" label for the NO side instead of asserting a specific opposing name Kalshi never actually gave this app.

Root cause 2: main.py's _relevant_tickers() only ever scoped the whale-follow broker's positions/trade log for title-resolution data, never market_broker's - so GET /api/market-strategy/state's own lookups were scoped wrong for its own strategy's tickers. For the width complaint: rather than a full shared-resolver migration, added a smaller-font "Betting: {what this side means}" second line below the market name (contextLineHTML() or an inline equivalent) everywhere a ticker+side is shown - Signal History, Possible Accumulation, the live Signal Feed, Trade Tape, the Portfolio Trade Log and Decision Feed table, real-account Positions/Fills/ Orders (Simple and Advanced), Trading History, Market-Native positions/trades/decisions, Shadow Mode's trade log, the Market Analyst's track-record/single-analysis panels, and the market-detail modal's Recent Trades. .decision-row had never actually been styled (only .position-row/ .trade-row were) - added to the shared row rule. Deliberately left the Advanced screener table's dense multi-column layout alone - a different, already-settled design boundary (phase 62), not an oversight.

phase 95 · trust, but verify

Auto-apply hardening: confidence gating, sample-size floors, staleness, and a display bug

Four direct reports acted on together. First: "the apply button should only appear next to config change options the system agrees with" — the Advisory Recommendations panel now hides its manual "Apply to config" button for any suggestion whose own confidence_label is 'low' (n<5) - the card still renders (nothing hidden), just without a one-click way to act on something the rule engine itself is hedging on. 'moderate'/'higher' recommendations keep the button unchanged.

Second: "auto apply should wait for a significant dataset... and predict how those changes may improve (or worsen) before applying." Both auto-apply paths already had a floor (the calibration report's own min_resolved_signals, the advisory confidence-tier check) - but that was the bar for showing a suggestion to a human, not a strong enough bar for writing to config with nobody reviewing first. New, separately-tunable, Config-tab-editable floors specifically for the unsupervised path: advisory.auto_apply_min_n (default 25, stacked on top of the existing confidence-tier check) and confidence_calibration.auto_apply_min_resolved_signals (default 150, vs. the report's own 50-signal display floor) - three times stricter, a deliberate margin. Neither touches manual Apply clicks. For "predict the effect": calibration auto-apply's logged rationale now names the specific calibration gap (factor + points) the reweighting was derived to close, rather than fabricating a forward win-rate number this app has no way to honestly back before the new weights have scored a single real signal - consistent with this app's existing "don't show a number you can't back" practice (shadow mode, the market analyst's own hedged track record).

Third: "make sure the suggested values arent stale." A real, previously-unguarded gap: unlike the rule-based Advisory apply route (which recomputes its recommendation fresh on every apply call and already 404s if the content-addressed id no longer matches), the series-analyst and full-spectrum-analyst apply routes trusted a suggestion's analysis-time current_value at face value - if the live config had moved since analysis (a manual edit, a second analysis, another auto-apply), applying the old suggestion would silently overwrite based on a premise that was no longer true, and the audit trail would log a fabricated "before" value that was never actually live at apply time. New _config_value_at_path() reads the field's real current value at apply time; a mismatch now 409s with a message naming the actual live value and asking for a re-analysis, on both routes.

Fourth: "the config change log shows [Object object]." Confirmed against a real live row - calibration auto-apply replaces the entire whale_confidence_weights dict in one shot, and the Change History panel's String(value) call on an object just invokes its default toString(), producing the literal string "[object Object]". New shared formatConfigValue() (JSON.stringify for objects/arrays, plain string otherwise, "—" for null) replaces every bare String(value)/JSON.stringify(value) call across Change History, Advisory Recommendations, and the series/full-spectrum suggestion cards - one formatter, used everywhere a config value renders. (Change History's list length was already capped via the existing ?limit=20 fetch call - confirmed unchanged, not a new fix.)

Also closed a real, unrelated gap surfaced while adding the new floors: confidence_calibration.* had zero Config-tab presence at all despite being a live, currently-auto-applying feature - new "Whale-Signal Calibration" accordion section (enabled toggle, both sample-size fields) alongside one new field on the existing Advisory Engine section; that section's own intro text, which still said auto-apply "is not wired up yet on purpose," was rewritten to match reality.

672 tests passing (was 668). Verified live via curl (a real pre-existing calibration-auto-apply row's dict value confirmed rendering as readable JSON instead of [object Object]) and selenium-chrome (both new Config-tab fields render correctly with real values, zero new console errors).

phase 96 · one series, one section

Watchlist series-grouping fix, plus two reports investigated and cleared

Direct report: "the watchlist groupings is broken" - and a follow-up diagnosis from the user that turned out to be exactly right: "likely a result of the active removal of watchlist items. reorganization should occur at the same time the watchlist updates."

Root cause, confirmed in main.py's _fetch_markets(): an open position that rotated off round_robin_select()'s own selection is kept alive via the existing extra_tickers mechanism (phase 59-61) - but it was appended to the *end* of the markets list regardless of series. static/index.html's renderMarketCards() assumes same-series markets are always consecutive (true of round_robin_select()'s own output, which groups per-series internally, but not of the post-append result) - when a series had one member survive via extra_tickers while others were still present earlier in the list, that one series rendered as two separate, non-adjacent on-screen sections instead of merging into one.

Fixed on both sides. Backend: _fetch_markets() now re-groups the full list by series after the extra_tickers append, preserving each series' first-occurrence order (a stable group-by, not an alphabetical sort, which would have destroyed round_robin_select()'s volume-priority ordering) - the same fix also now covers the manually-pinned kalshi.markets_watchlist branch, whose ticker order is whatever the user typed and was never guaranteed grouped at all. Frontend: renderMarketCards()'s seriesRuns builder switched from an adjacent-only linear scan to a Map keyed by series, merging any same-key ticker into its existing run wherever it lands in the array - belt-and-suspenders on top of the backend fix, not a replacement for it.

1 new regression test - a pinned watchlist with two series where one series' second ticker only arrives via extra_tickers, asserting the final order keeps both same-series tickers adjacent. 673 tests passing (was 672). Verified live via selenium-chrome: queried the real DOM (details.series-section[data-series]) against the actual live watchlist and confirmed every series key appeared exactly once, zero duplicates; visually confirmed "Professional Baseball Game" now renders as one consolidated 87-market section. Zero console errors.

Two more reports investigated in the same pass and found not to be code bugs: "price fluctuations arent showing" and "very few whale prints for baseball despite a low $500 threshold." Direct querying of signal_log showed 302 real MLB whale signals logged in a single recent 6-hour window, all above threshold, across many different games - detection is robust. The live Signal Feed panel's existing 50-item cap, shared across every concurrently-active sport (tennis alone was producing nearly as much volume as baseball at the time), is the more likely source of the "few prints" impression, not under-detection. The price-update and live-badge mechanisms both checked out correctly end-to-end (a real Kalshi live-data call confirmed a genuinely-finished game correctly resolving to "finished," and two genuinely in-progress games correctly resolving to "live") once the trading loop was running undisturbed - no code change made for either, since nothing was actually broken.

main.py · static/index.html · tests/test_trading_gate.py
phase 97 · the cap that was hiding a hang

Trade-tape overhaul, a real production-shaped incident, and fixing it properly

Started as a follow-on to phase 96's whale-print investigation. Direct report: "theres a lot of baseball games... i saw very few whale prints... there MUST have been more than 20." Tracing the pipeline found a real structural gap: main.py's _fetch_trade_tape() capped the platform-wide trade tape at 100 items total (_TRADE_TAPE_TOTAL_CAP) and fetched only the last 10 trades per watched ticker with no min_ts/cursor - both silently dropped real trades before whale-signal detection ever evaluated them, independent of anything threshold-related. Confirmed live: the 100-item cap was filling up within ~2 minutes under real load.

Direct instruction: "i want trade tape to be unlimited, never capped, for whale-watch-worthy markets" (confirmed to include series still mid-evaluation, not just series_evaluator-approved ones - already true, since ineligible_series() only ever excludes series serving backoff). KalshiClient.get_trades() gained real min_ts/cursor params - SDK-confirmed, already supported by Kalshi's API, never wired up. Shipping this exposed a genuinely dangerous cold-start bug, caught before it hit production: with no watermark yet, unbounded cursor pagination has no natural stopping point short of a ticker's *entire trade history* - every watched ticker would page up to the hard safety ceiling, simultaneously, on every single restart.

The fix that shipped anyway (before the cold-start guard was added) caused a real, live second incident. Removing the 100-item cap multiplied real per-tick trade volume 10-30x, and two existing functions on that exact hot path - series_evaluator.record_trade_observed() and candidate_log.record_rejection() - each open a brand-new SQLite connection (with a CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS check) per individual raw trade. Thousands of those in one tick, each a synchronous blocking call, froze Python's single-threaded asyncio event loop for several minutes - confirmed via nginx logging "upstream timed out" on /api/state, and an internal request timing out against localhost:8000 from inside the very same container. Not a network/rate-limit issue, a request-handling process that could no longer do anything else at all while it worked through the backlog.

Raised the question directly: given this, should the app move off SQLite entirely to a real DB server? Answered with AskUserQuestion rather than unilaterally either rewriting the persistence layer or dismissing the concern - the actual bug was blocking I/O running on an async event loop, not a SQLite capacity ceiling; a DB server wouldn't have prevented the identical mistake. Recommendation (finish hardening SQLite properly) confirmed. Three-part fix: (1) _fetch_trade_tape() now pages every ticker to true completion via a new _fetch_trades_for_ticker(), stopping only on Kalshi's own empty-cursor signal - except when there's no watermark yet (the very first tick after any restart), where it deliberately fetches one page and stops; a new state["trade_tape_last_fetch_ts"] watermark makes every subsequent tick a small, bounded incremental fetch instead. The Trade Tape UI panel keeps its own 100-item display cap, now fully decoupled from whale detection's input, which is genuinely uncapped. (2) kalshi_trade_tape.fetch_signals() restructured so its entire per-trade loop - every blocking call it makes, not just the two above - runs inside a new synchronous _process_trades_sync() dispatched via asyncio.to_thread(), so no volume of real trade data can ever again block request handling. series_evaluator. record_trades_observed_bulk() collapses what used to be one SQLite connection per trade into one per tick (counts pre-aggregated per series in Python first). (3) PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL added to the shared _connect() idiom across all 14 services/*.py modules that use it - concurrent readers no longer block behind a writer, the standard hardening step for exactly this bursty-write shape.

A real editing mistake happened during the WAL-mode rollout and was caught before any of it reached the live server: a scripted fix had an unescaped \3 inside a non-raw Python string literal, which Python's own parser silently converted to the octal escape \x03 (a literal control character) before the string ever reached the regex engine - deleting a real line of code from all 14 files. Caught immediately via ast.parse failing on every one of them; fixed with a corrected script and reverified clean before touching the running app again.

15 new tests: cursor pagination completing on Kalshi's own empty-cursor signal, cold-start deliberately not paginating, the min_ts overlap-margin math, record_trades_observed_ bulk()'s full state-transition matrix (fresh row / increment / approved-noop / backoff-noop / reactivation crediting the whole batch / empty-dict-noop), and a WAL-mode-enabled spot check on two of the fourteen patched modules. 687 tests passing (was 672). Verified live end-to-end after a full restart: the first cold-start tick (513 markets) took roughly 74 seconds, but the app stayed fully responsive - fast HTTP replies throughout - the entire time, which is the actual structural fix, not just "it didn't crash this time." Confirmed via selenium-chrome against the real running app that the market-detail modal still opens quickly with accurate pricing, Recent Trades, and sibling outcomes. One transient 502 was observed on a single per-market trades call shortly after the restart - confirmed as residual Kalshi-side rate-limit pressure from the cold-start burst (exhausting call_with_backoff's retries), not a code bug, by immediately retrying successfully.
