phase 98 · a web of expertise, wired for real

Six cross-engine gaps closed, plus a real "apply does nothing" bug

Direct instruction after phase 97's incident and the resulting hardening doc: "keep in mind that all of the analyzers, engines, heuristics, should have the potential to inform each other, like a web of expertise" - followed by a request to write a comprehensive hardening/accuracy to-do list (docs/hardening-and-accuracy-roadmap-2026-08-11.md), then "fill these cross engine gaps so they actually work, make sure nothing is broken particularly the whale watcher terminal." Every one of the doc's 6 claimed gaps was re-verified against the actual code via grep before being trusted, not assumed from the doc alone.

Item 6 - candidate_log/regime_analytics reaching the full-spectrum LLM scan: _build_full_spectrum_context() in main.py now includes rejected_candidate_gates (candidate_log.gate_summary()) and regime_by_category/regime_by_hour (regime_analytics.by_category/ by_hour_of_day). Item 1 - rejected-candidate counterfactuals reaching advisory_engine: new _rejected_candidate_recommendations(), a (strategy, gate_name) -> (config_path, direction) map covering 9 real gates, suggests loosening a threshold once ≥5 rejected candidates would have won. Item 4 - series_evaluator verdicts reaching advisory_engine: new _series_evaluator_recommendations() suggests adding a series to strategy.excluded_series when its real win rate sits below the floor with enough sample size - confirmed live against a real KXMLBGAME exclusion suggestion.

Item 2 - category-conditional tuning: new strategy.entry_threshold_by_category override dict, mirroring the existing min_notional_usd_by_series precedent exactly. FollowTheWhaleStrategy.evaluate() gained an optional category parameter (backward compatible - default None changes nothing for any existing caller), read live in the trading loop from state["event_titles"]; _category_conditional_recommendations() suggests per-category overrides off ≥5-sample category win-rate gaps. Item 3 (the harder half - using regime_analytics to actually gate live entries, not just inform suggestions) was deliberately scoped out of this pass and left open, disclosed rather than silently dropped - the direct instruction to make sure nothing broke, especially the whale watcher, argued against risking a second live-trading-path change in the same pass as Item 2's.

Item 5 - calibration parity for market_strategy's own confidence score: new services/market_strategy_calibration.py, deliberately scoped smaller than the whale-side confidence_calibration.py and disclosed as such in its own docstring - confidence-band calibration only, not per-factor discrimination, since market_strategy has no per-candidate factor log like signal_log.factors_json, only a placed trade's blended score (via trade_analytics.build_trade_history()'s entry_confidence field). New market_strategy_calibration.{enabled, min_resolved_trades} config, GET /api/market-strategy-calibration/status/.../report. First live run against real data (n=80) surfaced a genuine finding: every confidence band's observed win rate sits far below its predicted midpoint (90-100% predicted 95%, observed 26.7%) - market_strategy's composite confidence score is currently a poor predictor of its own outcomes. Flagged, not auto-corrected - that's this tool's job.

Separate direct bug report mid-arc, treated as a stop-the-line fix: "if i click apply it just gives me the same evaluation and same potential increase value... suggesting a massive bug." Root cause: every advisory suggestion function recomputed from the full trade history on every call, with no awareness a config_path had just been changed - clicking Apply repeatedly with no new trades in between kept re-suggesting the identical nudge off the same stale evidence. Fixed with a new _drop_stale_recommendations(), reusing the existing entry_timestamp/ applied_at before/after convention change_effect() already established rather than inventing a new one - a new config_performance.all_last_applied_by_path() lets the filter drop any suggestion whose config_path was changed more recently than the newest trade behind it.

Verified live end-to-end: curl against GET /api/advisory/recommendations showing real rejected-candidate and series-evaluator suggestions mixed into the same pool as the pre-existing suggestion types, and GET /api/market-strategy-calibration/report returning the real n=80 finding above. Per the direct instruction to make sure nothing broke, particularly the whale watcher terminal: full regression suite green, and a live selenium-chrome pass against the running Whale Watch tab specifically confirmed 20 real market cards, whale-signal breakdowns, and a populated trade tape all still rendering correctly - the phase 97 trade-tape/SQLite hardening was undisturbed by this pass.

phase 99 · the book, not the bet

Position netting for hedge-mode, and per-series config tuning

Real trade-history review (the same pass behind the unit_cost price-band fix) found a second, distinct pattern: 64 historical events where FollowTheWhaleStrategy had independently opened positions on BOTH sides of the same real-world 2-outcome matchup (e.g. YES on one NFL team and YES on the other) - the user's own named "hedge mode." Two live examples: a UFC pair doubled up on one fighter winning via two different tickers, and a 51-position PGA tournament concentration (43.4% of the whale-follow bankroll). Direct correction after an initial fix attempt: the mutually-exclusive complement gate (phase 96-adjacent work) only blocks a new entry into a confirmed pair - it does nothing for positions already open, partial hedges, or N-way concentration, and needed to be "fully researched and robust," not assumed solved.

New services/position_netting.py, grounded directly in docs/prediction-markets-research-reference.md's own research: Kalshi's CLOB doesn't mechanically enforce sum-to-100% across a mutually-exclusive event's sibling markets (only arbitraged - a cited 2026 arXiv preprint documents real violations), and the real fill-verified fee curve is parabolic, worst exactly at 50c. Computes the exact payout profile of any confirmed mutually-exclusive-event group (2-way or true N-way, generalizing the earlier pairwise-only detector) under every possible outcome - "locked profit" (provably optimal to hold, since settlement itself is fee-free), "locked loss" (the loss is already fixed regardless of timing), or "still variable" (the real case - evaluated against a small, fee-aware, volatility-scaled candidate set: hold, trim the worst leg, or close everything, only acting past a materiality bar). Deliberately does not include an "add to the best leg" action yet - the highest- risk direction, left for a validated follow-up. Off by default; a new read-only GET /api/position-netting/groups and a Portfolio-tab panel make every currently-open group's real classification visible before the automated action is ever turned on. Live-verified against the real UFC and PGA groups above before shipping.

Same session, the other half of the request ("these strategies need to be able to be tweaked for individual series... figure out the best values yourself"): new services/config_overrides.py, a generic per-category/per-series resolver for any strategy.*/market_strategy.* field (category layer applied first, series wins), replacing and subsuming phase 98's single-field strategy.entry_threshold_by_category entirely - no data lost, that field was empty. Wired into both strategies' entry AND exit paths, resolved per-position inside check_exits since different open positions can belong to different series. MarketNativeStrategy got the same mechanical wiring for symmetry, with zero override values ever populated, per the standing instruction that it's a control group, not a strategy to tune.

Real values were populated from 606 closed trades, but a first pass (raw per-series/per-unit_cost-bucket dollar totals) was explicitly challenged mid-session: "while N=X is useful, I need sigma scores as well." Re-run with a real one-sample t-test (mean pnl vs. zero) and a proportion z-score (win rate vs. the book's 68.5% overall rate) - several plausible-looking findings did not survive the check (a series that looked net-negative in every price bucket had an aggregate t-stat of only -0.98, within noise) and were deliberately left unshipped, documented in config/settings.yaml rather than silently dropped. Three overrides cleared a real bar on both an aggregate and a bucket-level test: a much shorter cooldown plus a higher entry threshold for the 15-minute BTC series (whose own fifteen-minute lifecycle independently argues for the cooldown change), and a modest position-size increase for two series with the strongest positive significance in the whole dataset.

810 tests passing. Live-verified via selenium-chrome against the real running Portfolio tab, and via direct API calls against the real open UFC/PGA positions.

The entire MLB proposition family was overcharged 2x in fees

Direct request mid-session, after mentioning Kalshi's real per-series API documentation: "consume samples of the category endpoints to get a better picture of each market." Live-sampled Kalshi's real GET /series endpoint (not just the bulk list this app already used) across all 13,029 real series. Found the phase-97-era fee model - a 10-series zero-fee list sourced from a static PDF - had two real gaps: 4 more genuinely zero-fee series the PDF pass missed (KXEXPAND, KXNEXTIRANLEADER, KXTRUMPOUT, KXGDPYEAR), and, previously entirely unmodeled, a real 0.5x fee multiplier on the ENTIRE MLB proposition-market family (spread, total, outs, HR, hits, F5/F3/F7 innings, RBI, stolen bases, strikeouts, ...) - one of this app's most actively-traded series families. Every MLB trade before this fix had been charged double the real fee.

phase 101 · maker orders and a floor above the average

Four real bugs, a global gate that punished good series, and a from-scratch maker-order path

Direct request to re-read the handoff doc, assess the whole app for logic holes/gaps/quirks/bugs, and produce a profit-maximization plan given the real 68.4% win rate - then, same session, "fix the bugs, act on the data-driven recommendations, implement new analyzers if needed." Four confirmed bugs, all fixed and tested: (1) strategy.kelly_fraction_of_cap: null crashed kelly_scaled_max_size() (None <= 0 TypeError) - null is this app's own established "disabled" convention for sibling fields, but this function had no guard for it, and a crash here also skipped that tick's check_exits/position_netting.review since all three share one try block in main.py's loop. Fixed with a real None-guard inside the function itself, not just at the call sites. (2) The real-account (not paper) header's "Change (session)" figure diffed current portfolio_value (cash+positions) against a cash-only historical baseline - main.py now persists portfolio_value history alongside balance, and renderHeaderStrip diffs like-for-like. (3) A latent, currently- unreachable no-side fallback in two spots in static/index.html (p.cost_basis ?? (p.size * p.entry_price)) - dropped entirely, since PaperBroker.state() already guarantees cost_basis is always present. (4) The advisory engine's rejected-candidate comparability check turned out to be a systemic pattern, not one call site: the same flat 15-point tolerance with no reference to sample size (_COMPARABLE_MIN_WIN_RATE_GAP) was reused across six different suggestion generators in services/advisory_engine.py. All six now go through a new _comparability_margin_pts() helper built on services/stats_power.py's existing real margin-of-error math - the same rigor this app's own sigma-vetted per-series analysis already uses, not a second, weaker standard living alongside it.

Three data-driven config changes applied via POST /api/config (real audit trail each time), with two real corrections along the way. strategy.min_whale_winrate_pct: reverted 76.5→85 first (the z-test above showed today's applied 76.5 change rested on evidence that was genuinely borderline, not the clean violation it first looked like) - then, later the same session, dropped much further to 50 after a live-reported symptom ("why is the system avoiding crypto markets with super high whale winrates") led straight to a real design flaw: this field is a flat global floor (strategy_engine.py's entry gate compares every series' own win rate against it), and at 85% it was flagging KXBTCD - 80.5% win rate, a proven top performer already sized up via its own strategy_overrides entry - as "avoiding," simply because a flat floor set above the book's own 68.4% average is guaranteed to reject roughly half of all series by construction. 50 stays comfortably below every currently-tracked series while still catching genuinely broken ones; the surgical, single-series cases stay the job of strategy_overrides.by_series, not the blunt global gate. strategy.take_profit_pct: enabled at 0.2, corrected down from an originally-considered ~0.5-0.95 once closer inspection found all 28 historical take_profit trades were priced outside the current 0.5-0.8 unit_cost band - that track record doesn't transfer to what the strategy can even trade today. strategy.kelly_fraction_of_cap: raised to 0.3 (market_strategy.* left at 0, control group untouched).

New: a full maker/limit-order path for the paper book, not just a scoping note - fees were consuming ~60% of gross profit on the whale-follow book, and this app had no maker-order simulation at all before now. services/kalshi_fees.py gained maker_fee() (1/4 the taker rate, same formula/rounding/per-series-multiplier convention). services/paper_broker.py gained a new PendingOrder dataclass, its own pending_orders SQLite table (same persistence idiom as positions/trades - survives a restart), place_limit_order(), and check_pending_fills() (side-aware fill check against a tick's real bid/ask, fills at the real available price when it's better than the limit, never worse; an order past its own expiry cancels unfilled rather than chasing a stale price as a market order). main.py gained a genuinely- missing-when-absent state["latest_asks"] (never defaulted, since guessing an ask would mean guessing whether a resting order should fill) and wired check_pending_fills into the tick loop right after signal handling. services/strategy_engine.py gained opt-in strategy.use_limit_orders/limit_order_timeout_sec - off by default, same "ships fully built, opt-in" precedent as every other risk-adjacent mechanism in this app. Deliberately not extended to the real-account order path this pass (real trading stays gated behind trading_enabled regardless), and no dashboard panel yet for the two new fields.

Two background diagnostics resolved open questions from the original assessment. market_native's apparent 27σ min_momentum_delta inversion turned out to be a confound, not a backwards thesis: the comparison mixed eventual-settlement-match win rate against realized-P&L win rate, where stop_loss mechanically scores 0% by construction (54.4% of that whole book closes that way). Recomputed on the same basis, the real gap is 10.1pts/z=5.12 - real, far smaller. A genuine side-finding surfaced along the way: 44.9% of market_native's stop-lossed trades would have gone on to win - not acted on, control group. Separately, depth_factor's failure to discriminate now has a confirmed root cause for KXBTC15M specifically: Kalshi's own volume_24h_fp field jumps in large discrete steps unrelated to that 15-minute contract's own trading (one ticker observed jumping 25x in 65 seconds) rather than accumulating like real per-contract volume - its current 0.03 live weight (vs. a 0.18 shipped default) stands confirmed-correct. Finally, direct request to re-run the full sigma-vetted series/category t-stat/z-score methodology against the fresh 607-trade book: honest conclusion, no new strategy_overrides entry is currently warranted - all three existing ones (KXBTC15M, KXMLBSPREAD, KXBTCD) reconfirm at essentially unchanged strength, KXMLBGAME's bimodal pattern reconfirms real but still correctly unshippable (no unit_cost-bucket-scoped override tier exists in config_overrides.py), and by_category should stay empty since Crypto's near-significant blended signal is fully explained by, and would actively conflict with, the two already- correct oppositely-signed series underneath it.

17 new tests, 827 passing (was 810). Verified live throughout: every config change confirmed via its own config_performance audit-trail row, GET /api/state checked clean (no tick errors) after each round of edits, persistence of the new pending_orders table confirmed surviving an in-process --reload restart triggered naturally by the session's own edits.

phase 103 · the rate limit was never a concurrency problem

Real incident: signal-resolution stall, a wrong rate-limit model, and a full API-usage audit

Direct live report: "why are there SO MANY unresolved signals???" Root cause: signal_log.unresolved_batch() was pure head-of-line blocking — ORDER BY seen_at ASC LIMIT N with no memory of ever having tried a row. A handful of genuinely long-horizon signals (a market closing in 2027, a Fed decision weeks out) sorted to the front of every batch forever, since nothing ever resolved them — confirmed live: 31,833 of 32,101 logged signals unresolved, 31,469 of those over 24h old. Fixed with a last_checked_at cooldown column so the query actually progresses through the backlog. That fix made _check_signal_resolutions do real, sustained work for the first time — running concurrently with discovery in the same tick pushed tick_duration/ rate_limit_hits to ~40s/200+, repeatedly. A concurrency Semaphore (the same fix pattern used earlier for get_candidate_markets) did not help — confirmed live, still spiking with Semaphore(5). Reading Kalshi's actual rate-limit docs (docs.kalshi.com/getting_started/rate_limits, at direct instruction rather than continuing to guess) found the real cause: it's a token-bucket throughput system (tokens/sec), not concurrency-based at all — a concurrency cap bounds simultaneous requests but does nothing to slow the rate new ones fire. Replaced with a real token-bucket limiter, split into independent read/write buckets matching Kalshi's own architecture (real order placement/cancel now correctly routes to the more conservative write bucket). Fixing the rate limit correctly then exposed a third, bigger problem: discovery's per-refresh get_candidate_markets fetch (12-120 series depending on config) now took proportionally longer under a correctly-conservative limit, blocking the whole tick loop every time a refresh was due — direct report: "you made the market watch list and whale watching grind to a halt and markets aren't even appearing anymore," confirmed live (watchlist collapsed to 7 markets). Direct instruction: "review the api documentation, implement best and recommended practices, and do a full audit of the api usage for the application. no stone unturned." Fixed at the architecture level: discovery, catalog-scanning, and signal-resolution are now independent background tasks (their own cadence, an overlap guard, never awaited by the tick loop); services/market_catalog.py gained open_candidates() (a pure SQLite read) and discovery now reads from it instead of a fresh REST fetch — zero network calls once the catalog is warm; _scan_catalog_batch is no longer gated behind the unused live_markets_only flag (it turned out to already have 4,160+ series scanned from an earlier period, just sitting unused); _fetch_account_snapshot (balance/positions/ fills) was found making 3 uncached REST calls every single tick, now interval-cached. Verified live: markets recovered from the crisis low of 7 to a stable ~32, zero rate-limit errors throughout. Real open item, not yet root-caused: tick_duration stabilized around ~27s, not the pre-incident ~3s baseline (leading hypothesis — uncached open-position tickers — checked directly and ruled out). A WS-based real-position-feed (direct request: "the open positions should feed from the websocket stream") was deliberately deferred rather than shipped unverified — fill events cannot occur while trading_enabled is off, so there is no live message to confirm parsing against; see docs/next-steps-2026-08-15-pt2.md for the three ways forward. 899 tests passing.

phase 104 · four background loops, one starved rate limiter

tick_duration root-caused: not one bug, four independent uncapped call sites sharing a budget

Picks up phase 103's own open item. Rather than keep guessing, instrumented the tick loop directly (temporary per-phase timing prints, removed once done) and let it run live. Found four separate, real problems, each contributing: propagate_milestone_winners called get_milestones_for_event() for every event on the watchlist, every tick, forever, unconditionally — most events (crypto, politics, ...) never have a milestone at all, so this was pure waste; _fetch_event_live_data called get_event_live_data() the same way — confirmed live, every event on the real watchlist 404s from that endpoint (it turned out to be the wrong endpoint for sports entirely, see phase 105); _fetch_live_status's per-tick poll batch had no cap at all, so a large fraction of the in-window candidate pool (measured up to 2,549 rows) becoming simultaneously due for its 5-minute repoll fired dozens-to-hundreds of concurrent calls in one tick, measured up to 48.78s alone; and _scan_catalog_batch's background task (40 series/batch) shared the exact same rate limiter as the tick's own reads — running as a background task decouples control flow, not resource contention, and with the overlap guard letting a new batch start the moment the previous one finished draining, it ran back-to-back continuously, confirmed starving _fetch_account_snapshot (19-33s stalls measured on affected ticks despite its own already-correct 20s cache). Fixed all four: repoll caches for the first two (state["milestone_cache"]/state["event_live_data_cache"], 60s), a bounded oldest-checked-first batch cap for the third (_LIVE_STATUS_MAX_POLL_PER_TICK = 10, same shape _check_signal_resolutions already used), and a straight batch-size cut for the fourth (_CATALOG_SCAN_BATCH_SIZE 40→10). Verified live, repeatedly: tick_duration dropped from a stable ~24-33s plateau to mostly 1-5s, occasional spikes to ~14s from residual background contention. Zero rate-limit errors throughout. 917 tests passing.

phase 106 · put the audit into practice

Phase 105's flagged batching, category routing, and rate limit applied — plus a real signal-resolution backlog found along the way

Direct instruction: "in an earlier session you mentioned how the api docs gave you a gold mine on how to redesign, reapproach api calls, shapes, analyzers, engines, everything. put the knowledge into practice and fix the backend." New batched KalshiClient methods (get_events, get_live_datas, get_milestones_bulk, get_markets_by_tickers) replaced individual-call gathers at every real call site — event-title fetching, live-status confirmation, milestone-winner propagation. Category routing applied: _fetch_event_live_data now skips Sports outright (confirmed 100%-404) instead of calling it every category. Real game-state surfacing: state["live_game_state"] — score/clock/quarter/down-distance data phase 105 found was already being fetched via the milestone-based calls but discarded. Read-rate limiter raised 3.0→8.0 req/sec, reasoned headroom below the confirmed 20 req/sec account ceiling rather than the full jump, since most of this app's read traffic is unauthenticated market data, not the authenticated account calls that ceiling was measured against. The biggest real find applying all this: signal-resolution's backlog — 26,903 of 32,480 logged signals sat unresolved, checked at only 10-per-30s (~22h for one pass); batching cuts that to ~200/check, ~1h. 947 tests passing.

main.py · services/kalshi_client.py · services/http_client.py · tests/test_kalshi_client.py · tests/test_event_live_data.py · tests/test_trading_gate.py · tests/test_winner_propagation.py · tests/test_signal_resolution.py
phase 107 · a 15-minute market needs a 15-minute memory

Stale/already-closed catalog rows were reaching the real watchlist

Direct live report: "we need to account for extremely short market times like the BTC 15min markets so their live status is accurate... im not seeing any positions being opened or signals being read or anything. it was in fact responsible for much of my success rates earlier on." Root-caused: market_catalog.candidates_in_window/open_candidates never filtered by close_ts > now, so a market whose catalog row predates its own close kept showing up as a valid candidate indefinitely — confirmed live, 3 real KXBTC15M instances sitting 2-8h stale, still reading status="active". Fixed the query filter, then found the deeper reason it kept happening: pure least-recently-scanned ordering has no way to know a series that gets a brand-new market every 15 minutes needs revisiting far sooner than one whose markets each run for weeks — a full scan cycle across every configured-category series was taking 2-8h regardless of batch-size tuning. New series_with_expired_data + next_series_to_scan two-tier priority (expired-data series first, regardless of recency, least-recently-scanned within each tier) — confirmed live: KXBTC15M picked up its actual current live window within ~6 minutes of the fix, down from 2-8h.

services/market_catalog.py · tests/test_market_catalog.py
phase 108 · a stale row and a stale field agree with each other

close_time itself can be wrong, not just old — plus a self-review pass that found two more real gaps

Investigating a further, more urgent report ("im seeing absolutely no signals and i know for a fact its not due to filtering") surfaced something deeper than phase 107's own fix: a real finalized MLB market's catalog row still read status=active, close_time 2.5 days out, 24 minutes after Kalshi's own API had already moved it to finalized with the true, earlier close time. docs/kalshi/market_lifecycle.md, read as this investigation's own focused follow-up, confirmed the exact mechanism: a close_date_updated WebSocket event fires "when a market is closed ahead of its scheduled close time" — this app doesn't listen for it, so a catalog row scanned before that event fires keeps its stale close_time/status indefinitely, which phase 107's close_ts > now filter can never catch since it trusts the same column that's wrong. Fixed with a real-time confirmation pass in main._refresh_discovery_cache: one batched get_markets_by_tickers call per refresh cycle against the final, already-narrowed watchlist selection, dropping anything Kalshi now reports past active before it ever reaches the watchlist. Also closed a related dead-code finding from the same doc: the catalog's status filter checked for the literal string "open" — never a real API response value, only ever a query-filter one. Direct efficiency instruction mid-session ("a lot of efficiency could be gained by using batch calls to the API vs individual calls for specific markets") led to batching three more per-ticker gather sites the same way: _cached_market_fetch, _fetch_markets' live-only fallback, and propagate_milestone_winners' related-market lookup (previously one gather per event with a declared winner, now one call across every such event in the tick). A routine self-review of open ROADMAP.md items against actual code state (direct instruction: "many things in the roadmap may have been addressed at this point, do a scan/self-review") found two more real things: the 2026-08-11 whale-exit stale-price fix had in fact been committed and was live (the roadmap entry saying otherwise was documentation drift, not a code gap) — live- verified directly against real trade history for the first time, 11 real round-trips since with a 392.9s shortest hold, nowhere near the original 0.146s bug signature; and _process_stream_ticker was the one of check_exits' three call sites missing that same fix's opened_since guard, fixed with the same idiom. Separately, an efficiency review of this session's own Claude Code usage (direct instruction, with a `/usage` screenshot) found /sync-status-docs — this skill — at 21% of a week's total usage, root-caused to implied full reads of both this file and ROADMAP.md every invocation; rewritten to use targeted grep + scoped reads instead, same output quality at a fraction of the context cost. 953 tests passing.

main.py · services/market_catalog.py · tests/test_market_catalog.py · tests/test_trading_gate.py · tests/test_winner_propagation.py · .claude/skills/sync-status-docs/SKILL.md · CLAUDE.md
phase 109 · connected doesn't mean subscribed

The trade stream reported connected the whole time — it just never subscribed to any market

Direct live report, picking up right where phase 108 left off: "the whale watching stream has halted completely. no signals at all." GET /api/state's trade_stream_status showed connected: true throughout, which is exactly what made phase 108's own fix look sufficient — it wasn't. A standalone diagnostic script, reusing the same KalshiTradeWebSocketClient class and real credentials outside the running app, proved Kalshi's API was delivering plenty of real trades for the app's own live watchlist tickers (58 in 30s) while the live app's own trade_tape stayed at zero for the same window — the break was inside the app's subscription bookkeeping, not the network or Kalshi's side. Root cause, caught via temporary print tracing: _sync_subscriptions used not self._subscription_sids — a dict shared across every subscribed channel — to mean "no real per-ticker trade/ticker subscribe sent yet." On every fresh connect, that initial force-subscribe fires before the trading loop's first set_market_tickers() call (desired tickers still empty), so it correctly no-ops — but the account-wide fill/ market_positions channels subscribe immediately after and populate that same dict with their own sids. The trading loop's first real ticker-set update then read the dict as non-empty and took the incremental add/remove path instead, sending update_subscription against the fill/market_positions sids — trade/ticker never got a real subscription, deterministically on every process start, since the raw WS handshake always beats the REST-based discovery pipeline that feeds the ticker list. Fixed with a dedicated _market_channels_subscribed flag, decoupled from _subscription_sids's unrelated bookkeeping; also fixed a second bug in the same function found along the way — the incremental path looped over every sid instead of just the trade/ticker ones. Verified live: trade_tape went from stuck-at-zero to its 100-item cap within seconds of the fix landing, and data/candidate_log.db showed real-time min_notional_usd rejections logging again — the whole detection pipeline running end to end, just correctly filtering sub-threshold trades. A separate, real client-lifecycle race noticed along the way (main._refresh_discovery_cache's background task reusing a per-tick KalshiClient the same tick closes) was flagged, not fixed — see ROADMAP.md's P4 list. 2 new tests, 955 passing.

services/kalshi_trade_ws.py · tests/test_kalshi_trade_ws.py
phase 110 · the other half of "own your client"

Discovery's background refresh reused a client the calling tick had already closed

Direct instruction right after phase 109 landed: "you found another race bug. fix it then keep going down the todos and roadmap." _maybe_refresh_discovery_cache scheduled _refresh_discovery_cache as a fire-and-forget background task but handed it the calling tick's own KalshiClient — and that same tick's own finally: await client.close() closes it at the end of that tick regardless of whether the background task has finished with it, producing the repeated [discovery] background refresh failed log lines cycling through RuntimeError('Session is closed') / ClientConnectionError('Connector is closed.') / AssertionError() noticed while diagnosing phase 109. The fix already existed elsewhere in the same file: _check_signal_resolutions_background owns and closes its own client for this exact shape of problem — its own docstring even described _refresh_discovery_cache as already following that pattern, which is what surfaced the gap on review. Split _refresh_discovery_cache into a pure worker (still takes client as a param, so the existing tests calling it directly with a fake client needed no changes) and a new _refresh_discovery_cache_background wrapper that creates and closes its own client, mirroring the established split exactly. First verification pass gave a false alarm — a Monitor watching ddev logs -f without --tail 0 replayed the container's full historical log buffer, including pre-fix failures, before following live; a timestamp check confirmed the last real failure (16:56:00 UTC) predated the fix's reload (17:02:48–17:04:24 UTC) by several minutes and refresh cycles, and a clean re-run with --tail 0 showed a genuine 60-second failure-free window. 2 new regression tests (success and failure paths, asserting the background wrapper's client is always closed). 957 tests passing.

phase 111 · a real click beats a fake call

Signal feed sorts by divergence now — and its filter controls quietly weren't working at all

Continuing straight down the roadmap after phase 110: shipped the P4 item asking for the signal feed to sort/filter by divergence size (bet vs. market price), not just recency or raw whale size. Added a "Sort: divergence" option next to the existing recency/impact sorts, computing each signal's own divergence in probability points via a new divergencePts() — the whale print's implied YES price against the market's current live price — reusing the exact math and "Betting is N pts more bullish/bearish than the market implies" framing marketCardHTML's own divergenceLine already validated, just at individual-signal granularity. Each row now shows its own divergence badge (≥5pt threshold, matching the market card's) so the new sort is actually legible. Browser-verified via the selenium-chrome ddev service (CLAUDE.md's frontend-testing requirement) and found two real bugs in the process. First, a self-inflicted one: divergencePts initially read a bare state.latest_prices, assuming state was global the way other call sites in this file seemed to imply — a live typeof state check proved it's actually local to refresh(). Fixed by threading latestPrices through as an explicit parameter, backed by a new terminalLatestPrices global (mirroring terminalSignalFeed/ terminalDecisionFeed) for the inline handlers that need it. Second, a pre-existing bug this session didn't cause: all 5 of the feed's filter/sort onchange handlers called renderSignalDecisionFeed(signalFeed, decisionFeed) with bare identifiers that were never actually declared anywhere. Invisible to a direct function call in a test script — it only surfaced via a real simulated click (Selenium's Select().select_by_value(), which fires inline handlers in the browser's real global scope, not the closure that generated the HTML), producing Uncaught ReferenceError: signalFeed is not defined. Because the filter-state assignment runs before the broken re-render call in the same handler, the dropdown's value still updated internally, so the feed just silently caught up on the next 5-second poll instead of re-rendering instantly — invisible in normal use, with the console error nobody was watching for. Fixed by pointing all 5 handlers at the real terminalSignalFeed/terminalDecisionFeed globals this file's own websocket push-update path already used correctly. 957 tests passing (no Python touched this pass — pure frontend, verified via the browser instead of pytest).

phase 112 · a market that can never grow up

KXBTC15M was structurally locked out of the watchlist by a 24-hour clock on a 15-minute market

Direct report while checking the app's own standing stability test case after an unrelated infra session (moving ddev's router off host 80/443 to free it for a separate Traefik stack — see ddev-kalshi-whale-poc-integration.md in the portfolio repo, outside this one): KXBTC15M — the series specifically kept in the watchlist to verify the full whale/trade/close lifecycle every 15 minutes — wasn't in state["markets"] at all. Root-caused against market_catalog.db directly rather than guessed: kalshi.min_volume_24h (100k) filters on a 24-hour rolling volume figure, but every still-open KXBTC15M instance sat at volume_24h_fp = 0.0 — real rows showed volume only turning up (600k–1.3M) after close_ts, by which point open_candidates's own close_ts > now filter had already excluded it as closed. A 24-hour window can never be satisfied by a market whose entire tradeable life is 15 minutes — not a regression, a standing structural mismatch between the filter and this series' shape. Fixed generally, not with a KXBTC15M-only special case: market_catalog.open_candidates now takes an optional min_volume_by_series dict, applied via a SQL CASE per series_ticker against the blanket min_volume for everyone else — zero behavior change for any existing caller that doesn't pass it. Wired through a new kalshi.min_volume_24h_by_series: {KXBTC15M: 0} config entry, so covering another fast-rotating series later is a one-line config change, not a code change. Verified live: KXBTC15M-26AUG161415-15 appeared in state["markets"] and started picking up real WS ticker-price updates within one discovery-cache refresh. 959 tests passing.

services/market_catalog.py · main.py · config/settings.yaml · tests/test_market_catalog.py
phase 113 · the whole exchange, and the number it settles on

Exchange-wide trade stream, the CF Benchmarks settlement feed, and the measurement that decides whether it's an edge

Started from a direct question — "if whales are winning at 70% I shouldn't be winning at 40" — and the answer needed a tool that didn't exist. services/series_watcher.py reconciles the two numbers per series and decomposes the gap: on KXBTC15M over 24h, whales resolved 88.8% correct while the realised win rate was 47.1%, of which −30.6pts is selection (the 12 signals the gates actually traded resolved only 58.3% correct against an 88.8% pool — the gates pick worse than the pool they draw from) and −11.2pts is exits. It also captures what the app had been discarding: _process_stream_ticker received 15 documented ticker fields and kept two, dropping yes_bid_size_fp, open_interest_fp and ts_ms — exactly the fields needed to ask why a directionally-correct signal still lost money. Raw payloads are now persisted whole, raw_json included, so a field Kalshi adds next month is captured the day it appears.

Coverage was the other half. docs/kalshi/public-trades.md states market specification is optional on the trade channel, so it now subscribes with no market_tickers at all — push, not poll, zero REST tokens, and it closes a gap where 425 markets traded in a 30-second window against 15 watched. Three things had to change for that to produce signals rather than noise: the provider used to skip any print whose ticker wasn't in the watchlist one line into scoring, so the notional gate now runs first (pure arithmetic, no I/O) and only survivors get a market resolved — 2 of 5,162 prints on KXBTC15M, so the fetch is paid for the 2; kalshi_trade_ws tracked trade and ticker with one shared flag, which would have duplicated or blocked the firehose; and per-message SQLite writes would have re-triggered the 2026-08-11 freeze, so captures batch.

The largest find came from reading the settlement rules instead of the prices. KXBTC15M resolves on "the simple average of the sixty seconds of CF Benchmarks' BRTI before close", with floor_strike carrying the previous quarter's average — already fixed and known. Kalshi streams that exact average as it accumulates (cfbenchmarks_value's last_60s_windowed_average_15min), so services/index_feed.py is not a signal about the outcome but partial knowledge of it, and required_remaining_average() inverts it exactly. Verified against a real 06:00:00Z boundary: the window opened at 05:59:00 and counted 3 → 58 of 60 second-by-second, with 31 of the 60 settlement observations known 29 seconds before close.

Nothing about it was assumed. A sweep of eight crypto series found they contradict each other — strike_type is greater_or_equal on the 15-minute series but greater on the dailies; the same index is named BRTI, ETHUSDRTI and ERTI across different rules; KXBTCMAXY passes both the strike and operator checks but is a year-long barrier market with no averaging window; KXDOGED has no strike at all — so settlement_spec() refuses rather than guesses on each. Wiring it also exposed a real starvation bug: subscribed alone the channel delivers exactly 50 messages in 50 seconds, but multiplexed behind the trade firehose it delivered ~20 and froze, because the single reader loop awaited each message's full handler before reading the next. run() now splits ingest from processing over a bounded queue with counted overflow, and the index feed gets its own connection.

Whether any of that is an edge is a separate question, so services/settlement_edge.py answers it by measurement instead of assertion: both forecasts of the same event — the market's own yes price and the projection — recorded at the same instant, Brier-scored once the outcome stamps onto those same rows. It reports insufficient rather than a verdict until the data earns one, and nothing trades on it. Inspecting its captured rows immediately found two bugs that the wiring had hidden: the q15 window opens before every quarter-hour, so matching on index alone had recorded 1,003 observations against KXBTCD markets whose close was 863 minutes away; and resolution driven from the trading loop's market list never fired, because the 15-minute series rotates its ticker out of the watchlist before result populates — a market was found finalized six minutes after close with 59 observations still pending. Both fixed and verified live: bad rows purged, 59/59 resolved.

Two supporting pieces landed alongside. services/trade_archive.py copies every trade, position and summary metric into an append-only epoch before POST /api/reset touches anything, because a prior reset had left paper_broker.db reaching back only six hours and made every earlier trade-level question unanswerable; its compare() ranks epochs by edge_pts, not win rate. And min_unit_cost moved 0.50 → 0.65 on measurement, not taste: across 593 resolved signals the 0.50–0.65 band runs −35.2% per dollar risked while 0.65–0.80 is the only positive window at +2.2%. 1,084 tests passing.

services/index_feed.py · services/settlement_edge.py · services/series_watcher.py · services/trade_archive.py · services/kalshi_trade_ws.py · services/whalewatchers/kalshi_trade_tape.py · services/diagnostics.py · main.py · config/settings.yaml · CLAUDE.md · docs/kalshi/CHEATSHEET.md
phase 114 · breaking up main.py

Shared state and the first route group move out of a 5,400-line module

Direct request: "main.py is now almost 6k lines long, it would be worth it to make it more modular/less monolithic for better logic targeting and also session efficiency." Measured before cutting anything: 3,615 lines of imports/state/loop/handlers, then 1,800 lines holding 79 routes. The blocker was never file size — it was that essentially every route and stream handler reads or writes the module-level state dict and the long-lived singletons, so nothing could be lifted out without a circular import. So the enabling move came first: services/app_state.py now owns state plus broker/risk/strategy/shadow, the market-native pair, the account client and both websocket connections. main.py imports them by name, which leaves all ~500 existing state[...] references in that file untouched — a move, not a rewrite. The module imports nothing from main, and that acyclic direction is the entire point. routers/diagnostics_routes.py is the first group out and the template for the rest: ten routes become @router.* on an APIRouter, pull shared state from app_state, and are re-attached with include_router — so every path stays byte-identical and no client or test moves. They belong together because between them they answer one question, and share one discipline: read-only with respect to trading, and an explicit "unknown" rather than a fabricated number. 5,415 → 5,124 lines, 1,084 tests passing unchanged, and all four extracted paths verified serving 200 against the live app.

services/app_state.py · routers/__init__.py · routers/diagnostics_routes.py · main.py
phase 115 · main.py modularization complete

Seven phases later: main.py goes from ~5,450 lines to 1,716

Phase 114's enabling move (app_state.py + diagnostics_routes.py) got main.py to 5,124 lines; new routes and features added since pushed it back up to ~5,450 by the time this seven-phase plan started. Six phases landed first, each its own commit and live smoke check: killing strategy_engine.py's last import main reach-around; four shared read-side helpers (state_view.py, market_lookup.py, account_positions.py, ws_manager.py); a services/config/ module added mid-execution on direct request ("the config section itself also needs to be modular"); services/position/routes.py; services/history/ and services/analytics/ (including the market-analyst/ series-evaluator orchestration — the one part of "analytics" that's real logic, not routing); and the whale/index stream callback layer (services/whale_stream/), the one phase touching the app's single long-lived background task. Restructured into services/<concern>/ folders partway through on another direct request ("restructure the folders... so I could scope these modules individually... in their own git repos or at least Claude sessions"), each with its own routes.py and a CHEATSHEET.md (module ownership, relevant docs/kalshi/ pages, handoff to adjacent modules). Phase 7 — market watch/discovery, ~1,300 lines, the largest single extraction and the current tick-duration bottleneck — landed last on purpose, so every helper it might reach for already existed. Moved verbatim into services/market_watch/market_watch.py: catalog scanning, discovery-cache refresh, live market/status/milestone fetching. A full AST free-variable scan of the moved block (not just the plan's own pre-extraction guess) turned up one real cross-module dependency the plan hadn't pinned down — market_lookup._sport_for_event. This phase also finally ran the full pytest suite for the first time since phase 2 (deliberately deferred — "skip expensive testing until the end" — while phases 3-6 relied on fast static checks plus live curl smoke tests alone): it surfaced two real, pre-existing gaps, neither caused by this phase's own code move. _process_stream_lifecycle (phase 6) was called qualified in lifespan() but never re-imported bare into main.py, so main._process_stream_lifecycle didn't exist for four tests that reached for it. And monkeypatch.setattr(main, "KalshiClient", ...) silently stopped reaching _refresh_discovery_cache_background the moment that function's home module changed — a name rebound in main's namespace doesn't follow code that moved elsewhere and now resolves KalshiClient from its own module globals instead, the same underlying Python semantics as the _full_spectrum_analyzing gotcha from phase 5, just via monkeypatch instead of a direct reassignment. Fixed by patching services.market_watch.market_watch.KalshiClient directly. All 1,158 tests green afterward, and a live tick-timing sample (/api/health/pipeline) confirmed tick_phase_timings.market_fetch/last_tick_duration_sec statistically unchanged — same numbers, not improved; improving them is separate, future work. main.py: 3,016 → 1,716 lines this phase. trading_loop's control flow and phase ordering stay in main.py by design — its phase bodies are now mostly thin delegations into the modules above.

phase 116 · epoch-aware diagnostics

Judging history against the config that was actually live, not today's

services/diagnostics.py's check_threshold_integrity and check_price_band_adherence judged every historical row against today's live config — a config change made hours after a signal or trade was recorded made that row look like a violation it never actually was. performance_by_epoch, in the same file, already solved the equivalent problem correctly for a different question (win rate per config epoch), by reading config_performance.applied_changes — every live tuning change, timestamped, with its old and new value. Reused that same data source for point-in-time lookups instead: two new helpers, _fetch_path_changes(paths, since_ts) (one query per check, not one per row) and _historical_value(cfg, path, ts, changes) — the value at a given "section.field" path at time ts is the old_value of the earliest recorded change to that exact path after ts, or today's live value (via services/config/config_paths.py's _config_value_at_path) if nothing has changed since. check_threshold_integrity now rewinds whale_watcher_kalshi.min_notional_usd/.min_notional_usd_by_series per signal, keyed on its own seen_at; check_price_band_adherence rewinds all four layers config_overrides.resolve() reads — strategy.min_unit_cost/ .max_unit_cost plus both strategy_overrides tiers — per trade, keyed on its own timestamp. Caught two bugs in the first draft before they ever ran: a nonexistent import path (services.config has no config_performance — that's the flat services/config_performance.py, already imported correctly elsewhere in the file) and a missing _config_value_at_path import entirely — both found by re-reading the diff, not by the test suite. Three new tests in tests/test_diagnostics.py prove the fix in both directions — a stale-config false positive now clears, and a real violation from before a later config change still gets caught — and all 11 pre-existing tests in that file pass unchanged (with no applied_changes rows seeded, the new lookups return nothing and both checks fall back to today's value exactly as before — backward compatibility fell out of the design, it didn't need its own special case). Full suite: 1,161 passed. Live-verified against GET /api/diagnostics: both checks report epoch_aware: true, and getting the epoch right immediately surfaced a genuine, previously-uninterpretable finding rather than resolving to zero — check_threshold_ integrity still reports 1,169/1,900 signals (61.5%) below the floor that was truly live for their series, spot-checked against real evidence (a $2,297 print judged against a contemporaneous, unchanged $5,000 floor) to confirm this is a real number, not the same epoch-attribution bug reappearing. Not root-caused — captured as its own new ROADMAP.md item.

services/diagnostics.py · tests/test_diagnostics.py
phase 117 · index.html → real frontend build (esbuild)

A file-per-concern split first, then a correction: bring in real tooling, not just files

static/index.html was one file: markup, an 894-line inline <style>, and a 5,758-line inline <script> defining 150+ top-level functions and globals for all 7 dashboard tabs — the frontend's own version of the exact bloat main.py's seven-phase modularization (phase 115) just spent a whole session fixing on the backend. First pass: a pure byte-range split into 12 plain <script src> files sharing one global scope, deliberately not real ES modules — reasoning at the time was "no build step, no bundler" per CLAUDE.md's description of this project's other static pages. Direct correction from the user mid-session: that was never an actual instruction, and the frontend-separation roadmap item already said the framework/bundler question was undecided — "we can use libraries and plugins and tools to help achieve our goals." Given a straight choice between a lightweight bundler (real modules, npm libraries, no framework) and a full UI framework rewrite, went with the bundler.

frontend/ is now a real npm project (esbuild + eslint, frontend/package.json): frontend/src/js/*.js are the same 12 files, now genuine ES modules with real import/export — no shared global scope, an actual enforced dependency graph — bundled into the single file index.html now loads, static/js/dashboard.bundle.js. index.html: 7,716 → 1,073 lines. .ddev/config.yaml runs npm install on every ddev start (hooks.post-start, so a fresh clone doesn't need a manual setup step) and esbuild's own watch mode as a background daemon (web_extra_daemons), so editing frontend/src/js/*.js rebuilds the bundle automatically — the same save-and-it-just-works loop uvicorn --reload already gives the backend.

Converting to real modules surfaced a correctness class classic scripts never had to deal with, and each was worked through methodically rather than guessed at: (1) inline HTML onclick=/onchange=/oninput= handlers — some built indirectly through a function parameter, e.g. renderSuggestionCard(rec, acceptOnclickJS) — resolve identifiers against window, not module scope. Found the full set with an acorn AST walk over every handler string across all 12 files (not by re-grepping the same regex and assuming it was complete this time); every top-level function now gets a blanket window.fn = fn exposure per file, and the specific state objects handlers mutate by property (decisionFilter, screenerState, realPositionsState, …) get the same, individually. (2) Five values (lastSignals, lastTradeTape, terminalSignalFeed, terminalDecisionFeed, tradeTapeMinSize) get wholesale-reassigned elsewhere, which a one-time window.x = x would silently go stale after — replaced their inline reads/writes with small same-module wrapper functions instead (_rerenderSignalsFilter() etc). (3) The real ES-module-specific one: an importer only gets a read-only live view of an imported binding, so a variable can only be reassigned by the file that declares it. A second AST script found every case where the first pass's byte-range split had left a let in one file with its only reassignment happening in another — ten variables the split had scattered across shared-utils.js/trading-gate-and-connectivity.js that all actually belong to polling-and-websocket.js's poll loop — and relocated each declaration to its real owner (or added a setter where two files legitimately mutate the same one, like accountMode). eslint's no-undef (this project's JS equivalent of the backend's pyflakes check, wired as npm run lint) then caught what none of that manual analysis did: a genuine pre-existing bug, unrelated to the split — comboLegsHTML() read a bare state variable that has never existed anywhere in this codebase (confirmed against the last commit, predates this session entirely). Its own comment already named the intended source ("the same … latest global prices caches"), so fixed to read terminalLatestPrices instead of guessing at new behavior.

Verified throughout, not just at the end: a diff against a reconstructed concatenation of the original inline script (no code lost or reordered) before any conversion began; an AST script cross-checking every file's real import/export graph; eslint --no-undef and node --check both clean on every generated module; the watch-mode auto-rebuild verified live end to end (edited a source file, confirmed the served bundle changed within seconds — and confirmed a dangling trailing comment genuinely doesn't survive esbuild's output, catching a false alarm before it became a wrong conclusion). Full suite (1,161 tests) green throughout; the one test coupled to the old file layout (tests/test_e2e_terminal_static_and_api.py, asserting a JS helper's name in index.html's own response body) fixed to fetch js/dashboard.bundle.js instead. .ddev/nginx/kalshi-proxy.conf gained a no-store rule for *.css/*.js, same stale-asset-after-edit risk this project already hit once for .html.

phase 118 · framework-alternatives audit

Not a framework migration: three narrow fixes mapped 1:1 to diagnosed bugs

Direct question: given the bugs already found, should the app move off its "handspun logic" onto a framework that does state management, task orchestration, or frontend reactivity better? Investigated before writing any code — three Explore agents covered the backend concurrency/execution model, the position-lifecycle/gate logic, and the post-esbuild frontend state. None of the diagnosed bugs turned out to be the class a heavy framework (Temporal/Celery, an ORM, a full SPA framework) actually solves — they were a missing task-death detector, a specific gate re-validation gap, and duplicated derivation logic. Adopting one of those would also have conflicted with this repo's standing one-SQLite-file-per-concern, no-ORM instruction. Three narrow, in-house patterns shipped instead, each traced to a real measured bug rather than chosen speculatively.

Task supervision + logging (commit aa6d98b): every asyncio.create_task() call site in main.py/market_watch.py was fire-and-forget — an unhandled exception just silently ended the task, no restart, no trace. The exact shape behind three already- diagnosed live incidents (6973974's subscription-flag bug, a31ae51's stale- session reuse, 12323cc's duplicate-LLM-call race). New services/task_supervisor.py wraps a coroutine, logs and records any exception into the existing services/fault_log.py, and optionally restarts — used for the three long-running loops (trading_loop, trade_stream, index_stream) that must never just stay dead. Also added a one-line stdlib logging setup (services/logging_config.py) — the app had zero logging usage anywhere before this, only print() and fault_log.

The "four-entry gate bypass" fix (commit 6921543): root-caused a ROADMAP.md item open since 2026-08-17 (four real entries at unit costs 0.97, 1.00, 0.20, 0.97, one at conf 0.25 against a 0.495 threshold). PaperBroker.check_pending_fills() called open_position() for a filled limit order using only the fill-time price — entry_threshold/the tradeable-price-range floor/min_unit_cost/ max_unit_cost were only ever checked once, at placement time, against the price the order asked for. Since price can move between placement and fill, a resting order could fill at a price a fresh signal at that price would have been gate-rejected for. Fixed by extracting those gates from strategy_engine.evaluate() into a shared _validate_entry_price(), called both by evaluate() itself and by a new FollowTheWhaleStrategy.validate_pending_fill(), wired into check_pending_fills via a validate_fn callback (defaults to None, so every pre-existing caller/test is unaffected). A rejected fill now cancels the resting order and records a "fill_rejected" decision instead of opening a position. market_strategy.py's MarketNativeStrategy has no limit-order path at all, so needed no changes. No new position-status enum/state machine added — the fix is "validate right before open_position() is called," at both call sites, nothing more.

Frontend no-side-price centralization (commit d64b633): even right after phase 117's esbuild modularization, the exact no-side inversion formula CLAUDE.md's "Bug pattern to watch for" section warns about (side === 'yes' ? price : 1 - price) was still hand-copied at 13 confirmed call sites across 6 module files — modularization split the files but never centralized this one recurring piece of logic. New sideAdjustedPrice(side, price) in shared-utils.js replaces every inline reimplementation. costHTML()'s signature deliberately left unchanged — trade-log-and-real.js has one call site that already passes an already-side-correct price sourced directly from Kalshi's own yes/no_price_dollars fields, so folding the inversion into costHTML itself would have double-inverted that site.

Full suite green throughout (1,178 tests after 13 new ones — 4 for task_supervisor, 9 across test_paper_broker.py/test_strategy_engine.py for the fill-revalidation fix), CI confirmed green on pytest (dependency-audit's 20 pre-existing tracked CVEs unaffected, same count as before), and the live ddev app spot-checked post-reload (GET /api/state returning clean, no new errors, structured log lines now visible in ddev logs -s fastapi).

phase 119 · continued modularization, nine phases

Exits, whale calibration, advisory, backtest, market catalog, market events, diagnostics — plus splitting the modularization's own largest file

Direct instruction to continue the roadmap's modularization work past phase 115, naming concerns still left as oversized flat files or lumped under the wrong package: exit position management, whale signal calibration, advisory engine, market catalog, market events, backtesting — "bridges" was already shipped (services/whale_stream/decision_bridge.py, phase 115). Nine phases, each its own commit, full pytest pass + CI green before the next started. Phase 1: exit-decision logic (check_exits/_whale_lean/_exit_confidence/close_if_settled) split out of strategy_engine.py (now entry-only) into new services/exits/exit_engine.py, alongside position_netting.py moved in beside it — FollowTheWhaleStrategy.check_exits stays a public method with an unchanged signature, delegating internally. Phase 2: confidence_calibration.py + calibration_history.py (zero cross-imports with any analytics sibling, confirmed before moving) into services/whale_calibration/, per an already-queued ROADMAP.md item. Caught a real bug moving it: calibration_history.py's DB_PATH is computed relative to __file__, and the extra directory depth silently retargeted it to services/data/calibration_history.db instead of data/calibration_history.db — the real 53KB history file was untouched, but a live process would have started a fresh empty one in the wrong place. Fixed and re-verified against the real path before committing; the same class of bug was then checked for (and found twice more) on every later phase that moved a file with its own DB_PATH. Phase 3: advisory_engine.py into services/advisory/, same ROADMAP item — its one real cross-import (regime_analytics) stays a plain shared dependency rather than dragged along. Phase 4: backtest.py (a leaf module, zero cross-imports) into services/backtest/. Phase 8: normalized diagnostics — services/diagnostics.py plus the top-level routers/diagnostics_routes.py (phase 115's own one-off Phase-0 template, left inconsistent with every later concern's services/<name>/ shape ever since) into services/diagnostics/; the now-empty routers/ directory deleted. Three bare from services import diagnostics imports (app_state.py and two test files) silently resolved to the new package's __init__.py instead of the moved file — Python didn't error until the functions were actually called, so the first full-suite run caught it as 15 real failures, not an import error; fixed and re-verified. Phase 9: services/market_watch/market_watch.py itself — at 1,337 lines, the largest file in the whole tree, and phase 115's own largest single extraction — split into five cohesion-based siblings (catalog_scan.py, discovery_cache.py, market_fetch.py, live_status.py, event_metadata.py), with __init__.py re-exporting every name external callers need so main.py's own import line only dropped the redundant .market_watch module segment. A KalshiClient monkeypatch in test_trading_gate.py needed retargeting from the old single-file module to discovery_cache.py, where _refresh_discovery_cache_background actually lives now — same underlying Python semantics phase 115 itself flagged for a near-identical monkeypatch gotcha. Every new module got a CHEATSHEET.md written to an audit-oriented standard, not just a reference index: how the module currently behaves, what the API docs say it should do, and any gap already visible while writing it. phase 121 · whale threshold: dollars → contracts

The whale-detection gate is contract count now, not dollar notional — the single biggest measured lever on the 70%/70% target

ROADMAP.md and docs/next-session-pickup-2026-08-17.md had already measured and documented this fix on 2026-08-17 but left it unshipped deliberately, since it redefines what the app considers a whale — a strategy call, not a bug fix. Confirmed with the user before shipping: a fixed dollar threshold is geometrically biased toward near-certain prices ($2,500 buys 125,000 contracts at 2¢ but only 2,505 at 99.8¢), so it was finding whoever could afford a near-certainty in size, not informed traders — measured across 145,785 real prints, the dollar gate's picks sat at mean unit cost 0.926 (75.9% of them ≥0.95, the band that bleeds); a count >= 5,000 selector lands at mean 0.759, with 27.3% inside the only profitable band versus 8.6% for the dollar gate. Chosen to fully replace (not run alongside) min_notional_usd. whale_watcher_kalshi.min_contracts (default 5,000) and min_contracts_by_series are the new gate in services/whalewatchers/kalshi_trade_tape.py — real dollar notional is still computed and recorded in raw_context for diagnostics, it just no longer gates anything. Per-series overrides were re-derived from real data rather than blindly renamed: signal_log.db's own logged whale-signal sizes for KXTRUMPSAY/KXTRUMPMENTION top out at 3,506/4,932 contracts, so a flat 5,000 global floor with no override would have silently gone dark on both — 2,500/500 (the old dollar tier's own round numbers, reused deliberately) sit at or below every affected series' observed history, so the unit switch doesn't zero out their signal flow. Every downstream consumer of the old dollar gate was updated in the same pass: check_threshold_integrity (now checks signals.size, a NOT NULL column every provider populates, using factors_json IS NOT NULL — the same idiom resolved_signals_with_factors already used — as the "real provider row" filter that raw_notional_usd IS NOT NULL used to serve) and check_coverage in services/diagnostics/diagnostics.py, the whale_sized_prints funnel stage in services/series_watcher.py, the per-series override shown to the series-analyst LLM (market_analyst_orchestrator.py/market_analyst_agent.py), and the live dashboard's Controls panel — static/index.html's whale-threshold field and frontend/src/js/config-panel.js's load/save wiring really do drive live config, not just document it, so both needed the same field rename the backend got (esbuild's watch daemon rebuilt static/js/dashboard.bundle.js automatically; npm run build re-verified it). The other roadmap item this superseded (a not-yet-root-caused investigation into real min_notional_usd violations) was closed as moot rather than resolved: the dollar floor it was checking no longer exists to be violated. 39 whale-watcher tests, 26 series-watcher tests, and 14 diagnostics tests updated for the new field names and gate semantics; full suite (1,141 tests) green throughout.

phase 122 · module quality: data-consumption audit

New standing goal (retiring the 70%/70% target): per-module effectiveness, efficiency, informativeness

Direct instruction: "forget the 70% thing. now that things are modular the goal is to make sure each module works at peak effectiveness, efficiency, and informativeness." CLAUDE.md's old "HARD COMMANDMENT" section (2026-08-17: reproduce 70% whale accuracy AND 70% own win rate) was rewritten in place to state this instead - the measured data stays in git history, but nothing gets judged against it going forward. Picked up ROADMAP.md's "per-module data-consumption audit" item (2026-08-22) as the first work under the new goal, since it already named four concrete, unfixed findings. Three were real bugs, fixed: (1) _fetch_markets's live_markets_only hydration fired an uncapped asyncio.gather - one client.get_markets(limit=100, series_ticker=s) per distinct selected series, no semaphore - at live watchlist_size: 150 that's up to ~50 concurrent calls every 6s tick, each one alone able to exceed Kalshi's entire 600-token read-burst budget (same failure shape live_status._fetch_live_status already hit and fixed once, see phase 97's own _LIVE_STATUS_MAX_POLL_PER_TICK). Replaced with one batched get_markets_by_tickers() call - chunks to 50/request, sequential, never concurrent - which also fixed a real correctness gap for free: no status filter, so an already-settled market hydrates on the same call instead of needing the old per-ticker fallback branch. (2) series_evaluator.evaluate_pending() opened a fresh SQLite connection per ready series inside its own loop, the exact per-call overhead this file's own record_trades_observed_bulk() was already written to avoid once before (2026-08-11 incident) - now one connection for the whole call, with an explicit commit per verdict to keep the original per-verdict durability. (3) trade_analytics.compute_summary() was computed twice inside advisory_engine.generate_recommendations() itself (once for gate_summaries, again for category_rows - every real caller passes both), and regime_analytics.by_category() twice inside market_analyst_orchestrator._build_full_spectrum_context() on the identical all_rows input - both hoisted to compute once. The fourth finding - market_history.snapshots' spread/volume_24h/ time_to_close_sec columns written every tick, read by nothing - was investigated and left alone: docs/advisory-engine-plan.md §9 (2026-08-08 direct request) names exactly this shape - persist real data ahead of a not-yet-built ML-agent consumer - as the intended design, not an accident. Verified live throughout: real API confirmation of the whale-threshold rename (phase 121, folded into this same push cycle), _build_full_spectrum_context() called directly against real trade history with no exception, full suite (1,142 tests) green after every change.

phase 123 · the caching half, not just the concurrency half

Real REST rate limiting, direct report: phase 122's own live_markets_only fix was incomplete

Direct report, same day as phase 122: "rest api rate limiting is happening in the history and especially position sections. i've insisted multiple times on streams to inform those sections and using REST API to only verify and act on decisions, not monitor data and make decisions every tick." Root-caused to the exact branch phase 122 had just touched: that pass replaced an uncapped concurrent asyncio.gather (one get_markets(series_ticker=s) call per selected series) with one safe batched get_markets_by_tickers call - genuinely safer, but still fired unconditionally on every tick, no caching, unlike the pinned-watchlist and extra_tickers branches sitting right next to it in the same function, which already routed through _cached_market_fetch - a 300-second structural-field cache with price freshness coming from the WS ticker-channel overlay instead, exactly the "websocket stream everything you can... leave the api calls for things that are absolutely necessary" instruction from 2026-08-15. Confirmed the gap was real, not theoretical, via direct instrumentation (wrapping the fake client's own method to log calls, not just reading the code) - the uncached call fired on every single _fetch_markets invocation. Fixed by routing live_markets_only hydration through _cached_market_fetch too, so all three branches now share one consistent pattern. Added a size cap + oldest-first eviction to that shared cache (_MAX_MARKET_OBJECT_CACHE = 2000, same shape as kalshi_trade_tape.py's own _MAX_MARKET_CACHE) since it now serves a much larger, faster-rotating population than the small pinned/open-position set it originally did. Also traced (no change needed) the other two things "position sections" could plausibly have meant: real-account balance/positions/fills (_fetch_account_snapshot) were already on a 20-second cache from an earlier pass, and a private, authenticated fill/market_positions WebSocket subscription already exists and is wired end to end (whale_stream_handlers._process_stream_fill/_process_stream_position, subscribed in kalshi_trade_ws.py's run()) - neither needed a fix here. Three new tests: caching actually prevents a second REST call within the TTL window (direct instrumentation, not just "still passes"), the new eviction logic under a lowered cap, and cache-isolation fixes for three existing hydration tests that started colliding once live_markets_only began sharing the cache (same ticker, different hydrated values, across tests in the same file). Full suite (1,144 tests) green.

phase 124 · risk-architecture backstop

Execution-layer risk enforcement, flatten-all, portfolio-wide exposure cap

Backfilled from commit a8de034 (2026-08-22), the earlier of two Phase-N/9 sub-phases from that day's continued-modularization session — shipped and tested at the time, but never carried into this file, so it sat undocumented until this pass caught it via a stale-ROADMAP.md check. Closed 3 gaps found via a gap-check against docs/prediction-market-bot-research.md's engineering-safety checklist. First: RiskManager.check_daily_loss()/max_trade_size() were only ever consulted from strategy_engine.py's own evaluate() — neither PaperBroker.buy/sell nor KalshiAccountClient.create_order() independently re-checked risk.halted or a position-size ceiling before executing, the same shape of gap the four-entry gate bypass (phase 121) had already proven reachable once. Fixed by making PaperBroker.open_position() itself refuse (return None) when the wired-in RiskManager is halted or a new exposure cap (below) would be exceeded — the same choke point both a market-order entry and a filled resting limit order already pass through, so a future bug in either caller's own gate logic now has a backstop. KalshiAccountClient.create_order() gets the same halt guard, with an is_closing_order escape hatch so a flatten/close order still works during a live halt. Second: no portfolio-wide exposure cap existed — max_position_pct caps one trade, max_open_positions_per_series caps count within one series, but nothing capped total dollar exposure summed across concurrently-open positions in different series. New RiskManager.check_total_exposure(current_exposure, prospective_cost, bankroll), stateless like max_trade_size, reads a new opt-in risk.max_total_exposure_pct config field (default null = no cap), enforced at the same open_position() choke point. Third: no single function closed every open position at once — if Kalshi access were cut off suddenly or the kill switch tripped on a real account, flattening was manual, per-position action. New PaperBroker.close_all_positions() plus KalshiAccountClient.flatten_all() (side/price mapping verified against docs/kalshi/create-order-v2.md's BookSide description and get-positions.md's position_fp sign convention — no bulk-flatten endpoint exists on Kalshi's own API, so this iterates and closes each position individually), wired into a new POST /api/trading/flatten-all route gated by the same typed-confirmation-phrase mechanism as /api/trading/enable. Re-verified live this session (2026-08-23), not just trusted from the commit message: re-read every call site above and re-ran the 4 relevant test files fresh — 261 passed, 0 failed.

phase 125 · lifecycle-driven settlement

market_lifecycle_v2 now reaches the persisted catalog and the real outcome pipeline

Closes the second half of a gap phase 122 (2026-08-17) deliberately left open: that phase wired close_date_updated into main.py's in-memory state["markets"] overlay only, and left determined/settled completely unwired - "re-routing this app's real outcome/P&L resolution onto a channel with zero live-verified message history" was judged too risky to rush. Verified that precondition before touching anything this time, not just reading the docs: /api/state's lifecycle_stream_stats already showed 2,256+ real determined and 2,372+ real settled events processed, and kalshi_trade_ws.py's own "first real shape" log (in ddev logs) confirmed the real captured payloads match docs/kalshi/market-and-event-lifecycle.md's documented schema exactly - determined carries result/determination_ts/ settlement_value, settled carries only settled_ts, no result. That made determined the correct (and only) trigger. Two real gaps closed: (1) services/market_catalog/market_catalog.py's persisted SQLite rows never got these events at all, only the next incremental per-series scan (potentially hours away) - new apply_lifecycle_update(ticker, *, close_ts=None, status=None) does a targeted single-row UPDATE, called from services/whale_stream/whale_stream_handlers.py's _process_stream_lifecycle for all three event types (close_date_updated -> close_ts, determined -> status="determined", settled -> status="finalized", the same values a real REST market object's own status would show). (2) market_history.record_outcome/settlement_edge.resolve_window/ market_analyst_agent.resolve_from_market_results/ candidate_log.resolve_from_market_results were fed exclusively from that tick's REST-fetched markets list - structurally blind to any ticker that rotates off the live watchlist/discovery scope before it settles (routine for short-lived series like KXBTC15M), no matter how fast the poll ran. market_lifecycle_v2 is exchange-wide, so firing all four from the determined event closes that blind spot - done in addition to, not instead of, the existing REST-tick path, safe because all four are idempotent (INSERT OR IGNORE / WHERE resolved = 0 / WHERE settled_yes IS NULL). A real test-isolation bug was caught along the way, not just new tests added: tests/test_trading_gate.py's _process_stream_lifecycle tests redirected market_catalog/ market_history's DB_PATH to a tmp dir but not settlement_edge/ market_analyst_agent/candidate_log's - since the new determined handling now calls all three, the existing test would have queried the real, live data/settlement_edge.db/data/market_analyst.db/data/candidate_log.db on every run (harmless that specific time since the test ticker never matched a real row, but a real violation of this project's test-isolation rule and a real WAL-lock contention risk against the running dev server). Fixed by extending the file's existing pre-import DB_PATH-redirect block to all three; confirmed no contamination by grepping the real databases for the exact test ticker afterward. Also added: two new lifecycle_stream_stats counters (catalog_updates_applied, outcomes_resolved_via_lifecycle) on /api/state, so this path's real activity is directly observable rather than trusted blind. Full suite (1,146 tests, up from 1,144) green. Verified against real production traffic after shipping: catalog_updates_applied incremented on real events while close_time_updates_applied stayed lower for the same events - direct proof the persisted- catalog path reaches markets the in-memory-only overlay from phase 122 could not.
